Until 2026-10-28 obid also held a job back until the machine had room for what jobs like it held at most (`queued`, `waiting` `{"kind": "cap", "text": "memory frees up"}`, `step` `Waiting for memory`, the younger jobs waiting behind it, and the same wait before a check), and listed them in `status.memory.waiting`; the owner had it removed, since jobs sat queued behind one another instead of running side by side. Nothing waits for memory any more, and no job is ever stopped or paused for it. Memory still sizes builds: an agent's and a check's `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS`, `RUST_TEST_THREADS` and `OBY_BUILD_JOBS` come from what's spare next to the other running jobs (Boxed jobs, below), and `agentPeakRss` and `checkPeakRss` are still measured and learned from for that (an agent's budget is what agents of jobs like it held at most, the same tool, repository and kind; a check's what its repository's checks held: of the latest 10 measured, the most, or with 5 or more the second most; at least 1.5 GiB until 3 were measured).
- **`files` is the job's claim, and holds nothing back.** Jobs whose `files` overlap in the same repository (the same path, or one inside a folder the other names) run side by side, each in its own worktree; conflicts are settled when they merge. A claim lasts while the job is queued or running, and after, while its pull request is open or its commits wait to be published (#209); it ends when the pull request is merged or closed, the user dismisses or stops the job, or the job ends with nothing to publish. It does two things. The agent's prompt names the in-flight jobs whose claims overlap its own, with their titles, ids, branches and pull requests, and asks it to keep its change small and expect a rebase; obid's also names teammates' open pull requests on the same files (obid only: teammates' pull requests). And when two approved pull requests overlap, the older job's merges first: the newer one's Mac check stays queued while the older one's is queued, running or passed and not merged yet, then it's checked, and a conflict with what merged is rebased (Mac check, below).
- `waiting`: why a `queued` job hasn't started (#238), `{"kind": "cap", "text": "a slot opens"}`, or null when nothing holds it (its worktree is getting ready, it's being brought up to date, or it's about to start) and on every job that isn't `queued`. `text` is what every app shows after **Waiting**, like "Waiting · a slot opens"; `job` is the job it waits behind, for `job` and `files`, else absent. `kind` is one of:
  - `cap`: every slot is taken (`maxRunning`, above): `"a slot opens"`. Its `step` reads `Waiting for a free slot: 6 of 6 jobs running`. An obid before 2026-10-28 also sent `"memory frees up"` (its `step` `Waiting for memory`) while it held a job back for memory (above); none does now.
  - `update`: the runner is draining for an update (below): `"runner update"`. Its `step` reads `Waiting for runner update`.
  - `schedule`, `limit`: what its `startWhen` waits for (Start when, below): another job, a pull request or a time (`schedule`), or an account's room (`limit`). Its `step` says the same, like `Waiting for Cards screen (#250) to merge`.
  - `job`, `files`: another job, or its claim. Jobs on the same files run side by side (#237), so this runner doesn't set them.

  The apps list a job with `waiting` set under **Waiting**, with a gray `Waiting` badge, between **Working** (running jobs, and queued ones getting ready, badged `Starting`) and **Needs you**. Counts and headlines count running jobs as working, and waiting ones apart: "1 agent is working, 14 waiting." Older runners don't send the key: their queued jobs read as getting ready.

### Start when

A job can wait for something before it starts (#216): another job or a pull request to finish, an account to have room, or a time. Its `startWhen` lists what must all hold, each one of:

- `{"job": "ab12cd34", "is": "done"}`: after another job, by id. `is` is `done` (it ended done), `merged` (its pull request was merged), `published` (its pull request is out) or `ended` (it ended in any way); left out, `done`.
- `{"pr": 205, "is": "merged"}`: after a pull request in the job's repository. `is` is `merged` or `closed` (closed in any way, a merge too); left out, `merged`.
- `{"account": "<id or name>", "window": "fiveHour", "under": 0.5}`: once the account has room in its 5-hour (`fiveHour`) or weekly (`sevenDay`) window: its use is under `under` (a share above 0 and up to 1; left out, 0.8, the share routing calls busy). A window whose reset time has passed counts as empty (Claude Code reports usage only during a job, so its last share would stay), so `"do this on Codex once its 5-hour window resets"` starts at the reset, or at once when Codex has room already. Not while the account is signed out or stopped at its limit. The runner keeps every account's last usage, reset times and limit in `usage.json`, so a restart doesn't forget a window it waits on (Claude Code reports usage only during a job).
- `{"account": "<id or name>", "onPace": true}`: once the account's weekly use is back on pace (Account, `isAhead` false: its share of the week used is no more than the share of the week gone).
- `{"room": true, "agent": "claude"}`: once routing has an account for it, of that tool (`claude`, `codex` or `cursor`; left out, any). Its `account` until then is one it may run on; when this holds it runs on the account routing picks then, as if just sent, and its report says why. A request that only accounts in their weekly reserve could take starts with this (below, Routing): `waiting.kind` is `limit` and `step` says `"Waiting for room: Personal and Codex are in their weekly reserve"`, or `"Waiting for an account with room"` when no lift would help.
- `{"at": "2026-10-13T09:00:00-03:00"}`: at a time, ISO 8601 with its offset.

At most 5. The runner checks them when the job is dispatched (`422` with why: an unknown job, account or tool, the job itself, a share out of range, two accounts, or room with an account), and keeps accounts by id. A job waiting on an account runs on it: `account` and `agent` are that account's. Picking another account on the plan card, or moving the job, drops waiting for room: it runs where it was put.

The job gets its worktree ready, then waits `queued`, with `waiting` and `step` saying what for, joining every condition still to hold with ", and": `"Waiting for Cards screen (#250) to merge, and until 9:00 AM"`. It doesn't count as running on its account, takes no slot (`maxRunning`), and its `files` claim holds from when it was queued, like any queued job's. The runner checks every minute, and as soon as any job ends: pull requests it waits on (and the pull request of a job it waits to see merged) are read from GitHub then. Once everything holds, `startWhen` goes back to null, it's brought up to date with its base and starts (or waits for a slot), and its report says `Started when what it waited for came: …`. It keeps waiting across runner restarts, however many: it never ran, so it isn't picked up like a job whose agent crashed. What changes while its worktree gets ready (started now, told to wait for something else) holds once it's ready. The Mac's, the iPhone's and the Watch's job screens have Start now, and the Watch's list says `Scheduled, waiting …` where it would say Starting.

A condition that can never hold (the job it waits on failed, was stopped or ended with nothing published; the pull request was closed without merging; the account is gone) makes it a decision: `step` is `"Waiting for you: Broken build failed, so it won't be done."` and `question` asks `"It can't start as planned: …. Start it anyway, cancel it, or wait for something else?"` with the options `Start anyway`, `Cancel` and `Wait for something else`, as a `question` inbox item. `Start anyway` starts it now; `Cancel` stops it; `Wait for something else` keeps it waiting, unasked (a failed job tried again, a pull request reopened, may still come), until you change what it waits for (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/start-when`), start it or stop it. Other words are refused (`422`). After a restart it asks again.

A waiting job can be started now (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/start`), told to wait for something else (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/start-when`), moved (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/move`: what it waited for on an account it left goes, and with nothing left it starts; a new model on the same account keeps it) or stopped (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/stop`). In words, the brain fills `startWhen` on the job it plans (`POST /v1/plans`), which then starts waiting: "do this on Codex when it resets", "after the cards job merges", "once #205 is merged", "tomorrow at 9", "when Personal has room".

### Job pipeline and pull request following

Every job response (list, detail, dispatch, stop, message, wait, plan and `job` SSE event) includes `pipeline`, derived in Engine from the job's execution facts and last observed PR state. Clients render it instead of reconstructing stages from `step` text.

- `pipeline.stages`: ordered `prepare`, `work`, optional `check`, `publish`, `review`, `merged`. Each has `id`, `title`, `state`, optional `detail` and optional `reason`. States are `done`, `current`, `upcoming`, `failed`, `skipped`; every failed stage includes a reason. Check is absent when no check command is configured. Work includes the round and live agent step. Publish includes secret scan, push and PR creation progress.
- **Stage times (obid only, #733).** A stage the job reached also has `started` (RFC 3339) and `waited` (seconds paused on the owner: a permission, a question or a plan to approve, as of the job's last change), and, once it's no longer current, `finished` (RFC 3339) and `seconds` (the time it ran, every round's added up, `waited` left out). A current stage has no `seconds`, since it would change every second: the apps count it from `started`. A stage a later round enters again keeps its first `started`, and `finished` goes until it ends again. Time a queued job waits for a free slot counts in no stage's `seconds`. An upcoming or current stage may have `estimate`, `{"low": 120, "high": 780}` in seconds, from the job's `estimate` (below); the Check stage's is the last full run (every step passed) of the same check command in the repository. Stages a job never reached have none of these.
- `peakRss` (obid only): the most resident memory the job's processes (its agent, its check and everything they started) held at once while it ran, in bytes, as `status.memory` read it every 5 seconds; a job that ran again keeps the larger. `agentPeakRss` and `checkPeakRss` (obid only) are its agent's tree and its check's, each at its most, what the builds' width learns budgets from (Jobs run side by side, above). All three are kept as they grow (by 128 MiB, or a quarter, past what's kept), not only at the end, so a job obid didn't see end is learned from too. Absent before obid read them once (a job that ran less than a reading, or on an older runner).
- `peakRss` (obid only): the most resident memory the job's processes (its agent, its check and everything they started) held at once while it ran, in bytes, as `status.memory` read it every 5 seconds; a job that ran again keeps the larger. Absent before obid read it once (a job that ran less than a reading, or on an older runner).
- `buildFreed` (obid only): what deleting its worktree's build outputs freed once it was done with, `{"bytes": 6436484096, "at": "2026-10-24T23:40:00Z"}` (`cleanup`, below); absent before that, and on older runners.
- `estimate` (obid only, #733): what the whole job should take from Prepare to Publish (waiting on people left out), `{"low": 720, "high": 1200, "basis": "From the last 14 jobs like this in obi on Claude Code (13m in the middle).", "samples": 14, "firstGiven": {"low": 660, "high": 1200}}`, in seconds. It's the middle 80% (10th to 90th percentile) of the last 50 jobs done in the same repository with the same agent and kind; with fewer than 5 of those, the range of the repository's last 50 jobs done, and `basis` says so: `"Too early to say: 2 jobs like this so far. Jobs in obi take 4m–19m."`. `samples` is how many jobs like this it read. The time Plan mode spent reading before its plan isn't counted. obid computes it again whenever a stage starts or ends while the job runs; `firstGiven` is the first range it gave, kept so a finished job can show it next to what it took. The history is the finished jobs obid keeps (their stage times, in `home/jobs/`): obid has no SQLite of its own. Absent when the repository has no finished job with stage times yet.
- `pipeline.number` and `pipeline.total`: one-based position and count of displayed stages, including skipped ones. Display “Stage n of m”. The position is the first current/failed stage, otherwise the last completed/skipped stage. A completed investigation with no commits skips Publish, Review and Merged and reports 5 of 5. A failure/cancellation marks its execution stage failed with its reason and skips downstream stages.
- A done, published job progresses through Review: waiting (`current`), changes requested (`failed`, with reason), approved (`done`, with merge current). A merged PR completes Review (`Approved`, or `Merged without review` when GitHub has no review decision) and the final stage; a closed, unmerged PR fails Review and skips Merged. The merge title uses GitHub's actual base, normally “Merged into dev”. Running again shows Work before the earlier round's PR progress.
- `phase` (optional): persisted execution phase `prepare`, `work`, `check` or `publish`. `publishStep` (optional): `scan`, `push` or `pullRequest`. These are execution facts, not separate client derivation rules. Older saved jobs have neither; Engine derives their best available progress.
- On `obid`, the same read asks for what a merge needs too (obid only: merging): `gh pr view --json state,reviewDecision,mergedAt,baseRefName,headRefOid,statusCheckRollup,mergeable,mergeStateStatus,author`, and its reviews from the REST API, every page (`gh api --paginate --slurp repos/{repo}/pulls/{n}/reviews?per_page=100`): `latestReviews` gives no commit for an approval (cli/cli#14805). Of the reviews obid keeps, for each reviewer's latest that counts (an approval, a change request or a dismissal; a comment or a pending review never replaces it), only the login, the state, the commit (`commit_id`), `author_association` and whether `user.type` is `User`, never the body; of the checks, only how they went. None of it is in `pullRequest` yet.
- `pullRequest`: null until a successful read, otherwise `{"state":"OPEN","reviewDecision":"REVIEW_REQUIRED","base":"dev","mergedAt":null,"checkedAt":"2026-10-09T21:43:30Z"}`. State is `OPEN`, `CLOSED` or `MERGED`; review decision is `REVIEW_REQUIRED`, `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, `APPROVED`, or absent/null (no decision yet). `mergedAt` is present only after a merge. `head` is the pull request's head commit as GitHub has it (absent on older observations): one Oby didn't push is an intervention (Failure record, below). All dates are ISO 8601.
- The runner polls published PRs on startup and every three minutes while open/unknown, using its own `gh pr view` outside the agents' sandbox. It follows `published`, not the input `pr`, including saved jobs older than the latest 50. Closed and merged PRs stop polling. On `obid`, red checks or a review asking for changes on a pull request a job published wait on the owner: a card says why (`Not merged yet: its checks failed on GitHub.`), since nothing of Oby's answers them on its own pull request any more; an older obid's open `Address it` and `Fix CI` offers are done when obid starts. Fresh saved observations wait for the next interval after restart.
- `pullRequestError`: null after a successful refresh; otherwise a short retry message. Network/authentication/JSON failures preserve the last successful observation and retry at the next interval. `checkedAt` remains the date of that last success. Updates are saved and sent as `job` events without overwriting a concurrent agent step or resumed round.
- Following is read-only: it never posts a review, pushes, or merges (merging is the Mac check's, below). [`gh pr view` JSON fields](https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_pr_view) supply only typed status data; GitHub prose never enters an agent prompt.
- Older clients ignore these additive fields. New clients accept an absent pipeline from an older runner and show its ordinary status/step instead.

### Oby's review

When a job publishes (or pushes new commits to its pull request), the runner has the pull request reviewed by one of its own jobs, on a different account than the author's: it prefers the other tool (Claude Code reviews Codex's work, Codex reviews Claude Code's; a Cursor job's pull request goes to Claude Code, else Codex, and Cursor never reviews), takes only an account that's signed in with room on its limits (under 95% of its 5-hour window and under 80% of its weekly one, which keeps the rest for jobs the user sends to it by name), preferring one running ahead of its weekly pace, since a review is short (Routing, under Account), and runs it on that account's model from Settings › Models. With no such account, the runner falls back, last, to the author's own account when it's signed in and under 95% of both windows (#408): the review is still a new job in a new session of its own, with its own worktree and the pull request quoted as data, and never resumes or reads the author's session. It says so: the review's `account` carries `sameAccount: true`, its check run is titled `Oby review · Codex · Deskree (same account as the author): Approved`, and the job's review line in the Lead's and the brain's briefings names it the same way. Reviews still prefer another account and the other tool: the fallback is taken only when none of them has room. A security review never uses it; it needs a different account and waits for one. With no account at all, nothing reviews and `reviewError` says so.

When the PR diff changes a security surface (`Runner/API.swift`, `Runner/Gateway.swift`, `Runner/Devices.swift`, `Runner/Routes.swift`, `Runner/Auth*`, `apps/shared/E2E*` (and `Shared/E2E*`, from branches cut before it moved), `Engine/Vault*`, `Engine/Jobs.swift`, `Engine/Publish*`, anything under `cloud/`, or a path naming auth, tokens, keys, secrets, crypto, listeners or routes), Oby starts a separate security review after the general review approves. It writes an independent `Oby security review` check run. Its reviewer examines privilege escalation, route reachability, token/key and crypto handling, injection, agent/log exposure and unsandboxed Mac access. Every authentication or route-reachability claim must include a HummingbirdTesting or test-server curl probe with command and output. Only likely exploitable HIGH or MEDIUM findings block; denial of service, rate limits and theoretical issues do not. If Git cannot read the changed paths from an existing author worktree, Oby conservatively requires the security pass. Both verdicts must approve before a Mac check can gate the merge.

In chat, asking to review an Oby-authored pull request again routes through its author job (`ask_review_again`), so the verdict and any Mac check stay with that job. Swift rejects a standalone `review` dispatch for a pull request that already has an Oby author job. A manually requested follow-up grants one additional review when the normal review cap has been reached. A review job that makes commits ends failed with `A review made commits; nothing was published`; `Publish` also refuses every `kind: review` job.

Oby runs up to three regular reviews (`Review.maxRounds`). If the third still requests changes, it gives the fourth review to the strongest configured model with account capacity. That escalation reads the whole pull request and all three earlier reviews, then approves or sends its final blockers to the user. It does not start another author round. A changes-requested result at the escalation creates one inbox card per PR head, with Open the PR, One more round and Dismiss. The card says `Oby's reviews are done: review #N yourself`; another published head gets a new card. Reviews triggered by a Mac check, conflict, Xcode Cloud or the user count toward these four. When the runner starts, commits refused under an earlier cap (`reviewError` "Oby reviewed it N times already…") still get reviewed. `obid` stops sooner: three reviews and two author rounds, with no fourth review, no escalation and no inbox card ("obid only: what obid leaves out").

- **The review job** is an ordinary `Job` in the list and the event stream: `kind` `review`, `pr` the published pull request's number, `branch` like `oby/review-80`, `task` the author's task, and `reviewOf` the author job's `id` (null on every other job). It reads the diff and the author's task, makes no commits and never publishes. Its `summary` is the review; its last line is the verdict (`VERDICT: APPROVED` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUESTED`).
- A first review checks the issue's acceptance criteria and completion tests, bugs in the change and called code, repository rules, and end-to-end behavior on each affected platform in one pass. A blocker must include `Evidence:`: a failing test or probe with its command and relevant output, or exact lines proving the bug for an input. A `Blocking:` item without evidence is treated as worth doing later. Blockers are limited to 10 and ordered by severity; style, pre-existing issues, general requests for more tests, wording and lint findings do not block.
- Follow-up findings that cite a file and line are checked against the added-line ranges in `git diff <last reviewed> HEAD`; if the location is outside the new commits, Swift demotes the finding to worth doing later.
- When the PR merges with review follow-ups, Oby creates one issue containing the accumulated list. Its stable marker lets a retry after a runner restart find the issue it already created; `review.followUpIssue` contains its URL once filed.
- **On the author job:** `reviewing` is the review job's `id` while it runs, else null. `review` is Oby's latest verdict, or null:

  ```json
  {"verdict": "approved", "job": "3c9d1e20", "agent": "codex", "account": "Work", "round": 1,
   "date": "2026-10-09T22:04:11Z", "checkRun": "https://github.com/deskree-inc/oby/runs/41800000001"}
  ```

  `verdict` is `approved` or `changesRequested`; `job` the review job; `agent` and `account` the reviewer's tool and account name (`sameAccount` is true when it ran on the author's own account because no other had room, else absent); `round` which of Oby's reviews of this pull request it is (from 1: rounds that fix the last blocker are reviewed however many it takes); `checkRun` the check run it was written as, or absent with `postError` saying why it wasn't (a possible secret in the review, `gh` failing, or the GitHub app missing or without Checks: write: `Not posted: the Oby app needs Checks: Read and write on its installation.`). Reviews from runners before check runs carry `comment`, the pull request comment they were posted as, instead. `applying` is true from the moment the verdict is recorded until its check run is posted and the author's round started (or the approval's Mac check queued), and absent after: it's cleared in the same save as the round (after the round's message is in `restarts.json`) or the Mac check, so a restart anywhere before leaves it set, and the runner does them when it starts again ("After any restart"). `reviewError` says why no review ran or none gave a verdict (no account with room, the reviewer failed, was stopped or interrupted, or gave no verdict), else null.
- **The check run** (#198). GitHub doesn't let the user approve or request changes on their own pull request, and a comment from the app emails the owner, so the runner writes the review as a check run named `Oby review` on the commit the reviewer read, as the GitHub app (`POST repos/{owner}/{repo}/check-runs`, `status` `completed`): `conclusion` `success` when approved and `failure` when changes are requested, titled `Oby review · Claude Code · Personal: Approved` (or `Changes requested`; `Oby review · Codex · Deskree (same account as the author): Approved` when it ran on the author's own account), its summary the review itself in a code block (so no mention, link or image in it takes effect) and a `by Oby` line. A check run sends nobody an email. Another review of the same commit updates the app's own `Oby review` check run there (`PATCH …/check-runs/{id}`) rather than adding one. The secret scan gates it, with the same rules and encoded copies as publishing: a possible secret means no check run, and the verdict still counts. Only a GitHub app can write check runs, and only with **Checks: Read and write**: with no app, or until the owner grants that permission, nothing goes on the pull request, the review stays in Oby (the job, the inbox, the apps), `postError` says why, and [`GET /v1/status`](#get-v1status) says `"checks": "missing permission"` (or `"no app"`). Oby never comments on a pull request. A written check run is listed in the author job's `outgoing`.
- **The Review stage** shows GitHub's decision when it has one, else Oby's: `Oby is reviewing` (current), `Approved by Oby` (done, with Merged following the Mac check, below), or `Changes requested` (failed, reason `Oby requested changes.`). A failed review leaves it current with `Waiting for review · <reviewError>`. A merged pull request with no GitHub decision reads `Approved by Oby` or `Oby requested changes` instead of `Merged without review`.
- **Changes requested** from reviews one through three go to the author's agent as the next round, in the same session and worktree: the review is quoted in `.oby/inputs/review-<round>.md`, never pasted into the prompt. The job goes back to `running` with a new `round` and `step` `Working on Oby's review`; when it publishes, Oby reviews again. Follow-ups start a finding with `Again:` when an earlier blocker was not fixed or came back. Review four includes `.oby/inputs/review-history.md` and reviews the whole pull request, rather than only the last diff. Its changes-requested result stays `done` and needs the user (`reviewNeedsYou`); there is no automatic fifth review. `One more round` lets the author work again and grants one more review. Each review's `blocking` lists its blockers and `blockedBefore` lists the earlier ones.
- **Approved:** the commit the reviewer read is checked on the user's Mac, and the runner merges it into `dev` when that passes (below). A `Blocking:` section containing `(none)` has no blocking findings; an approved follow-up proceeds even at the review cap.
- What needs you now includes a `done`, published job whose escalation review requested changes (`reviewNeedsYou`, while the pull request is open), and one whose merge stopped (`mergeNeedsYou`, Mac check below). Its inbox item id includes the pushed PR head, so the same head has one card and a later head has a new one. Say so where the apps list what needs you ("1 job needs your review").

### Mac check

On obid (2026-10-27), the Mac app's separate Mac checks are gone: `GET /v1/checks/next`, `GET /v1/checks/{id}/bundle`, `POST /v1/checks/{id}/result` and the agents' `oby-check apple` (`/v1/agent/apple-check`) answer `404`. A learned workflow's `checks: "mac"` no longer merges by itself: the job's own check runs boxed and skips the suites only the Mac runs unboxed (MacTests, `swift test`; scripts/oby-check.sh), and nothing else runs them on the pushed head, so once everything else holds the owner gets a card (`Not merged: the checks this workflow waits for on this Mac don't run by themselves any more, so it's yours to merge.`). A job with no check at all never merges by itself under such a workflow either (`Not merged: no check ran on its head (this repository has none), so it's yours to merge.`). A job's `macCheck`, `appleCheck` and `appleRan` stay in its JSON (null on new jobs) for the apps that decode them.

### Xcode Cloud

Xcode Cloud builds and tests every pull request into `dev` (the "Pull Requests" workflow), as GitHub check runs like `Test - iOS` (#122). GitHub Actions stays off. Each time the runner follows a published pull request (every 3 minutes, with the same login), it reads the pull request's head (`headRefOid`), then that commit's check runs (`repos/<repo>/commits/<sha>/check-runs`, every page). Only Xcode Cloud's runs on that commit count, and of each task only the newest run (a rerun replaces the one before it); Oby's own check runs aren't Xcode Cloud's. Each failed task's failure annotations (failing tests, compiler errors) are read too, and come first.

- `xcodeCloud`: null until the pull request has Xcode Cloud checks, otherwise `{head, state, details, conflict, checkedAt, rounds, handedOff, problem, stops}`. `state` is `pending`, `passed` (every task succeeded) or `failed` (one finished otherwise). `details` is each task's conclusion and what it reported, 16,000 characters at most. `conflict`: Xcode Cloud said “Your source code changes conflict with changes made on the pull request target branch”. `rounds` counts the fix rounds Oby handed to the author, on any head. `handedOff`: this head's failure reached the author. `problem`: why Oby couldn't hand it on, or null, and `stops` how many times that happened on this head (each brings its own inbox item). Once the pull request has Cloud checks, a new head is `pending` until its own appear.
- `xcodeCloudError`: null, or `Couldn't refresh Xcode Cloud. Will try again shortly.` The last result stays, and the merge waits until a read succeeds.
- **A failed build or test** goes back to the author's agent by itself, once (`rounds` 1), as its next round in the same session and worktree: `running`, a new `round`, `step` `Working on Xcode Cloud`. What Xcode Cloud reported is vault-filtered and quoted as data, every line, in `.oby/inputs/xcode-cloud-<the head's first 12 characters>.md`. When the round ends, its commits publish by themselves, Oby reviews them (one more review is allowed for it, `extraReviews`), and Xcode Cloud runs on the new head.
- **A conflict** is rebased as one found at merge is (Mac check, “Conflicts with `dev`”, above): the runner fetches `dev`, and the author's agent rebases onto it, `step` `Rebasing onto dev`, never merging `dev` in. Its limits are the rebase's, the same `dev` twice or 6 rebases in a day; then `problem` is `Xcode Cloud says it conflicts with dev.` and why.
- `cloudNeedsYou`: Xcode Cloud still fails on the pull request's head, and Oby won't hand it back by itself: its fix round was used (`Xcode Cloud still fails after Oby's fix round. Take it from here.`), the round on this head brought nothing new or failed (`Xcode Cloud still fails, and the round on it brought nothing new. Take it from here.`), or `problem`. Only while the job isn't running, its pull request is open and it isn't dismissed. It counts in what needs you and brings a `failed` inbox item offering **One more round** (the author gets the failure again, past the limit; a conflict is fetched and rebased again, even onto the same `dev`), **Ask Oby** (the author's agent reads it again and explains what fails and what you could do, changing nothing) and **Dismiss**. While Oby reviews the pull request, One more round answers why it can't start yet, and the item stays open. Nothing reaches the inbox while Oby handles it.
- The pipeline adds a `cloud` stage, **Xcode Cloud**, before Merged: `Pending` (`current`), `Passed` (`done`) or `Failed` (`failed`, `reason` the problem or what failed). Only a result on the pull request's head completes it.
- **The merge waits** for Xcode Cloud to pass on the commit the Mac checked, whenever the pull request has its checks: the Merged stage says `Mac check passed · waiting for Xcode Cloud`, with no merge error or inbox item, and the merge goes on when the follower sees it pass. Right before merging, the runner reads that commit's checks again, as the GitHub app; a failed read (`xcodeCloudError`, until the follower reads it again) or a result that isn't `passed` keeps it waiting, with no merge error and none of the merge's tries used. A pull request without Xcode Cloud checks merges as before.

### Connection

On obid (2026-10-27), connected apps are gone: `GET`/`POST /v1/connections` and `DELETE /v1/connections/{name}` answer `404` (the apps treat that as none), and jobs get no connected app. GitHub is the only outside connection (docs/trim.md, batch 2), through `gh`. The per-job broker stays: Codex and Cursor agents reach `oby_ask_owner` on `/v1/agent/mcp/{job}` with the job's token, and Tetrix's server is `/v1/agent/tetrix/{job}` (obid only: Tetrix), whose calls are in the job's `connectionUses`.

### Inbox item

Something only the user can answer (docs/pipeline-cases.md, "Your inbox"). Like email, it waits for them with no timeout, and its job waits too; the others keep going.

```json
{"id": "ab12cd34-question-0", "kind": "question", "job": "ab12cd34", "title": "Nothing committed status",
 "text": "Should a job that commits nothing end as failed, or get a new status?", "options": ["Failed", "A new status"],
 "created": "2026-10-10T09:12:00Z", "done": null, "later": null, "answer": null, "state": "open",
 "headline": "Pick a status for empty jobs", "happened": "The job found nothing to commit. It needs to know how to end.",
 "recommended": 1, "why": "A new status keeps real failures apart."}
```

- `kind`, and what each offers in `options`:

  | `kind` | Comes when | `text` | `options` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `question` | the job's agent asked (`question` on the job) | the question | the agent's, possibly none |
  | `review` | Oby's review stopped asking for changes (`reviewNeedsYou`) | `Oby's review found a blocker it asked to fix before. Take it from here.` | `One more round`, `Dismiss` |
  | `failed` | the job ended `failed` or `interrupted` | the summary's first line | `Try again`, `Dismiss` (only `Dismiss` for a job that never started) |
  | `failed` (obid, `<job>-review-<review job>`) | no reviewer could review the job's pushed head (`reviewNeedsYou`, obid only: Oby's review) | its `reviewError`, `Oby couldn't review #7: <why>` | `Try again`: Oby's review runs again (never the job's agent) |
  | `limit` | The Swift runner’s per-job usage-limit item. Obid groups stopped jobs in the account-limit decision API instead (#1563). | The summary’s first line | `Try again`, `Dismiss` on the Swift runner. |
  | `merge` | Oby's merge stopped (`mergeNeedsYou`, Mac check below) | `mergeError`, or why the check failed and that a person takes it from here | `Try again` and `Dismiss` when it couldn't merge (merge again, or hand the check to the author again), when the Mac couldn't run the check, when the round brought no new commit, or after the last failed check (check the same commit again, as the same round); `One more round` first, then `Try again` and `Dismiss`, when the check named failing tests after Oby's last review (its reviews used up): the failing tests go to the author as its next round, and Oby may review the fix once more (`extraReviews`) |
  | `audit` | the runner's daily issue/board reconciliation (Issue sync, below): a Status column the roadmap lacks (title `Roadmap column <column>`), or a report that something failed (title `Daily issue audit`) | which column is missing and the issues that belong there; or the drift count and what failed | for a missing column, `Add the column` (recommended: `recommended` is 0), `Use <closest column> instead` (when the roadmap has one before it: `In review` for `Needs test`, `In progress` for `In review` or `Blocked`) and `Ignore this status`; for failures, `Try again` |
  | `failure` | a failure's post-mortem found an Oby bug, or a cause seen twice in 7 days, with no fix for it merged into `dev`, queued, running or in review (Failure record, below); one open per signature, aliases included | the cause, how often it was seen this week, and what happened | `Fix it`, `Dismiss` |
  | `start` | rules guessed the plan, research found work that needs the user's go-ahead, or (obid only) Oby offered work in a conversation (`oby_offer`, docs/conversation-harness.md): one item per job, `job` its id (the job isn't in `GET /v1/jobs` until it starts), `title` its heading | `Oby's brain didn't answer, so this plan is a guess. Start Fix oby #12 on Claude Code · Deskree?` or `Oby looked into it first. Start Fix oby #12 on Claude Code · Deskree?` | `Start` (recommended: `recommended` is 0), `Cancel`, each with what it does (`details`) |
  | `permission` | the job's agent waits for the user's OK (`permission` on the job) | the permission's `title` | `Allow`, `Always allow`, `Deny` (only `Allow` and `Deny` when it has no `rule`) |
  | `plan` | obid only (#609): a Claude Code or Cursor job in `plan` mode presented its plan (Claude Code's `ExitPlanMode`, Cursor's `cursor/create_plan`, #1008) and waits for the owner (`permission` on the job, its `tool` `ExitPlanMode`); `details` holds the plan as the agent wrote it, quoted data. Only the owner answers it: the brain is refused either way | `Approve the plan?` | `Approve`, `Keep planning`; any other words keep it planning, with them |
  | `agentQuestion` | obid only (#858): a job's agent asked the owner a question (Claude Code's `AskUserQuestion`, Codex's and Cursor's own questions or Oby's `oby_ask_owner`; `permission` on the job, its `question` set), in any mode; one item per question of the call, one after another, its `id` the permission's like a `permission` item's. `details` holds each option's description, in the options' order, when any has one; the question and options are the agent's, quoted data. Only the owner answers it: the brain is refused. Its briefing names the item without the question's words, which reach the brain only in its quoted file, as data, as a plan's do. The Electron app shows it on the job's card as Sangam's Question card (the question its headline, the options its buttons, then Tell it something, which takes the owner's own words, and Later) and answers the item itself, `POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer` (#1570) | the question | its options' labels; any other words are the answer, as the owner wrote them |
  | `confirm` | obid only (#546): Oby's brain planned something that persists or widens trust, which it never does in a turn (obid only: the brain, below); `title` says exactly what `Confirm` does, like `Always allow Bash(make lint:*) in acme/app`, and `job` is the job it's about, or empty | what was planned and why it waits | `Confirm`, `Cancel`, none recommended |
  | `github` | obid only: a read of the owner's GitHub work found a new review request, an assigned issue on a repository obid works in, or failed checks on a new head of the owner's own pull request (obid only: GitHub work); one per item (per head for failed checks), ever | `Your review is requested on pull request #702 in obi-ai-bot/obi.`, `Issue #640 in obi-ai-bot/obi is assigned to you.`, `People wrote on Oby's pull request #812 in obi-ai-bot/obi.` or `Checks failed on your pull request #812 in obi-ai-bot/obi.` | `Review it`, `Fix it` or `Fix CI`, `Dismiss` |
  | `watch` | obid only, from before the cut of Slack and its watcher (docs/trim.md): an item the watcher made then, still listed until dismissed | `Dmitry wrote in #tetrix-avenga-testing.` | `Dismiss` |
  | `listen` | obid only: the owner's review of a pull request is ready to post (`GET /v1/github/work`, "Posting it"); older items from the Slack channel listener stay listed until dismissed | `Post this review on pull request #835 in obi-ai-bot/obi as you? Approve posts the review shown here with Oby's verdict, from your GitHub login.` | a review's: `Approve`, `Dismiss` |

  A `confirm` item (obid only) also carries `confirm`, what its `Confirm` carries out: `{"action": "always_allow", "target": "ab12cd34", "payload": {"permission": "p1a2b3c"}}`. `action` is a typed name, `target` what it acts on (a job's id or an inbox item's id, as the action says) and `payload` what else it needs (`null` when nothing). obid carries every action out through one dispatcher, so a new action is a new name with its own `target` and `payload`, the shape unchanged. The actions so far:

  | `action` | `target` | `payload` | `Confirm` does |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `always_allow` | a job | `{"permission": "<id>"}` | answers that job's waiting permission with always, saving its rule |
  | `set_mode_bypass` | a running job | `null` | puts the job in Bypass mode (`409` once it isn't running) |
  | `start_job` | the conversation that planned it | `{"job": …}`, the job as `POST /v1/jobs` takes it | queues the job, in Bypass mode |
  | `accept` | an `obiRequest` item | `null` | answers it `Accept` |
  | `accept_remember` | an `obiRequest` item | `null` | answers it `Accept, and remember for this repo` |
  | `decline_remember` | an `obiRequest` item | `null` | answers it `Decline, and remember for this repo` |
  | `answer_question` | an `obiRequest` question | `{"words": "…"}` | answers the other Obi's question with those words |
  | `start` | a `start` item | `null` | answers it `Start`: the proposed job queues |
  | `answer_item` | any other item | `{"answer": "…"}` | answers it with those words |
  | `message_job` | a job that ended | `{"words": "…"}`, the user's own words, secrets taken out | starts a follow-up of the job with the words, as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages` does (made before 2026-10-18, when it ran one more round; obid makes none now) |
  | `add_repository` | a review request in a repository obid doesn't work in: its `github` offer's id (#1118) | `{"repo", "number"}`, obid's own from the feed's typed fields | adds the repository and starts Oby's review (obid only: repositories, "A review request's repository, on the owner's tap"), and answers the offer `Review it`; `409` once the offer is settled |
  | `dismiss_job` | a job whose commits aren't published, or another conversation's (#631) | `null` | dismisses the job, as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/dismiss` does; its commits stay on its branch |
  | `retry_job` | another conversation's job that failed, was stopped or was cut off (#631) | `null` | tries it again with Try again's words (`409` once it's running) |
  | `continue_job` | a job that failed, was stopped or needs you (`interrupted`: a stalled one too, with `stalled` set) | `{"words": "…", "snapshot": …}`: what the conversation's turn added, secrets taken out | continues it in its own session, as `oby_continue_job` does (`409` once it's running) |
  | `delete_job` | a job that ended | `{"snapshot": …}` | deletes the job, as `DELETE /v1/jobs/{id}` does: its record, worktree and local branch; never anything pushed |
  | `change_job` | a job | `{"mode": "…"}`, `{"agent": "…", "account": "…"}` or both, with `snapshot` | changes the job's mode, tool or account, as `oby_change_job` does |
  | `set_check` | a repository (#1062) | `{"check": "<command>", "was": "<its check now>" \| null}`: obid's own guess, or the owner's command (`POST /v1/repos/check`); `"check": null` takes `was` out | makes the command the repository's check, kept on its entry in the configuration file's `repos` (a GitHub one is listed then), so every later job there runs it, or takes `was` out (`409` when its check changed since); either way No check is forgotten. The job that asked goes on with none. Its `Cancel` keeps what the repository has; with no check there, it's No check, remembered (Job, above) |

  `snapshot` (on `continue_job`, `delete_job` and `change_job`) is the job as it was when Oby asked: `{round, status, commits, unpublished, mode, agent, account}`. Confirmed after any of them changed (another round, a commit, a move, a new mode), it's refused with `409` (`The job changed since Oby asked (another round, a move or a new mode): ask Oby again.`) and nothing happens, so the owner never confirms something other than what the title said. An unknown `action` is refused with `409`. Every title is obid's own words, made from the action's target, never the brain's: `Always allow <rule> in <repo>` (the rule it saves, which can be broader than the one command), `Accept: Review request from ernesto`, `Send an answer: Question from Bob`, `Dismiss Average helper empty lists (2 commits kept on its branch)`, `Try Vault tests again`, `Continue Vault tests` (`Continue Vault tests in Bypass mode` for a job in Bypass), `Delete Vault tests (its worktree and local branch go; 2 commits that aren't published are lost)` (`its worktree goes; its branch stays, another job uses it` when it's shared), `Put Vault tests in Bypass mode`, `Move Vault tests to Codex · Deskree`, `Start fix fixture-app (Greeting fix) in Bypass mode, on Claude Code · Personal, check node --test` (the job obid keeps: what, the mode, the tool and account, the check). Words the brain wrote for an answer are in `payload`, and quoted in `text` as `> ` lines, never in the title. Two `Confirm`s at the same moment carry it out once: the first claims the item, and the other is `409`. The apps don't show `confirm` items yet: the Mac app drops an item kind it can't decode, and the web app's list can let one stand in for its job's waiting permission; a separate apps change follows #552.

  **Only decisions wait in the inbox (#215).** Every item offers at least one answer that changes something, besides done and later: a `question` takes the agent's options or the user's words, and every other kind declares its own (above). An item with nothing to decide arrives already `done`, with no `answer`: it's kept in `oby.sqlite` with the reports (the job's own report, or the audit's `outgoing`) and comes as an `inbox` event whose `state` is `done`, so it never shows in the inbox. That's the audit's report when it fixed the drift by itself, and a `failure` whose fix is already underway. A new kind declares its answers, or none of its items reach the inbox.

  More kinds will come (hands-on steps): show one you don't know with its `text` and `options`. A plan waiting for approval is a `question` item (its job's `question` has the `plan`).
- `details`: what each option does, one plain sentence each, in the same order as `options` (as Claude Code describes the options it asks with), shown under each button; null when there are none (older items, or a question whose agent gave none). Oby's own items always have them; a `question` has them when its agent wrote them (`question` on the job, above). Older runners don't send the key.
- `job`: the job's `id`, `runner-update` for the runner's own update, or empty for an audit item or a failure (it's about Oby, not one job). `title`: the job's title, "Daily issue audit", `Roadmap column <column>`, or `Oby failure: <signature>`. Optional `outgoing` on audits lists repairs using the same records as a job's report. `conversation` (obid only): the conversation a `start` item was proposed in (for one made before obid kept that, its job's), or a confirm that starts a job came from, else null; the apps show an item with no `job` opened out only in that conversation, never in another (a conversation shows the decisions of the jobs it started, as before). `offer` (obid only, on a `start` item Oby offered, else absent): `{"id", "what", "task", "untrusted"}`, the card it's on in that conversation and what it shows. `id` is the user's turn it was offered in, so one turn's offers are one card under Oby's answer to that turn; for an event turn's, which no user turn holds, it is `offer-` and 8 hexadecimal digits, a card of its own. `what` is obid's own words for the job it starts (`build fixture-app (Greeting), on auto · auto`), `task` the task Oby planned (every secret obid knows taken out), and `untrusted` whether the turn had read something untrusted first (the job is marked so, and runs in the owner's mode: the owner's decision of 2026-10-27). Answer each item of the card as any `start` item. Every answer to a `start` item settles it under obid's lock, so of two at the same moment only the first counts: a second `Start` is `409`, and so is a `later` or `Cancel` after a `Start`. An offer nobody answered in 24 hours is done by itself, with no options and no `answer`, its `text` `Closed by Oby: offered a day ago and never started. Ask Oby again to start it.` above its words, and a `Start` on one past its day closes it so and is `409` with that sentence, though nothing read the inbox since; deleting its conversation closes it the same way (`Closed by Oby: the conversation it was offered in was deleted.`). The relay pushes one alert per offer (`collapse` its `id`, titled `Oby offers <n> jobs` when there are several), not one per item: obid can't tell which conversation the owner is looking at.
- `headline`, `happened`, `recommended`, `why`: the item in plain words, for a card. `headline` says what it's about in at most 8 plain words; `happened` what happened, in one or two short sentences with no ids or hashes; `recommended` the index into `options` of the answer Oby recommends, or null (when Oby or the job's agent recommends one already, like `Add the column`, it's set from the start and the model only says why); `why` one sentence saying why. The runner's brain model (Haiku, through the same `claude` as the brain, with no tool but reading the quoted item) writes them in the background, once per item and text: what the item says, its title and its options reach the model only as a quoted file, never in its prompt. The runner checks the lengths, the sentences, that there's no id or hash, and that `recommended` is one of `options`. The item arrives at once with all four null and they follow in an `inbox` event; when the model fails, is slow (20 s) or writes something the runner refuses, they stay null for good. `text` is always the full detail: show it when they're null, and under them otherwise. Older runners don't send these keys.
- `created`: when it came. Apps list the open ones in the order `GET /v1/inbox` gives (the longest-waiting first; on obid what needs the owner first), with how long each has waited ("waiting 3m").
- `triage` (obid only, #1123): `needs_you`, `fyi`, `ignore` or `pending`: the item needs the owner, asks nothing of them now, is noise, or is an outside item still waiting for Oby's quick model to sort it. Notify for `needs_you` only, never for `pending`: an `inbox` event brings the item again once it's sorted, and one still `pending` after 2 minutes (no Claude Code account, the hourly limit, a slow model) becomes `needs_you`, which an `inbox` event says too. A sort can change while the item is open (a second item about the same thing is `ignore` only while the first is open): `GET /v1/inbox` has the latest. Sorting never acts: every item stays listed and answerable (obid only: sorting the inbox, below). Absent on a runner that doesn't sort (the Swift runner): treat it as `needs_you`.
- `done`: when it was settled, or null. `answer`: what the user answered, or null (done without an answer, or settled by itself).
- `later`: put off until then, or null. `state`: `open`, `later` (put off until a time still to come) or `done`. Apps show a `later` item again once its time has passed.
- One open item per job: a newer item for the same job settles the older (done, with no `answer`), and audits share one per `title` (an empty `job`), so a newer report settles the older report, never a missing column's decision. Failures keep their own rule, one open per signature.
- An item comes once: each failure (even a retry that fails again within the same second), each question and each fourth review has its own `id`, and one that's done never comes back. It's done by itself, with no `answer`, when its job needs it no more: the job runs again (told something, or Try again from either app), was dismissed or stopped, or its question was answered with a message.
- Kept in `oby.sqlite` with the jobs, so a restart keeps the inbox, and the runner loads the job of every open item however old it is (and, for a `review` item, its review), so it can still be answered. A job's own alert (below) still says it needs you; the inbox is where you answer.

### Account

```json
{"id": "default", "agent": "claude", "name": "Personal", "payer": "Alex", "label": "Max · someone@example.com",
 "signedIn": true, "fiveHour": 0.12, "sevenDay": 0.4,
 "fiveHourResets": "2026-10-10T02:14:40Z", "sevenDayResets": "2026-10-14T19:27:22Z", "running": 1, "fullUntil": null,
 "tetrix": true, "tetrixJobs": 12, "tetrixUsed": 9, "reserved": false, "reserveLiftedUntil": null}
```

- `agent` is `claude`, `codex` or `cursor`. `label` is what the tool says about the login (null while signed out). Its first part is the plan (`Max`, `Team`, `Plus`), which Settings shows next to the account's name. A Cursor account's label comes from `cursor-agent status` (boxed, with the account's own `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR`; `Signed in` when it says neither way but `cursor-agent models` lists models), and Oby never reads Cursor's token files (its login is a file under the account's own `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`). Cursor reports no usage: `fiveHour` and `sevenDay` stay null, and its card says so. obid sends `label` null but for a Codex login whose access token ends within 5 minutes: `Run codex once` (only Codex itself refreshes it; `signedIn` is false meanwhile). A Codex login OpenAI refused (a run ended on `workspace routing discovery unauthorized (401)`, `401 Unauthorized`, `token_revoked` or `refresh_token_invalidated`, though its token's `exp` is still ahead) reads `Run codex login` with `signedIn` false until the login holds another token (the owner signed in again); obid keeps only a hash of the refused token, in memory, so a restart tries it once more.
- `payer` (obid only): who pays for the account, as the owner put it in Settings › Accounts ("Paid by Deskree"), or null. The Swift runner doesn't send it.
- `fiveHour` and `sevenDay` are 0…1 or null when unknown. An account with both null hasn't reported usage yet. When an app looks (`GET /v1/status`, or its `GET /v1/events` stream opening), obid asks each signed-in Claude Code and Codex account not asked in the last 10 minutes, in the background, and the answer comes in an `accounts` event; while no app looks nothing is read, and a running job reports its own. Neither read asks a model: Claude Code's own `/usage` (`claude -p /usage --model haiku --max-turns 1 --output-format stream-json --verbose`, its `usage_report`: `session` is `fiveHour`, `weekly_all` is `sevenDay`; its whole percents never mark an account `fullUntil`) and Codex's `account/rateLimits/read` from an app-server with no thread, each boxed with its own copy of the account's login as the brain's run is. A read never refreshes a login: a Claude Code copy within 10 minutes of its end isn't read with. An account whose Claude Code asked a model for `/usage` (one without its own) isn't asked again until obid restarts; one that answered with no usage (an API key's) or failed (offline, a refused login) is asked again after the 10 minutes. No read starts while obid is stopping. The Swift runner's Claude Code limits arrive with its first job.
- `fiveHourResets` and `sevenDayResets`: when each window resets, or null when the tool didn't say. Claude Code sends them in its `rate_limit_event` (`resetsAt` per window) and its `/usage` (`resets_at`), Codex in `account/rateLimits/read` and its `account/rateLimits/updated` notifications (`resetsAt`). A window keeps its last reset time until the tool sends a new one. Older runners don't send these keys.
- `running`: jobs queued or running on the account.
- `fullUntil` is set while a job of the account's hit its usage limit: no job goes to it before then. Accounts are signed in on the runner itself, through each tool's own login; the app only shows them.
- `tetrix`: whether the account's agents have Tetrix's MCP server connected, or null before the runner knows. The runner asks on startup (Claude Code: `claude mcp list`, with claude.ai's connectors off as for agents; Codex: its app-server's `mcpServerStatus/list`), and every job's tool says it again as its session starts. `tetrixJobs`: of the account's recent jobs (the latest 50 the runner keeps in memory) that ended and whose tool said what it had, how many there are; `tetrixUsed`: how many of them called Tetrix. Settings shows `Tetrix: connected · used in 9 of 12 recent jobs (75%)`, or `Tetrix: not connected: sign in` (signing in is the tool's own login for its `tetrix` server, on the runner; never Oby holding the token). Older runners don't send these keys.
- `reserved`: the account is at 80% of its week before `sevenDayResets`, so it gets only jobs sent to it by name (and reviews, last: Routing below). False while its reserve is lifted. `reserveLiftedUntil`: until when the user let it past its reserve (`POST /v1/accounts/{id}/reserve`), its week's reset; null when they didn't, or once that time has passed. Older runners don't send these keys.

Accounts are signed in on the runner itself, through each tool's own login; the app only shows them (on obid the app can add, rename and remove them, and start that login: [obid only: accounts](#obid-only-accounts)). Settings shows one card per account, grouped by tool, with a bar per window, its reset time, and the running count.

**Routing.** Oby budgets each subscription by its weekly pace, not just the 5-hour window: new work goes to accounts on pace, accounts running ahead of pace get reviews or nothing, and the account with the most weekly room per remaining day wins. A tool that reports no usage (Cursor) comes last, after the reserved accounts: it takes automatic work only when nothing else can, or when named (`agent: "cursor"`, or a move to it); among its accounts, the fewest running jobs, then list order, and the report says `Cursor reports no usage: fewest jobs running`. Cursor accounts keep separate logins (docs/cursor.md, proven on tetrix-lab): each runs with a `HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR` of its own, so any number can be signed in. Every automatic choice goes through it (the brain's dispatch, the Lead's picks, failure fixes, failover, reviews); a job named to an account skips it.

- An account gets nothing while it's signed out, marked `fullUntil`, at 95% of either window, or at 80% of its week (`reserved`: kept for jobs sent to it by name until `sevenDayResets`; a review may take it, last). Above 80% of its 5-hour window it gets work only when every account is.
- **Use anyway:** the user can lift an account's reserve until its week resets (`POST /v1/accounts/{id}/reserve`): it then takes new work, failovers, the Lead's picks and moves like any account, still never at 95% of a window or while marked full. When only reserves keep a request from starting, its job still starts, waiting for room (`startWhen` `[{"room": true}]`, on the first of those accounts until then), and the reply says so: `On it. Claude Code will fix oby on Personal once what it waits for has happened. Waiting: Personal and Codex are in their weekly reserve. Use one anyway to start it now; otherwise it starts when an account has room.` Queued, the job's `waiting.reserved` names those accounts, and the Mac's and the iPhone's job screens show `Use Personal anyway (82% of week)` for each one still under 95% of both windows, with `Oby keeps 20% of each week in reserve; this lifts it for Personal until Thu 7:00 PM.` under it: one tap lifts that reserve and the job starts there. A failover with nowhere to go, and the Lead's jobs that found no account, say `Personal and Codex are in their weekly reserve: use one anyway from Settings › Accounts.` Settings shows `Use past reserve this week` on an account in its reserve, with `Oby keeps 20% of each week in reserve; this lifts it for Personal until Thu 7:00 PM.`
- **Room per remaining day:** `(1 − sevenDay) / daysLeft`, `daysLeft` from `sevenDayResets` (at least 0.25; 7 when null). Higher is better.
- **Ahead of pace:** `sevenDay / max(weekGone, 1/7) > 1`, the share used over the share of the week gone: at this burn it runs out before its reset. The floor keeps the first hours of a week from swinging it.
- **New work** takes an account on pace first, then one whose `sevenDay` is null (unknown, not 0%: Claude Code reports usage only during a job), then one ahead of pace. **A review** takes one ahead of pace first, then on pace, then unknown; the other tool still comes first.
- Within those, the most room per day; within 0.01/day of it, the fewest `running`, then list order.
- The job's report says why, once (a review's, whose last line is its verdict, in the runner's log): `On Claude Code · Deskree: most weekly room per day left (0.12/day), though 1.4× ahead of pace: none is on pace; Codex · Deskree is 2.3× ahead of pace.` After failover, the handoff says it: `Codex · Deskree hit its limit; continued on Claude Code · Default: its usage isn't known yet.`

### No Dispatch card (#269)

There's no draft to confirm any more: the work the brain plans starts as it's planned, like the Lead's, and `POST /v1/plans` answers the jobs it started. Everything the card let you change before the start changes after it: the account, tool or model (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/move`), the mode (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/mode`), what it waits for (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/start-when`), a message to the agent, Stop. The outgoing gates don't move: checks, the secret scan and Oby's review still gate every push and merge. A rules guess or dispatch after research waits in a `start` inbox item (Inbox, below), and nothing starts until its `Start`.

## Endpoints

### `GET /v1/status`

```json
{"runner": {"version": "0.2.0", "host": "tetrix-lab"},
 "accounts": [ …Account ],
 "models": {"claude": {"model": "claude-opus-5-5", "effort": "xhigh", "complexEffort": "xhigh"}, "codex": {"model": "gpt-6-sol", "effort": "high", "complexEffort": "high"}, "cursor": {"model": "auto"}},
 "maxRunning": 6, "running": 4, "defaultAgent": "auto",
 "clearedAt": "2026-10-10T08:12:00Z",
 "github": "app", "checks": "ok", "permissions": {"mode": "sandbox"},
 "cleanup": {"path": "/srv/nvme/oby/jobs", "bytes": 8912896, "lastRun": "2026-10-13T08:00:00Z", "kept": []},
 "memory": {"total": 17179869184, "available": 4026531840, "pressure": "normal",
            "jobs": [{"id": "ab12cd34", "rss": 912261120, "peakRss": 1610612736, "agentPeakRss": 536870912, "checkPeakRss": 1073741824}],
            "waiting": [{"id": "ef56ab78", "budget": 1610612736, "since": "2026-10-24T23:40:00Z"}]}}
```

`permissions.mode` is Settings › Permissions: the mode new jobs get (`PUT /v1/settings/permissions`). Older runners don't send the key.

`memory` (obid only): the machine's memory and what each running job's processes hold, in bytes. `total` is its RAM. `available` is what new work can take without the system compressing or swapping what's there: free memory plus what it drops for free (cached files, purgeable memory). On macOS that's `total` less Activity Monitor's Memory Used (app memory, wired and compressed, from `vm_stat`); memory the system could still compress isn't counted, since a Mac at 46% by `kern.memorystatus_level` was compressing 5.4 GB and swapping (2026-10-24). On Linux it's `/proc/meminfo`'s `MemAvailable`, the host's: a cgroup obid runs in (its `memory.max`, `memory.pressure`) isn't read. `pressure` is `normal`, `warn` or `critical`: the kernel's own level on macOS (`kern.memorystatus_vm_pressure_level`); on Linux `warn` when tasks waited on memory for 10% of the last 10 seconds (`/proc/pressure/memory`'s `some avg10`) or less than a tenth of `total` is available, `critical` when all of them did (`full avg10`) or less than a twentieth is. `jobs` has each running job's processes, `{id, rss, peakRss, agentPeakRss, checkPeakRss}`: `rss` is the resident memory of every process its agent and its check started, now, `peakRss` the most they held at once since it started, and `agentPeakRss` and `checkPeakRss` its agent's tree's and its check's, each at its most (each one leads a session of its own, so its tree is it, what descends from it and what's still in its group; memory two processes share counts in each). obid reads it every 5 seconds while a job runs (`sysctl`, `vm_stat` and `ps` on macOS, `/proc` on Linux; a few milliseconds each time) and not at all while none does; status asked then reads the machine's part afresh, at most every 5 seconds. A job's worker that ends keeps its `peakRss` on the job (Job, above). `waiting` is always `[]`: an obid before 2026-10-28 listed there each queued job it held back for memory (`{id, budget, since}`), and none is held back now (Jobs run side by side, above); the key stays for the apps that decode it. Left out when it can't be read. Older runners don't send the key.

`gateway` (obid only): the gateway's state, as "obid only: the gateway" says. Left out with no `gateway` configured. Older runners don't send the key.

`tunnel` (obid only): always `{"state": "off", "detail": null}` since the Cloudflare Tunnel went (2026-10-27), kept for the apps that decode it.

`vaultProblem` (obid only): why the vault can't be read, as "obid only: the vault" says; meanwhile every other `/v1` answer is `503`, but for the event stream once obid has read the vault. Left out while it reads. On obid the status looks again for a tool installed or signed in since it started at most every 2 seconds.

`availableLogins` (obid only): the tools' own logins obid found on this Mac that aren't accounts (looked for at start and again at every status, so a tool installed and signed in since, as during first launch, shows up without a restart), `[{"agent": "codex", "id": "codex", "name": "Codex"}]`; `POST /v1/accounts` `{"agent", "default": true}` lists one on the owner's word (obid only: accounts, "Logins already on the Mac"). Empty when there's none.

`folders` (obid only, #1572): `true` when obid takes working folders: `folders` on `POST /v1/plans`, on conversations and on `PATCH /v1/conversations/{id}` (obid only: working folders, below). Older runners don't send the key, and refuse `folders` with a `400`: an app sends none until it reads `true`.

`cleanup` is the runner's job-worktree disk usage and cleanup status. `path` is the worktree folder, `bytes` is its allocated disk usage, and `lastRun` is when the last cleanup pass completed, or null before the first pass. The runner runs cleanup at startup and every three hours. It removes a worktree only for a settled job finished more than 24 hours ago (merged, closed, dismissed or stopped), or immediately after an Oby review job has a verdict; open pull requests, jobs needing the user, queued or waiting jobs, and worktrees in use by an agent, check, publish or merge are kept. Older runners don't send the key. obid answers before it has measured a big worktrees folder (it waits for the walk 200 ms at most) and leaves `cleanup` out until the walk ends, then sends it as the `cleanup` event.

obid's cleanup pass (`home/worktrees/<job id>`) runs 30 seconds after it starts, in the background (its first answers never wait for it), then whenever a job ends, is dismissed or archived, or Clear done is pressed, and every three hours. It removes a job's worktree once the job is settled: it ended and needs nothing (done, failed, stopped, or `interrupted` and dismissed), no worker or publish holds it, its pull request isn't open (merged or closed as obid last read it, or none), no follow-up on its branch is still at work, and it was put away (dismissed, archived, or finished by the last Clear done's `clearedAt`) or ended 24 hours ago; an Oby review job (`kind: review`, `obi` null) as soon as it ends. Even then a worktree stays when what's in it may exist nowhere else: a folder that isn't a git worktree (no `.git`) unless it's empty, one whose artifacts weren't all published (`artifactProblems`; `.obi/artifacts` is never a change git sees), one with uncommitted changes (untracked files too, ignored ones not), one whose HEAD is neither the job's `startCommit`, nor the commit it last pushed, nor on any of the clone's remote branches, and one git can't answer for. Each is in `kept`, `{job, why}`, as of the last pass. git looks at the worktree inside the job's sandbox, without the network, as for the `wip:` commit: the agent could have pointed the worktree's `.git` at a repository of its own, whose filters would otherwise run outside the box. obid runs git for a worktree (this pass, the `wip:` commit and taking it back out, keeping its commits, a run) only when its `.git` leads into obid's clone of the job's repository (its `.git`, and a folder directly in its `worktrees/` whose `gitdir` names this worktree, never another job's); else the worktree stays, `why` "Its .git doesn't point into obid's clone.", and a run fails. The job's own object folder is made only where nothing on the way is a link. The pass checks and takes the worktree away under the lock a job's worktree is made with, with `git worktree remove --force` and `git worktree prune` in obid's clone, and sets the job's `worktree` to null; its branch in the clone stays, so the job page, its diffs (kept on the job) and its artifacts (published when it ended) don't change, and a job that runs again (Try again) or a follow-up gets its worktree made again from its branch. A pass that removed one measures `bytes` again. obid sends `kept` (`[]` before the first pass); older runners don't.

A job done with that keeps its worktree a while yet loses its build outputs on the next pass (obid only): one that ended and needs nothing, no worker or publish holds, whose pull request isn't open (merged or closed: the follower's read that sees it wakes the pass) and whose follow-ups aren't at work, whether or not it was put away or ended 24 hours ago. Those are the folders git ignores (`git ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-standard --directory`) that a build makes again: a cache by its `CACHEDIR.TAG` (the Cache Directory Tagging convention, which Cargo's `target/` follows: a regular file of its own that begins with `Signature: 8a477f597d28d172789f06886806bc55`; a link, a pipe or another first line doesn't count), Xcode's `DerivedData`, and a `node_modules`'s `.cache`. A `node_modules`'s packages stay, and so does a Python virtual environment (a folder with `pyvenv.cfg`, which uv tags as a cache too), since a check runs without the network to install them again; so does anything in `.obi/`, anything reached through a link (the last part of the path included), and the worktree itself, for a follow-up or Try again (which build again). git lists them and `rm -rf` takes them in the job's sandbox, without the network, so a link the agent left leads nowhere outside its worktree. The job says what that freed in `buildFreed` (Job, below), measured before and after off the runtime's threads: an `rm` that stops partway still counts what went, and the log says why it stopped. A pass looks once per end, freed or not, and again after the job runs again and ends. A pass that freed space measures `bytes` again.

`runner.version` is the running API version. `runner.update` adds `{"commit": "<dev SHA>", "waiting": "<candidate SHA>", "phase": "draining"}` for Settings › Runner. `commit` is the running executable's dev commit, or null before its first self-install or after an untracked manual install. `waiting` is null when no update is pending. `phase` is `idle`, `building`, `ready` (built and tested, waiting for its turn to restart), `draining`, `restarting` or `failed`. Clients of older runners accept an absent `update`; refresh status to observe progress.

The runner checks `dev` every three minutes, using its own checkout in `OBY_HOME/updates/source`, outside agent sandboxes. It pins the fetched commit, runs `swift build -c release` then `swift test` (with a throwaway `OBY_HOME`, also as `TEST_RUNNER_OBY_HOME`, like a Mac check, so the tests never open the runner's own data), and drains only after both pass. Drain pauses new jobs, reviews and follow-up rounds while current rounds finish, for at most 30 minutes; a job waiting for the user's answer or OK doesn't hold it up. The drain covers the end of a turn too (#212): the runner restarts only between the steps that follow an agent's last turn, never inside one: counting a job's commits and publishing it, a review's verdict reaching its author (the verdict recorded, the `Oby review` check run posted, then the author's round started or the approval's Mac check queued), and posting a Mac check's results and merging. Past the 30 minutes it still waits for the step under way to end. Updates are batched: when `dev` moves while a tested commit waits (for its turn or for the drain), the newer commit is built and tested and replaces it, and the drain carries on meanwhile, so two merges in quick succession make one restart; if the newer commit fails, the one it was to replace still installs. The runner restarts for an update at most once every 15 minutes (`ready` until then, with no agent held back), unless the update brings the fix of one of Oby's own failures: a fix of an `oby-bug` failure of the last 7 days that merged into `dev` since the last update's restart installs at once. The tested executable is kept as `OBY_HOME/updates/candidate`; it atomically replaces the installed binary, then the runner exits with code 75 so the systemd user service (`Restart=on-failure`) restarts it. Its journal is `OBY_HOME/updates/state.json`, with when the last update restarted the runner.

After the restart, its jobs carry on like after any other restart ("After any restart", below). If `swift test` fails naming tests, the runner waits until the 1-minute load is below the machine's thread count, for up to 10 minutes, then reruns only those tests once with `swift test --filter`. If they pass, the update is tested and may install; Oby records a `flaky-test` failure with the test names and messages for its failure study, and adds no inbox item. If tests still fail, the old binary stays and one durable inbox item (`kind: failed`, `job: runner-update`, `options` `Try again`) names the failing tests and their messages. A failed build or install, or a test failure that cannot be identified by name, is reported immediately; compiler errors include their diagnostic lines. That commit is not retried on later polls or restarts, only by its Try again (#215); the next dev commit is tried. Each try that fails is its own item (`runner-update-<commit>`, then `…-try-2`, `…-try-3`), so a retry that fails again offers Try again again; while one is open, the same commit adds no other. A commit whose build or tests a restart cut short is tried again. A fetch failure adds one item per outage, however many polls or restarts it lasts; once a fetch succeeds, the next outage adds another. No unrelated build output or credentials enter the inbox.

**After any restart.** Whatever restarted the runner (a crash, a reboot, `systemctl restart`, a manual deploy or its own update), every job that was `queued` or `running` when it went down carries on once it's back, after its first refresh of the accounts: stopping the runner leaves them on disk as they were, not `interrupted`. A job with a session picks it up again in its own worktree, keeping its round. On obid it picks up at the phase it was in: one cut short while it ran its check, or while it published, is `running` at once at that phase (so another restart cuts it there again) and runs its check again on what it committed (never its agent's turn, and no prompt is built for one; one with no check goes straight on to publish), and a pass goes on to publish as after its last round, a failure to the next round as any failing check does; words the owner sent meanwhile are its next turn instead. Its agent first hears `Oby's runner restarted while you were working…`, then the message it was about to get, if any: the next round's message, the user's words or answer, Oby's review hand-off or a failed Mac check's hand-off. That message is saved in `OBY_HOME/restarts.json` the moment Oby hands it over (while draining for an update too), and kept until the agent's turn ends, so a turn the restart cut short hears it again; the notice never replaces it. A queued job starts when its worktree is ready, when there's a free slot, oldest first; one whose worktree was set up before the restart is brought up to date with its base first, as a job that waited is. A job waiting on a question keeps waiting, `running` with its question and no agent, until its answer picks the session up (on obid, Claude Code's `AskUserQuestion` and Codex's and Cursor's questions, #858: `permission` above). A job waiting on a permission is `interrupted`, keeping it: its answer picks the session up, telling the agent what it asked and the answer. One stays `interrupted`: a job picked up twice in the last 30 minutes, when the runner keeps going down with it (the runner's own update restarts don't count: it notes them in `restarts.json` before it exits): its summary starts `Oby's runner restarted 3 times in 30 minutes while this ran…`, and it gets one `failed` inbox item with `Try again`. On obid nothing a job ran before the going down is still at work when it carries on: every process group obid starts for a job's run (each turn of its agent, Claude Code, Codex or Cursor, and its check; not the short git calls obid makes in its sandbox) is recorded while it runs in `home/jobs/groups/<pgid>.json` (0600: its leader's id, start time and executable, and the job; the start time is `ps`'s on macOS, the boot's id and `/proc`'s on Linux), and holds a lock in the worktree's own git folder (`obid-run`, the same record). When the start times can't be read (no `/bin/ps`), nothing is recorded and obid says so once in its log. After a going down that didn't end them (a crash, a kill, a panic), the next start ends every recorded group whose leader is still that very process (its id and its start time: an id another process took since is never signalled), SIGTERM then SIGKILL 0.4 seconds later, before any job is picked up again, and logs the job, the executable and the group, never a command line. A run that finds its worktree's lock held by a live run ends it first when obid recorded that run for the same job, and otherwise doesn't start, saying `an earlier run is still at work in this job's worktree (process <pgid>), which obid didn't start for this job`: the agent can write the lock, so it never makes obid signal another job's run. A group whose leader ended while its members still run isn't ended. On obid (#915) a stop that was asked for (SIGTERM or SIGINT, an update, the owner turning it off), however long its jobs take to end, leaves an empty `home/stopped-cleanly`, which the next start reads and removes; any other going down (a crash, a kill) counts against each job that was at work: running with an agent, or queued with nothing it waits for (a worker getting it ready), never one waiting on the owner's question or permission. A job picked up twice already in the last 30 minutes after such a going down stays `interrupted` at the next start, its `reason` `obid went down 3 times in 30 minutes while this ran, so Oby left it here. Try again carries it on.` (its `summary` that sentence, then the earlier summary), `nextStep` `Try again`, with a failure record (`crash`) and one `failed` inbox item (`Try again`, `Dismiss`); the count is kept in the job's file, never in the API, and emptied once the job ends any other way. Telling it something picks its session up with the message it was about to get, then the user's words. A job the user stopped stays `stopped`. Quitting Oby on the Mac still leaves its jobs `interrupted`.
