Its first prompt quotes what the first job did (its title, status, request, report, branch, commits and pull request, every secret obid holds taken out), in a fence with a random word, as data. The first job stays as it ended.
  - **Where it starts.** When the first job has commits that aren't merged, the follow-up's worktree starts at the head of the first job's branch, so its agent has that work, and `startCommit` is that head. Its `commits` are what it publishes (#770), counted with git: what its branch has over the last commit any job pushed to that branch or to the branch it started from (unless that work's pull request was closed unmerged: pushed with no pull request, its commits are out), or, when none is under its head (a branch of its own, or one rebased since), over its base (its pull request's head, for a job on a pull request), the first job's commits included. So a follow-up whose agent adds nothing still ends `done` and publishes the first job's commits (after the check and the secret scan, as any job), and its pull request says which job it follows up; `Nothing committed` is only for a branch with nothing left to publish. Two exceptions publish nothing by themselves: a part of a stalled job's split counts only its own commits (its siblings carry the same ones), and a follow-up that adds none to commits whose pull request was closed without merging (that job's, or the one before the follow-ups between them that published nothing) ends `done` with them counted, unpublished, `reason` `The pull request of <title> was closed without merging, so Oby didn't publish its commits again: Publish opens a new pull request with them.`, Publish the owner's. While the first job's pull request is open (it was published, and obid hasn't read it merged or closed), the follow-up works on that very branch and publishes onto it, updating that pull request instead of opening a second (a push that fast-forwards it; if the branch moved on GitHub, nothing goes out, as for any job). Otherwise it gets a branch of its own (`oby/<kind>-<id>`), started from that head, and opens its own pull request with both jobs' commits. With no such work, it starts from its base (or the pull request's head) on a branch of its own.
  - **One at a time.** A job has one follow-up working at once, and no two jobs work on one branch: while a follow-up of it is queued or running, the words go to that follow-up instead, as if sent to it (`200` with it; `422` naming it, `<title> (<id>): …`, when it can't take them yet), never to a second one. While another job works on the branch the follow-up would take, the words are refused (`422`, naming that job: tell that one instead).
- **Try again:** the exact words `Try again, picking up where you left off. Finish with a short summary as asked.` (what Try again and `retry_job` send), or on obid those words with more after them on lines of their own (`oby_continue_job`'s), pick the agent's session up again in the same worktree and branch instead (a job moved since its last round starts a new session on its new choice: `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/move`). The job keeps its `id`, goes back to `running` and gets a new `round`; when `rounds` is above 1 it grows by one too, so a loop still has a round for this. On obid it's what an `interrupted` job's `nextStep` sends, `One more round` and `Try again` alike, and it clears `reason`, `nextStep`, `limit` and `stalled`. A dismissed job is dismissed no more. It ends as usual, except that this round may end `done` with nothing committed.
- The text is saved in `notes` of the job that hears it, and `step` shows `"You: …"` until the agent moves on.
- **From a task's page** (obid only, Sangam's TaskView): `"conversation": true` puts the words, then Oby's answer, on the end of the job's `conversation`, as if said there, which the app then opens. Oby's answer is Sangam's (`tasks.ts` `relay`): `This one’s finished, so I started a follow-up. <tool> has it in <repository> on <account>.` (the follow-up's card on it, `jobs`), else `Passed to <tool>. It’ll pick that up after its current step.` (`after this round` for a loop of rounds). `"conversation": false`, or left out, adds nothing. A job whose conversation isn't kept (none, or deleted) just hears the words; the apps show the same answer on its page. `"attachments"`, paths in obid's own attachments folder as `POST /v1/plans` takes them (`422` naming a file outside it), go with the follow-up the words start, into its `.obi/inputs`; words that start none (a job at work, Try again, a follow-up already working, or a job past its safety limit, whose Confirm takes words only) are `422` with them: `Files go with a follow-up of a finished task or a new task: tell this one in words.`

Returns the job that hears the words: `200` with the job itself (or its follow-up already working, obid only), or `201` with the follow-up it started (obid only). `404` for an unknown job. `422` when the text isn't 1 to 2,000 characters, or the job can't take it: wrapping up and the words aren't the owner's own (obid; the frozen Swift runner also refuses a job still getting ready or wrapping up its only turn), a review another Obi asked for, a follow-up already working (above), or, for Try again, never started (no `session`, and never moved) or a follow-up that carries its branch on (trying it again would move that branch under it).

### `GET /v1/events`

Server-Sent Events, so the app stays live while it's open:

```
event: job
data: { …Job }

event: accounts
data: [ …Account ]

event: clear
data: {"clearedAt": "2026-10-10T08:12:00Z"}

event: cleanup
data: {"path": "/srv/nvme/oby/jobs", "bytes": 8912896, "lastRun": null}

event: inbox
data: { …InboxItem }

event: check
data: { …Mac check }

event: turn
data: { …Turn }

event: conversation
data: { …Conversation }

event: step
data: { …Step }

event: thinking
data: { …Thinking }

event: reply
data: { …Reply }

event: stalled
data: { …Job }

event: recap
data: {"text": "Two jobs are done. Pick a base branch needs you.", "since": "2026-10-17T08:10:00Z", "at": "2026-10-17T09:02:00Z"}
```

- A `job` event comes with every change: a new step, a status, the summary, `commits`, `published`, `publishError`, `pipeline`, `pullRequest`, `pullRequestError`, `dismissed`, `macCheck`, `mergeError`, a move to another account. The app replaces the job with that `id`.
- `stalled` (obid only) comes once when a job stalls (`limit`, above), with the job as its `job` event carries it, `stalled` and the handoff note included: what the conversation supervises (continue, split or restart).
- `transcript` (obid only) comes with each transcript entry that comes or changes: `{"job": "<id>", "entry": {…}}`, and with each line the agent writes: `{"job": "<id>", "n": 9, "append": "…"}` (add it to entry `n`'s `text`), the entry as `GET /v1/jobs/{id}/transcript` has it. Update by `n`: a call comes again under its `n` when it ends. These events have a queue of their own in obid, so a busy job's calls never push another event (a job's end, say) out of a slow app's backlog; an app that falls behind on them fetches the transcript again with `after`.
- `accounts` comes when accounts are added, renamed or removed and a sign-in ends (obid only: accounts), and when sign-ins, usage, reset times, running counts, `fullUntil`, a lifted reserve (`reserveLiftedUntil`) or Tetrix (`tetrix`, `tetrixJobs`, `tetrixUsed`) change.
- `cleanup` (obid only) comes once obid has measured the job worktrees after it started answering, with what `status.cleanup` says from then on (above).
- `clear` comes when Clear done is pressed on any app (`POST /v1/clear`): hide the settled jobs that finished by `clearedAt`.
- `inbox` comes with every inbox item that arrives or changes: answered, done or put off, on any app, or settled by itself. Replace the item with that `id`; one whose `state` is `done` leaves the inbox.
  obid sends the item as it is when the event goes out, one event at a time, so the events for one item never go back to an older state and the last one is the item as `GET /v1/inbox` lists it (the same goes for `exchange` events, obid only: Obi-to-Obi exchanges, below). Two changes close together can come as two events that both carry the newer state: an item answered as soon as it arrives may never be seen `open`.
- `check` comes when a Mac check or an Apple check (below) is queued, taken by a Mac or ends. A Mac that runs checks asks for one (`GET /v1/checks/next`) when one is `queued`; other apps can ignore it, since the job's own event carries `macCheck` and `appleCheck` too.
- `turn` comes with every turn of the conversation that's added, from any app, and again when one changes (its `jobs`): the turn as `GET /v1/conversation` lists it. Replace the turn with that `id`, or add it in `at` order. Older runners don't send it.
- `step`, `thinking` and `reply` (`thinking` and `reply` obid only) follow a turn of the conversation as it happens: `POST /v1/plans`, above. On obid a turn in an agent session also sends `delta`, its words as they're written, and `step` events with `tool` and `detail` (obid only: the conversation harness, below).
- `recap` (obid only) comes when the owner is back after more than 30 minutes away and the recap is written (`POST /v1/plans`, "What happened while you were away"): `text` is one to three sentences, `since` when the owner was last seen, `at` when they came back. It isn't a turn; the next answer starts with it. Older runners don't send it.
- `artifact` (obid only) comes when an artifact is made or gets a new version: `{"id", "version", "title", "kind", "conversation", "job"}`; and `{"id": "<id>", "deleted": true}` when it's deleted ([obid only: artifacts](#obid-only-artifacts)).
- `conversation` (obid only) comes whenever one of obid's conversations starts, gets a turn, is renamed, pinned or deleted: obid only: conversations, below. The Swift runner doesn't send it.
- Settled jobs, failed checks, review errors and studied failures wake Oby's orchestrator. Events arriving within 30 seconds are handled in one turn. Event summaries are written by Oby; the brain must read the related job before acting, and agent/check output remains quoted evidence.
- A `: ping` comment every 15 seconds keeps the connection open. Reconnect after any drop. Fetch what you show (`GET /v1/status`, `GET /v1/jobs`, `GET /v1/inbox`; a runner older than the inbox answers `404` there, which means an empty inbox, and any other failure means try again) only once the events' response has arrived, and apply the events that came meanwhile over it: the runner follows a connection before it answers, so nothing that changes in between is lost.

### Orchestrator policy

The event turn uses the same brain account routing as a user turn, including Codex fallback when Claude accounts have reached their limits. Swift validates its actions against the event and the job it read first.

| Action | Automatic policy |
| --- | --- |
| `retry_job` | Retry the job's offered Try again or One more round action, except an interrupted job with an open usage-limit item, a scheduled reset retry, or a reason naming a usage limit. |
| `message_job` | Send the agent Oby's concrete findings and requested correction. |
| `answer_inbox` | Use only Try again, One more round or Rebase now. Usage-limit items, permissions and product questions stay with the owner. |
| `start_now` | Start a job whose start condition can safely be skipped. |
| `move_job` | Move work to an account with room. |
| `fix_failure` | Start a fix for a studied record marked `obyCouldHandle`, with cause `oby-bug`, `agent-mistake` or `flaky-test`. |
| `ask_review_again` | Retry a review that ended with `reviewError`, using another account when needed. |

The orchestrator never merges, publishes, pushes, changes settings, dismisses committed work, handles credentials, or answers permission and product-decision items. Those events remain in Needs you with Oby's diagnosis. Swift permits at most 3 automatic actions per job per day, stops after a repeated failure signature, and starts no more than 10 event turns an hour; excess events wait for the next hour.

### obid: account-limit decisions (#1563)

This section describes the implementation in builds containing #1563. Installing that build upgrades legacy waits; an already installed older release retains its own automatic behavior until updated.

A limit-blocked job exposes additive `accountLimit` state (`decision`, `state`, `resetAt`); `state` is `pending`, `resume` or `stop`, and the reset is an ISO timestamp or null. This replaces a redundant generic interruption choice while preserving unrelated permissions or questions. Accepted moves clear this object when queued. The pending list and job state are authoritative; clients do not implement account-selection or retry policy.

`GET /v1/limit-decisions` lists pending grouped decisions. Each has `id`, `revision`, the limited `agent` and `account`, `resetAt` (Unix seconds, or null), affected `jobs` (`id` and `title`), and current `options`. A `move` option names its `account`, `agent` and `name`; `resume` authorizes waiting for room; `stop` leaves the work stopped. Options come from obid, and clients refresh on the `limitDecision` event. A changed decision must be read and chosen again. A destination is offered only with a connected login that can run and recent provider evidence for both usage windows; missing or stale usage is not treated as verified room. The choice is generic across supported adapters, but a provider that currently supplies no verifiable room cannot be offered until that capability is available. If the decision's scope/options cannot be saved, the group remains visible with a `problem` and no executable options until storage recovers. A reset's clock alone does not prove room; opted-in resumption waits for a fresh provider report and normal job concurrency limits.

`POST /v1/limit-decisions/{id}/answer` takes `revision`, `choice` (`move`, `resume` or `stop`), `account` for a move, and `approval` (`device`, `action`, `signedAt`, `signature`). On the owner's click, their registered device signs the UTF-8 sentence `oby-limit-decision\n<id>\n<revision>\n<choice>\n<account-or-empty>\n<signedAt>` with its P-256 key (ECDSA-SHA256, DER, base64); `action` is the choice. obid checks the exact signature, freshness and replay, then the current revision and options. A paired phone's signing key must belong to its gateway pairing. A missing or malformed approval is `422`, a bad, stale, mismatched or replayed signature is `403`, and a decision or option that changed is `409`. The API token alone cannot answer. Both endpoints are available to paired devices through the gateway.

The job's additive `accountLimit` is null outside this flow, otherwise `{decision, state, resetAt}` (`state`: pending, move, resume or stop; `resetAt`: ISO date or null). It keeps an accepted wait or stop visible without creating another interruption question for each job. Separate permission and question requests remain visible. A disconnected source or failed move returns affected work to a fresh decision with the failure in its reason. Manual continuation via `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages` requires a registered app's `X-Oby-Words` signature while this marker is present; ordinary bearer requests and legacy unsigned inbox retries cannot bypass the choice. Electron signs that explicit manual action in main, and the phone uses its existing signed-word transport.

The iPhone shows one grouped decision on Home and in the Inbox, with all affected task titles, reset evidence and the offered destinations. A tap refreshes the options and signs the displayed revision with its Secure Enclave key; the approval is never queued offline. Unknown option kinds remain data, so future providers do not require a closed enum change.

Older iPhone keys registered by a Mac's endorsement may not have a pairing recorded. `GET /v1/devices/{id}/pairing`, through an authenticated gateway pairing only, issues `{pairing, nonce}` for an existing unbound key; the nonce lasts two minutes and is replaced by the next challenge. On the owner's choice, the same private key signs `oby-device-pairing\n<registered key id>\n<authenticated pairing id>\n<nonce>\n<signedAt>` (`action: "bind-pairing"`). `POST /v1/devices/{id}/pairing` takes `{pairing, nonce, approval}`. obid verifies possession of that exact registered key, the current gateway pairing, freshness, nonce and replay before persisting the association. It cannot replace a bound key or a pairing's existing key. The loopback API token cannot use this route. A restart discards outstanding challenges, and revoking the pairing forgets its bound key as usual. Normal registration is unchanged; a public key alone never establishes this association.

### `GET /v1/inbox`

The inbox items not yet done, the longest-waiting first, items put off `later` included (say so, or hide them until their time). On obid, what needs the owner comes first (`triage` `needs_you`, and `pending` with it), then `fyi`, then `ignore`, each the longest-waiting first (obid only: sorting the inbox).

```json
[{"id": "ab12cd34-question-0", "kind": "question", …InboxItem}]
```

`GET /v1/inbox?state=done` is the history instead (#215): the items that are done, the latest settled first, at most `limit` (50 unless asked, at most 1,000). Reports with nothing to decide arrive there directly, never in the inbox: the audit's report of drift it fixed by itself (with its `outgoing` repairs), a `failure` whose fix was already under way, and items saved before #215 that offered no answer (done once when the runner starts). Answered, dismissed and settled items are there too, with their `answer`. `state=open` is the default; any other `state` is `422`. Older runners ignore `state` and list the open items.

### `POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer`

Settles an item, on the iPhone or the Mac. The body is exactly one of:

```json
{"answer": "A new status"}
{"done": true}
{"later": "2026-10-10T10:12:00Z"}
```

- `answer`: one of its `options`, or the user's own words (1 to 2,000 characters). It goes to the job:
  - `question`: the answer is the agent's next message (as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages`): a waiting job goes on, an ended one gets a follow-up. A plan's question takes `Approve: <mode>` or `Change it` (Permission modes, below).
  - `permission`: `Allow`, `Always allow` or `Deny`, as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/permission`; any other words are a deny with those words as the message for the agent.
  - `Dismiss` (on `review`, `failed`, `limit` and `merge`): dismisses the job (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/dismiss`).
  - `Try again` (on `merge`): the merge is tried again, or the Mac check is handed to the author again or queued again on the same commit (Mac check, above).
  - `Try again` (on `failed` and `limit`): the job runs again in its session, told to pick up where it left off.
  - `One more round` (on `review`): Oby's last review is quoted in `.oby/inputs/review-4.md` and the agent works on it, as a message does. The runner doesn't review it again.
  - `Fix it` (on `failure`): the fix job starts, as `POST /v1/failures/{id}/fix` does, and the signature's items are done. When the cause is being fixed or is fixed already, nothing starts and the answer is refused (`422`) saying so, like `Fixed by #189 on dev.` `Dismiss` (on `failure`): done, nothing starts. Other words on a `failure` are refused (`422`).
  - `Add the column` (on an `audit` item about a missing column): the column joins the roadmap's Status field, then the audit runs again and moves its issues there. GraphQL's `updateProjectV2Field` keeps an option, and the cards on it, when the option goes back with its `id`, and the runner checks that by reading the ids back: it reads the field's options (`id`, `name`, `color`, `description`), sends every one back with its `id`, in its order, to GraphQL's `updateProjectV2Field` (`singleSelectOptions`), with the new one (gray, no description, no `id`) after the column it would stand in for (`Needs test` after `In review`), else last, and reads the options back: any old option whose `id` or name changed, or that moved, refuses the answer (`422`) and the item still waits. The request passes the secret scan first and is listed in the audit's ledger. When it fails the item waits, and the answer's error says why.
  - `Use <column> instead` (on an `audit` item about a missing column): remembered for that status in `issue-audit.json`, and the audit runs again now, moving its issues to that column; from then on issue sync and the audit put them there. `Ignore this status`: remembered too; from then on their column is left where it is. Neither asks again.
  - `Try again` (on an `audit` report of what failed): the audit runs again now, whatever the day; its report is a new item.
  - `Start` (on `start`): the guessed job is queued as planned, routed as it was when planned, and an Oby turn carrying it joins the conversation; `422` when its account has been signed out since (ask again). `Cancel`: done, nothing starts. Other words on a `start` item are refused (`422`).
  - `Try again` (on `failed` with `job` `runner-update`): the runner tries that commit again now (Runner update, below).
  - Other words: sent to the job as a message. On an `audit` or `runner-update` item, only its `options` are taken; other words are refused (`422`).
- `done`: settled without an answer; the job is left as it is.
- `later`: put off until that time (ISO 8601).

**Confirm takes the owner's tap (obid only, #776).** `Confirm` on an open `confirm` item also carries `approval`, a registered device's signature, as publishing by hand does (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/publish`): `{"answer": "Confirm", "approval": {"device", "action", "signedAt", "signature"}}`. `action` is the item's `title`, word for word, and the device signed `"oby-confirm\n<item id>\n<title>\n<signedAt>"` on the owner's click (P-256 ECDSA with SHA-256, DER, base64), within two minutes, used once, remembered across restarts. The `oby-confirm` prefix and the item's id mean no publish or connection signature passes for a Confirm, and one tap confirms one item. A Confirm without an approval, or with one that doesn't hold, is `403` with `{"error", "action"}` (`action` the title to sign) and the item stays open, unclaimed. `Cancel`, `done` and `later` need no approval; an `approval` with anything else is `422`. Inside obid nothing confirms either: the brain, oby-mcp, a conversation's tools and obid's own callers answer without a device and are refused. A browser has no device key: the web app's Confirm says to confirm on the Mac or iPhone.

Returns the item. `404` for an item the runner doesn't know, `409` when it's done already, `422` for a body that isn't exactly one of these, a `later` that isn't a date, an empty or too-long answer, or a job that can't take it (the same reasons as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages`). Every app following events hears the item (`inbox`) and its job (`job`).

### Failure record

Oby studies every failure and fixes the cause itself (#139, Engine/Failures.swift). Whenever a job fails, stalls, or someone had to step in, the runner writes a record in `oby.sqlite` with the evidence, kept for good, after the job list forgets the job.

```json
{"id": "ab12cd34-limit-1791582173", "trigger": "limit", "date": "2026-10-09T21:42:53Z", "job": "ab12cd34", "repo": "deskree-inc/oby",
 "title": "Mac runner notifications", "what": "Stopped at a limit: the same failure twice in a row", "rounds": 3, "intervention": false,
 "evidence": {"summary": "…", "step": "Stage 3 of 6, Check: swift test · phase check · round 3 of 4", "check": "`swift test` printed (its end): …",
              "reviews": [], "log": ["2026-10-09 21:42:50 UTC notice [jobs] job ab12cd34 stopped at a limit: …"],
              "worktree": "git status --porcelain:\n?? Engine/Left.swift\n\ngit diff --stat HEAD:\n(nothing)", "source": "b11afca…"},
 "postMortem": {"happened": "…", "cause": "environment", "obyCouldHandle": true, "signature": "simulator-hung-before-connecting",
                "fix": {"files": ["Engine/AppleChecks.swift"], "change": "…"}, "by": "claude Sonnet 5.5", "date": "2026-10-09T21:44:10Z"},
 "tries": 1, "acted": true, "studyError": null, "issue": "https://github.com/deskree-inc/oby/issues/140", "issueError": null, "fix": null,
 "fixedBy": null, "fixedAt": null}
```

- `trigger`: what Oby saw. `failed`, `interrupted` (a restart or quit cut it off) and `limit` (case 3.1); `reviewCap` (Oby's review requested changes for the last time it may), `noVerdict` (the review ended with no verdict, failed or was interrupted), `noReviewer` (no account could review it); `checkError` (the Mac couldn't run a merge or Apple check) and `unnamedFailure` (one failed without naming a test); `publishError` and `mergeError` (not a conflict Oby rebased by itself, Mac check above); `stuck` (it stayed in one step past its own limit: work 10 minutes past the round's, a check 10 minutes past the test run's, publishing 20 minutes, getting ready 30; or a Mac check queued 2 hours, or running past its lease); `brainFallback` (the brain didn't answer and rules planned: `job` is null); and the interventions, `handMerge` (a pull request Oby opened was merged, and Oby never merged it) and `outsideCommit` (its head is a commit Oby didn't push), with `intervention` true. A review job's own end reaches its author as `noVerdict`; a job the user stopped isn't a failure. Each is recorded once, and only while it's news (within a day).
- `what`: what Oby saw, in its own words. `rounds`: the rounds the job had used. `evidence`, with the vault's secrets taken out: `summary` (the job's report, or what the user said and why the brain failed), `step` (its stage and step), `check` (the end of what its check printed, and its Mac checks with what they printed, or null), `reviews` (Oby's reviews of it, oldest first), `log` (the runner's log lines of the last hour that name the job, and the last few of all), `worktree` (#221: what the job left in its worktree, `git status --porcelain` and `git diff --stat HEAD`, read at its first post-mortem, so a "nothing committed" failure shows the work left behind; null for the brain or with no worktree) and `source` (the commit of `origin/dev` the last post-mortem read, or null).
- `postMortem`: null until it's studied. A fresh, read-only turn boxed like the Lead's (`claude-sonnet-5-5`, only Read, Grep and Glob, no settings or MCP) reads the evidence as a quoted file and Oby's own source at a freshly fetched `origin/dev` (#221: a detached worktree of the runner's clone of `deskree-inc/oby`, `failures/source` in `OBY_HOME`, reset before each study; never the clone's own working tree, which is on whatever branch a job left it; the evidence names the commit), and answers in a JSON schema: `happened`; `cause`, one of `oby-bug`, `environment`, `agent-mistake`, `account-limit`, `flaky-test`, `feedback-not-converging`, `outside-service`; `obyCouldHandle`; `fix`, the `files` in Oby and the `change`; `signature`, a short kebab-case name for the cause that groups repeats (the turn sees the signatures used before); and `sameAs` (#197), `{"kind": "issue" | "pull-request", "number": N}` or null. Before the turn the runner reads the open `oby-failure` issues (number, signature, one-line cause) and the pull requests merged into `dev` in the last 7 days (with the issues their `Closes #N` lines name), and quotes them in a second file, `.oby/evidence/known.md`; `sameAs` is the one with the same root cause, and Swift drops one it wasn't offered. With an issue, the record's signature is kept as an **alias** of that issue's (in `oby.sqlite`), so later records with either name count, file and offer as one; with a merged pull request, it's an alias of the signature whose issue that pull request closes, when Oby knows it. It runs on the least busy Claude account with room, one record at a time, never holding a job up; with no account, it waits for the next minute's sweep. `tries` counts the turns (3 at most), and `studyError` says why the last one didn't answer.
- **Acting on it** (`acted`): the job's report (`summary`) gets a `What went wrong: <what happened> (cause: <cause>)` paragraph, before the review's follow-ups. When a fix merged into `dev` in the last 7 days already handles the cause (`sameAs` a merged pull request, a fix job of the signature's issue merged, or a merged pull request that closes it), the record is against that fix, `fixedBy` its pull request's URL and `fixedAt` when it merged, and nothing is filed or offered. The 7 days run from the merge, never from the records in between: a cause that still comes back after them is filed again. Otherwise an `oby-bug`, a signature seen twice in 7 days (an `environment` cause 3 times: most were a missing Mac or an old rate limit), or a record `sameAs` an open issue, becomes or updates one open GitHub issue labeled `oby-failure` in `deskree-inc/oby` (created as the GitHub app, after the secret scan of its title and text, with no assignee and no mention; a repeat, or a `sameAs`, brings that issue's body up to date instead of filing a new one, with how many times it was seen and the alias, never a comment, which would email the owner (#198); an issue Oby didn't file keeps its own body), with the post-mortem, the record and the evidence fenced; `issue` is its URL, or `issueError` why it couldn't. It also brings a `failure` inbox item offering `Fix it`, one open per signature; an `environment` cause seen twice brings only the item. When a fix of its cause is already underway (a fix job queued or running, or its pull request open, on the record's issue, its signature's or an alias's: what `Fix it` would refuse), it reaches no inbox (#215): the issue's body counts it and carries its evidence as any repeat, its item arrives `done` saying `Already being fixed by …` with no options, and the signature's open item is done by itself. A `failure` item that's open when a fix job on its cause starts or opens its pull request is done then too (Fix it would be refused), and the runner checks the open ones once after it restarts.
- **Closing** (#197): GitHub honours `Closes #N` only on merges into the default branch (`main`), and Oby merges into `dev`. So when a pull request of Oby's in `deskree-inc/oby` is merged into `dev` (Oby's merge, or one the runner sees merged), within a minute's sweep the runner closes every issue it closes (its job's `issue` and its description's `Closes`/`Fixes`/`Resolves #N`) that's open and labeled `oby-failure`: its body ends with `Fixed by #<pr>, merged into dev as <merge commit>. …` (after the secret scan), then it's closed as completed, never with a comment (#198). Feature issues keep their own lifecycle (#116). The closed issue's records get `fixedBy`, and their open `failure` inbox items are done. When GitHub fails, it's tried again 5 minutes later.
- `fixedBy`: the fix pull request merged into `dev` the record is against, or null; `fixedAt`: when it merged, or null.
- `fix`: the fix job Fix it started, or null. It's a `fix` job in `deskree-inc/oby` (on the account with the most room; the issue's when it has one, so its branch is `oby/fix-<issue>`), checked with `swift test` over up to 4 rounds, told only Swift's words; the post-mortem and the evidence are quoted in `.oby/inputs/failure-<record>.md`, as data.

### `GET /v1/failures?limit=50`

The failure records, newest first, each with its post-mortem (null until studied). `limit` goes up to 1,000.

### `GET /v1/failures/{id}`

One record. `404` for one the runner doesn't know.

### `POST /v1/failures/{id}/fix`

Fix it: starts the fix job (Failure record, above) and answers it, `201`, as `POST /v1/jobs` does; the signature's `failure` inbox items are done. `404` for a record the runner doesn't know; `409` when it isn't studied yet (`"Oby hasn't studied this failure yet."`), no account has room, or the cause is being fixed or is fixed already (#197): on the record's issue, its signature's or an alias's, a job is running (`"Already being fixed by job <id>."`), a pull request of Oby's is open (`"Already being fixed by #<pr>, its pull request open."`), or a fix merged into `dev` in the last 7 days (`"Fixed by #<pr> on dev."`). Then nothing starts.

### `GET /v1/jobs/{id}/publish`

What publishing this job by hand would do, in one sentence: for a job whose own publish failed (`publishError`). A task's page shows exactly this under Publish before the owner's click publishes it. An alert's Publish (`ready`, on the Mac and the iPhone) is that click already: it opens the task's page, which shows the sentence as it publishes. Since every job publishes by itself (#1386), this is only for a job whose own publish failed.

```json
{"action": "Push oby/fix-ab12cd to deskree-inc/oby and open a pull request into dev.", "base": "dev"}
```

Once the job is published and has new commits, the sentence is about them: `"Push the new commits on oby/fix-ab12cd to deskree-inc/oby, for its pull request into dev."` A rebased branch's rewritten commits are new commits too, until they're pushed.

`409` if there's nothing to publish (no commits, not done, or published with every commit: the branch on GitHub is at the worktree's head). On obid, also when a remote with no pull requests has every commit: `Nothing to publish: the remote is already at its head.` (#627). One whose pull request failed after the push is still offered: publishing it again opens the pull request.

How a push goes out, by itself or by hand: the runner reads the branch's head on GitHub. No branch yet, the same commit, or one the new head builds on: a plain push. Anything else is a rebase, pushed with `--force-with-lease=refs/heads/<branch>:<the commit there>`, and only when that's the commit the runner itself pushed last (`outgoing`) and the branch is one of its own `oby/` branches. When someone else pushed to it, nothing goes out: `publishError` is `Not published: oby/fix-75 on GitHub is at <commit>, not the commit Oby pushed, so the rebase would overwrite someone else's work.` (or `… moved on GitHub while Oby pushed the rebase …` when the lease didn't hold). The secret scan, the pinned commit and the stop checks are the same either way.

### `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/publish`

Publishes by hand, for a job whose own publish failed, after the user approved on a registered device: the owner's click in the iPhone app or the Mac app (`apps/mac/app/MacDevice.swift`). Each has its own key (`apps/shared/DeviceKey.swift`); either one opens the same door.

```json
{"device": "d7c1…", "action": "Push oby/fix-ab12cd to deskree-inc/oby and open a pull request into dev.",
 "signedAt": "2026-10-09T22:01:07Z", "signature": "MEUCIQ…"}
```

- `signature`: ECDSA P-256 with SHA-256 (DER, base64) over the UTF-8 bytes of `"oby-publish\n<job id>\n<action>\n<signedAt>"`, made with the device's Secure Enclave key. The owner's click is the approval: the key has no presence check (`.privateKeyUsage` only), so signing asks for no Touch ID, Face ID or password (the owner's decision of 2026-10-24). The key never leaves the device's Secure Enclave, and its wrapped blob is kept where only Oby reads it without asking: the iPhone app's own keychain, and on the Mac the login keychain, whose access list makes macOS ask the owner before another app reads it (a boxed agent can't reach the Keychain at all). So an approval comes only from the owner's own app, never from a job, another Oby or the API token alone. A program running as the owner that the owner lets read the Mac's item could sign; the data-protection keychain, which binds it to Oby alone, waits for Developer ID's provisioning profile. Until 2026-10-24 the keys asked for Touch ID, Face ID or the Mac's password; each device makes its new key once (Keychain account `approval-key`, the old ones deleted) and registers it: the iPhone as its paired phone's key on its next connection, the Mac on the owner's first click (`POST /v1/devices`).
- The runner checks:
  - the device is registered;
  - the signature;
  - `action` matches its own sentence exactly;
  - `signedAt` is within 2 minutes;
  - this signature hasn't been used before.
- A Confirm in the inbox is the same proof with its own prefix, `oby-confirm\n<item id>\n<title>\n<signedAt>` (obid only, `POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer`, #776): a publish signature never passes for a Confirm, nor the other way.
- Then it runs Publish, as a job's own publish does: guards, the secret scan, push one pinned commit, then the pull request, ready for review (the one already open for the branch, if there is one). It returns `{"url": "https://github.com/…/pull/42"}`, or an error line (for example the scan's findings, never the secret).

### `POST /v1/devices`

Registers a device's publishing key, once per device: the iPhone's (named "iPhone") or a Mac's (named after the Mac, `Host.current().localizedName`):

```json
{"name": "Wellington's MacBook Air", "publicKey": "BJ3x…"}
```

`publicKey` is the P-256 public key, X9.63 (65 bytes, uncompressed), base64. It returns `{"id": "d7c1…"}`. Only the runner's owner can register (the tailnet header, or the API token); the runner lists devices in its log.

- A key the runner knows returns its id right away, so the apps register every time they connect. That adds nothing.
- A new key goes in only with the owner's presence, in one of three ways (otherwise `403` with one line to show):
  - **Vouched for** by a device already registered: `"endorsement": {"device": "<its id>", "signedAt": "…", "signature": "…"}`, ECDSA P-256 with SHA-256 (DER, base64) over `"oby-device\n<publicKey>\n<signedAt>"`, within 2 minutes, once (kept used in `devices.json`, as approvals are). The apps sign only on their owner's tap or click.
  - **A paired phone's own key, from an invitation the owner made**: a request through the gateway (or the Obysphere relay) from a phone paired with an invitation the owner made on their own device (`POST /v1/pairing/invite` with its `approval`, below) registers one key, bound to that pairing, within 10 minutes of redeeming it. A pairing from an invitation the API token made alone, or one older than that, adds no key (`403`: `To add this iPhone's publishing key, pair it again from Oby on your Mac, then open Oby on the iPhone within 10 minutes.`), so the API token alone (an MCP client, a leaked token) can't invite, redeem and register a key that approves. A second new key from the same pairing is refused (`This iPhone registered its publishing key already. To register another, pair it again from your Mac.`), and revoking the pairing (`DELETE /v1/pairing/devices/{id}`) forgets its key.
  - **Named in `home/pairing`**: the file holds exactly this `publicKey` (whitespace around it is ignored) and was written in the last 2 minutes. The registration that it names uses it up at once; one older than 2 minutes is removed when it's looked at, and a file naming another key admits nothing. A request through the gateway never uses it. The Mac app writes it for the runner on the same Mac only on its owner's click (the first Publish, Confirm or Disconnect that runner doesn't know this Mac's key for, or Settings' Try again), and removes it right after, whatever happened; it never writes it when it merely connects. For a runner on another machine the `403` says the command for its owner to run there: ``To add this device, run `echo '<publicKey>' > <home>/pairing` on obid's machine, then try again within 2 minutes.`` An empty file (`touch`), as pairing was opened until 2026-10-24, admits nothing.
- A Mac whose new key went in this way revokes the ones it replaced (`DELETE /v1/devices/{id}`, signed by the new key): its Touch ID-only key (until 2026-10-23) and its Touch ID or password key (until 2026-10-24).
- Why: agents run on the runner's machine, so through its tailnet address they arrive with the owner's header, and a token can leak; with the API token alone, or a paired phone's token, nothing can add a key that signs a publish, a Confirm or a connection change. Agents can't write `pairing`, because `OBY_HOME` is hidden inside their sandbox. A program that can write obid's home can rewrite `devices.json` too: that is outside what this guards.

### `DELETE /v1/devices/{id}`

Forgets a device's publishing key, with a registered key's signature for it (its own, or another device's):

```json
{"approval": {"device": "d7c1…", "signedAt": "2026-10-24T12:00:00Z", "signature": "MEUC…"}}
```

- `signature`: ECDSA P-256 with SHA-256 (DER, base64) over `"oby-revoke-device\n<id>\n<signedAt>"`, within 2 minutes, once.
- It returns `{"revoked": "<id>"}`; `404` for a device it doesn't know, `403` for an approval that isn't a registered key's for this device. Not on the gateway.

### `POST /v1/activities`

Hands the runner a Live Activity push token, so it can keep the phone's Lock Screen and Dynamic Island current while the app is suspended:

```json
{"token": "80a1f2…", "kind": "update", "topic": "com.deskree.oby", "environment": "development"}
```

- `token`: the push token in hex. `kind`: `"update"` for one activity's token, `"start"` for the app's push-to-start token (a newer one replaces the older for the same app and environment). `topic`: the app's bundle ID. `environment`: `"development"` (a build from Xcode) or `"production"` (TestFlight and the App Store).
- It returns `{"push": true}` when the runner pushes, `{"push": false}` when it has no APNs key (the token isn't kept). `422` for a token, kind, topic or environment that isn't one of those.

Two seconds after a job changes, the runner pushes what its jobs look like then (one Live Activity, as the app shows it):

- **A job is active** and the runner has update tokens: an `update` with the newest active job (priority 5, skipped when nothing the card shows changed).
- **A job is active**, no update tokens, and it hasn't started one yet: a push-to-start (`start`, priority 10, with an alert) to the start tokens. The phone then hands over the new activity's update token.
- **Nothing is active:** an `end` with how the last job finished and a dismissal 15 minutes after it finished, with no alert (a job that needs you says so with its own notification, below); then the update tokens are dropped.

Apple's answer `410` or `BadDeviceToken` drops a token. The runner keeps tokens in `apns/tokens.json`.

### `POST /v1/notifications`

Hands the runner the app's device token, so it can tell the phone when a job needs you:

```json
{"token": "5b0e9c…", "topic": "com.deskree.oby", "environment": "production"}
```

- `token`: the device token from `registerForRemoteNotifications`, in hex. `topic` and `environment` as for `/v1/activities`. The app sends it on every launch once notifications are allowed (it asks the first time something said on the phone starts a job).
- It returns `{"push": true}` or `{"push": false}`, and `422`, as `/v1/activities` does.

Two seconds after a job changes, the runner sends an alert push (`apns-push-type: alert`, the app's bundle ID as topic, priority 10) for each job whose need changed. Nothing else notifies: steps, rounds, and jobs that end needing nothing (stopped, or done without commits) only move the Live Activity.

| The job | Title | Body | `category` |
|---|---|---|---|
| Done with commits it couldn't publish by itself | `Couldn't publish: <title>` | `publishError` (else the summary's first line) | `ready` |
| `failed` | `Failed: <title>` | the reason (the summary's first line) | `job` |
| `interrupted` | `Interrupted: <title>` | the reason | `job` |
| Oby's review stopped asking for changes | `Changes requested: <title>` | `Oby's review found a blocker it asked to fix before. Take it from here.` | `job` |
| Its agent asked a question (`question`), which waits in the inbox | `Question: <title>` | the question | `job` |
| Its agent waits for the user's OK (`permission`), which waits in the inbox | `<permission title>?`, like `Run swift test?` | `<title>: <detail>` | `permission` |

```json
{"aps": {"alert": {"title": "Couldn't publish: Review oby #33", "body": "Couldn't push oby/review-33: the remote hung up."},
         "category": "ready", "sound": "default", "thread-id": "jobs"}, "job": "9f08fd10"}
```

- `job` is the job's `id`: tapping the notification opens it. The `ready` category offers **Publish** (it unlocks the phone and brings the app forward on the job, where it publishes) and **Open**; `job` offers **Open**; `permission` is for **Allow**, **Always allow** and **Deny** (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/permission` with the job's `permission.id`) and **Open**. On obid, the agent's question (#858) alerts as `job`: its options are its own, so it's answered in the app.
- `apns-collapse-id` is the job's `id`, so a newer state replaces the older notification.
- When a job stops needing you (published, from the iPhone or the Mac, dismissed, or running again after a message), the runner sends a background push (`apns-push-type: background`, priority 5), `{"aps": {"content-available": 1}, "clear": "<job id>"}`, and the app removes that job's notification. The app also removes them whenever it opens and sees the job needs nothing.
- The runner remembers what it last said for each job in `apns/notices.json`, so a restart doesn't say it again, and jobs a restart interrupted notify once it's back.

**On obid** (#452, partly ported: Firebase Cloud Messaging for the Android app, and the iPhone through Deskree's sealed relay, below; no APNs key of its own). The above is the Swift runner's.

```json
{"token": "<FCM registration token>", "platform": "fcm", "topic": "com.deskree.oby"}
```

- `platform`: `"fcm"` for an Android phone's FCM registration token; absent or `"apns"` is the iPhone's bare device token as above, which `obid` answers `{"push": false}` and doesn't keep (it has no APNs key: the iPhone registers through Deskree's relay instead, below). `token`: 1 to 4,095 printable ASCII characters, no spaces. `topic`: `"com.deskree.oby"` or left out, for FCM; an APNs registration's `topic` and `environment` aren't looked at.
- It returns `{"push": true}` when the token is kept and `obid` can send (a Firebase service account is in the vault, below), `{"push": false}` when it can't (nothing kept), `422` for a token, platform or topic that isn't one of those, and `503` when the vault can't be read, as every answer.
- Tokens are kept in `home/push/fcm.json` (0600, written to a new file renamed into place) as `[{"token", "device"}]`, at most 20, the oldest out; a token handed over again becomes the newest. `device` is the paired device whose gateway request handed it over (the gateway passes it to the API inside obid, where no socket can claim it), or null when it came on the loopback API with the API token. A device keeps one token: its new one replaces the old. Revoking the device (`DELETE /v1/pairing/devices/{id}`) drops its tokens, and a device whose pairing ran out is sent nothing, so a lost phone gets no more alerts. FCM answering `404` `UNREGISTERED`, or `400` `INVALID_ARGUMENT` about the registration token, drops it.
- **When.** As the Swift runner: two seconds after a job changes, each job whose need changed. obid's jobs never ask a question (`question` is null). A `done` job whose Oby review needs the owner (`reviewNeedsYou`, #525) alerts once per pushed head, urgent: `Couldn't review: <title>` (body its `reviewError`) when no reviewer could review it, else `Changes requested: <title>` (`Oby's review still asks for changes. Take it from here.`), both `job`. Its other alerts are `Couldn't publish: <title>` (`ready`, body `publishError`, else the summary's first line), `Failed: <title>` and `Interrupted: <title>` (`job`, body the job's `reason`, else the summary's first line), and `<permission title>?` (`permission`, body `<title>: <detail>`, 200 characters at most). What was last said for each job is kept in `home/push/notices.json`, so a restart doesn't say it again and jobs a restart interrupted notify once it's back; the first time, what the jobs already need isn't news.
- **What's sent.** `POST https://fcm.googleapis.com/v1/projects/{project_id}/messages:send`, one data-only message per token (no `notification`, so the app draws it), every value a string, `android.collapse_key` the job's `id`, `android.ttl` `"86400s"`:

```json
{"message": {"token": "…", "data": {"job": "9f08fd10", "title": "Couldn't publish: Review oby #33", "body": "Couldn't push oby/review-33: the remote hung up.", "category": "ready"},
             "android": {"priority": "HIGH", "collapse_key": "9f08fd10", "ttl": "86400s"}}}
```

- When a job stops needing you, `data` is `{"clear": "<job id>"}` with `android.priority` `"NORMAL"`, and the app removes that job's notification.
- An alert's title and body pass the secret scan first (`crate::scan`, gitleaks too): a finding, or a scan that can't run, sends nothing for that job, and the log says so with the job's id only. Sending never holds up the API; a failure is logged with FCM's status and error code, never the token or the access token.
- **The service account.** The owner's vault secret `oby.fcm.serviceAccount` ([obid only: the vault](#obid-only-the-vault)): the Google service account's JSON (`project_id`, `client_email`, `private_key` as PEM PKCS#8 RSA, `token_uri`, which must be `https://`). It lives where the vault lives, never reaches an agent, an answer, a log line or a push, and its private key and every access token it gets are taken out as `obid`'s own secrets too. `obid` signs a JWT with it (RS256; `iss` the `client_email`, `scope` `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/firebase.messaging`, `aud` the `token_uri`, an hour), trades it at `token_uri` for an access token (`grant_type=urn:ietf:params:oauth:grant-type:jwt-bearer`) and uses that until 5 minutes before it ends. It's read again for each registration and each burst of alerts, so adding, replacing or removing it needs no restart.
- **What the owner does.** In the Firebase console, open the project that has the Android app (`com.deskree.oby`) › Project settings › Service accounts › Generate new private key, which downloads a JSON file. On the machine `obid` runs on, as its user: `obid vault --config <path to config.json> add oby.fcm.serviceAccount < ~/Downloads/<the file>.json`, then delete the downloaded file. The phone's next `POST /v1/notifications` answers `{"push": true}`.

**The iPhone, through Deskree's relay.** Apple's push key is Deskree's, so `obid` never talks to APNs: the iPhone registers with Deskree's relay (the control plane, which holds the key), then hands `obid` its push id, a key to sign pushes with (derived from the `pushSecret` the relay gave it, which `obid` never gets), and a key of its own that `obid` seals every alert to. The relay and Apple only ever carry a sealed blob; the iPhone's Notification Service Extension opens it. Nothing to set up on `obid`.

```json
{"apns": {"pushId": "…", "pushKey": "<base64url, 32 bytes>", "sealKey": "<base64url X25519 public key, 32 bytes>", "relay": "https://oby-control.deskree.com"}}
```

- `pushId`: the relay's id for this iPhone, 1 to 512 printable ASCII characters, no spaces. `pushKey`: what `obid` signs its pushes with, 32 bytes in base64url (padding optional); the phone derives it from its `pushSecret` and never hands `obid` the secret itself. `sealKey`: the iPhone's X25519 public key (CryptoKit's `rawRepresentation`), base64url, padding optional; `obid` seals a test alert to it, and one it can't seal to (not 32 bytes, a low-order point) is refused. `relay`: the control plane `obid` pushes through, its `pushRelay` (Deskree's, `https://oby-control.deskree.com`, unless config.json says another), written as an origin in any case (`https://Oby-Control.deskree.com/` is the same); any other is refused, so a device token can't point `obid`'s pushes anywhere else.
- It returns `{"push": true}` once kept, and `422` for a body that isn't one of these.
- Kept in `home/push/relay.json` (0600, written to a new file renamed into place) as `[{"pushId", "pushKey", "sealKey", "relay", "device"}]`, by the FCM tokens' rules: at most 20, the oldest out, one per paired device (its new one replaces it; a push id handed over again by another device replaces nothing), dropped when the device is revoked, and a device whose pairing ran out is sent nothing. One made with another control plane (`pushRelay` changed since) is sent nothing. The `pushKey` is `obid`'s own secret from then on (and again each time it starts), as written and as its raw bytes: taken out of every answer, log line and push. The relay answering `410` drops the registration.
- **What notifies** (the owner's rule of 2026-10-28, `push.rs`'s `notifies`: only what waits on the owner to decide or act; the phone shows nothing while Oby is in front). Two seconds after a job, the inbox or a conversation changes:

| What | `category` | Urgent | Title | Body |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A job's agent waits for the owner's OK (`permission`) | `permission`; `permissionRule` when it offers Always allow (`always`); `plan` for a plan to approve | yes | `<permission title>?` | `<title>: <detail>` |
| A conversation's shell command waits for Allow (an inbox `permission` item with no job) | `permission`; `permissionRule` when its `options` hold `Always allow` | yes | the item's `title` | the first line of its `text` |
| Oby planned something only the owner's Confirm carries out (a `confirm` item) | `confirm` | yes | the item's `title` | the first line of its `text` |
| Work waits for the owner's Start (a `start` item; one turn's offers, `offer`, are one alert) | `start` | yes | the item's `title`, or `Oby offers <n> jobs` for an offer of several | the first line of its (first item's) `text` |
| An inbox item sorted `"triage": "needs_you"` | `inbox` | yes | the item's `title` | the first line of its `text` |
| A job the owner started ends done with commits it couldn't publish by itself | `ready` | no | `Couldn't publish: <title>` | `publishError`, else the summary's first line |
| … `failed` or `interrupted` | `job` | no | `Failed: <title>`, `Interrupted: <title>` | the `reason`, else the summary's first line |

- Nothing else notifies: a job that ended done with nothing to decide (`Done: <title>`, FYI, built but gated out since 2026-10-28, as is Oby's message in a conversation, `message`: its page and its conversation say them), steps, rounds and other progress, a stopped or dismissed job, a job another Obi asked for, an inbox item sorted `fyi` or `ignore`, not sorted yet, or still being sorted (`pending`, until it becomes `needs_you`), a job's own `permission` item (its job's alert covers it), and the turns that tell a conversation a job ended (`ended`) or a routine ran (`routine`). Before the gate, Oby's message was pushed only while no paired device followed `GET /v1/events` through the gateway (Oby open on it), and not within two minutes of the owner's own words in that conversation; obid still keeps those rules, so letting FYI through again is one line in `notifies`.
- **One alert per thing.** Its `collapse` is the job's, the inbox item's or the conversation's id (`h-` and 32 hex digits, a digest, for one longer than 64 bytes or not printable ASCII, APNs' collapse id), and a newer alert for the same thing replaces it: a job's permission alert by its end. When an urgent alert's thing settles (answered, or gone), a short replacement with the same `collapse` goes: title `Settled`, body the old title, `category` `settled`, the same `thread`, `job`, `conversation` and `item`. A result isn't taken back. What was said for each thing is kept in `home/push/said.json`, so a restart doesn't say it again; the first time, what's already there isn't news.
- **The alert**, sealed as JSON: `{"id", "at", "title", "body", "thread", "category", "job"?, "conversation"?, "item"?}`, the absent ones left out. `id` is this send's own (32 hex digits, new for every send, a `Settled` too): the phone shows an `id` once. `at` is when it was sealed, in seconds since the epoch: the phone drops one more than 24 hours old. The thing's id isn't in it; it's the relay's `collapse`, outside. `thread` is the conversation's id when there is one, else `jobs` or `inbox`. `job`, `conversation` and `item` say what a tap opens; `item` is the inbox item's id, which the alert's buttons answer (`POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer` with `Allow`, `Always allow` or `Deny`; `Approve` or `Keep planning` for `plan`), on a job's permission `<job>-permission-<permission id>`. The title is at most 100 characters and the body 200, each on one line. Both pass the secret scan first: a finding, or a scan that can't run, sends nothing, and the log names only the alert's `id`.

```json
{"id": "5f0e8c1a3b2d4e6f8a9b0c1d2e3f4a5b", "at": 1800000000, "title": "Run swift test?", "body": "Android push: swift test --filter Push", "thread": "jobs", "category": "permission", "job": "9f08fd10", "item": "9f08fd10-permission-p1"}
```

- **Sealing.** HPKE (RFC 9180) base mode, DHKEM(X25519, HKDF-SHA256), HKDF-SHA256 and ChaCha20-Poly1305 (CryptoKit's `.Curve25519_SHA256_ChachaPoly`), `info` the UTF-8 of `oby push v1`, no associated data, to the `sealKey`, with a fresh encapsulation each time. `sealed` is base64url, unpadded, of the encapsulated key (32 bytes) then the ciphertext (the alert and its 16-byte tag). The iPhone opens it with `HPKE.Recipient(privateKey:ciphersuite:info:encapsulatedKey:)` (the first 32 bytes) and `open(_:)` (the rest). `core/crates/obid/src/push.rs`, `cryptokit_opens_what_obid_seals`, holds a vector CryptoKit opened on macOS 26 (an alert with its `id` and `at`), and one CryptoKit sealed that `obid` opens.
- **The send.** `POST <pushRelay>/v1/push`, `Content-Type: application/json`, one per registration, with `Oby-Push-Mac`: base64url, unpadded, of HMAC-SHA256 keyed by the `pushKey`'s raw 32 bytes (decoded, never its base64url text), over the body exactly as sent. `collapse` is the thing's id, `urgent` whether it needs the owner, `at` the time now in seconds since the epoch (the relay refuses one more than 5 minutes off):

```json
{"at":1800000000,"collapse":"9f08fd10","pushId":"…","sealed":"…","urgent":true}
```

- An alert whose `sealed` would be longer than 3,072 characters (the relay's limit, so Apple's payload stays under 4 KB) isn't sent; titles and bodies are cut and lose their control characters so none is.
- Every alert goes to every phone at once, each push waiting at most 10 seconds for the relay, so a slow relay holds up none of the others. A `2xx` is taken. A `429`, a `5xx` or no answer is tried once more, the same send (its `id` too), after the relay's `Retry-After` (at most 10 seconds). `410` drops the registration, and so does the third `401` in a row. Anything else is logged with the relay's status only (its body isn't read; never the push id, the key or the blob). An urgent alert, or its `Settled`, that no phone took is said again at the next look; a result or a message isn't. Sending never holds up the API.
- **What leaves the Mac, and to whom.** To the control plane at `pushRelay` (Deskree's by default): the push id, the sealed blob, the `collapse` (a job's, an inbox item's or a conversation's id, or its digest), whether it's urgent, the time, and the MAC. Apple gets what the relay passes on. Neither sees a title, a body, the push key or anything else `obid` holds; the relay sees when and how often alerts go, and which are urgent.

### `GET /v1/settings/permissions` and `PUT /v1/settings/permissions`

Settings › Permissions: `{"mode": "ask"}`, the mode new jobs get (`bypass` until changed, on obid too since #1570; an obid install that saved `sandbox`, #586's default, keeps it; a saved mode obid can't read, or a `permissions.json` that exists but can't be read or parsed, is `ask`), kept in the runner's `oby.sqlite` (obid: `home/permissions.json`). `PUT` answers with the setting; `422` for a mode that isn't one. Jobs already dispatched keep theirs. On obid it applies to a job a conversation started after it read something untrusted too (`untrusted`; the owner's decision of 2026-10-27). The Electron app's Settings › General › "Permissions on this Mac" shows and sets it, with the saved rules below it: it offers Sangam's four modes, and `sandbox` only while it's the one saved.

### `GET /v1/permissions/rules` and `DELETE /v1/permissions/rules/{id}`

The saved rules, oldest first:

```json
[{"id": "r1a2b3c4d", "rule": "Bash(swift test:*)", "repo": "deskree-inc/oby", "decision": "allow", "created": "2026-10-11T09:12:00Z"}]
```

`decision` is `allow` or `deny`; deny beats allow. "Always" on a permission saves one. `DELETE` forgets one and answers with the rules left; `404` for a rule the runner doesn't know.

### `GET /v1/settings/models` and `PUT /v1/settings/models`

Each tool's model, routine effort and complex-job effort, as in `status.models`; Cursor has a model and no effort: `"cursor": {"model": "auto"}` (`auto` lets Cursor pick). `PUT` takes `cursor` optionally: older clients leave it out and the value is kept; it must be a model id (`422` for anything else, such as an option). `GET /v1/settings/models/options` lists the choices: Claude Code's models and efforts, the models the signed-in Codex account offers (from its own `model/list`) with the efforts each takes, and Cursor's models from `cursor-agent models` on the first signed-in Cursor account (`{"models": [{"id", "name"}], "efforts": []}`, no models while none is signed in). Defaults are Claude Code `xhigh` and Codex `high` for `complexEffort`; older clients that omit it keep the current value.

The chat's set_setting action uses those same choices. It changes one tool's model, routine effort or `complexEffort` (Cursor's model only: `effort` and `complex_effort` must be null for it), maxRunning (1 through 20), or an account's weekly reserve (lift true or false). For example, "complex jobs on Codex at xhigh" sets Codex's `complexEffort`. Swift checks every value against the briefing before the runner applies it; a rejected model or effort includes the available choices. Each action reply names the old and new value. A request that changes two tools is planned as two actions so the brain receives a fresh briefing between them.

```json
{"claude": {"models": [{"id": "claude-opus-5-5", "name": "Opus 5.5"}], "efforts": ["low", "medium", "high", "xhigh", "max"], "complexEfforts": ["low", "medium", "high", "xhigh", "max"]},
 "codex": {"models": [{"id": "gpt-6-sol", "name": "GPT-6-Sol", "efforts": ["low", "medium", "high", "xhigh", "max", "ultra"]}], "complexEfforts": ["low", "medium", "high", "xhigh", "max", "ultra"]},
 "cursor": {"models": [{"id": "auto", "name": "Auto"}, {"id": "gpt-5", "name": "GPT-5"}], "efforts": [], "complexEfforts": []}}
```

### `PUT /v1/settings/default-agent` (obid)

Sets the default for new conversation tasks: `{"defaultAgent":"auto"}`, `"claude"`, `"codex"` or `"cursor"`. The default is `"auto"` (the planner's automatic choice). The runner persists this in `home/settings.json`, exposes it as `defaultAgent` in `GET /v1/status`, and answers `200` with `{"defaultAgent":"codex"}` after a successful update. Missing, unknown or non-string values are `422`; malformed JSON is `400`. It uses the same owner authentication as the other settings routes. Older runners answer `404` and send no `defaultAgent`.

When the owner's words name an agent (Claude, Codex or Cursor), or the plan names an account, the planner's selection takes precedence. Otherwise the default is used if it has a signed-in, usable account with room under the existing usage limits; a disconnected default, no usable account or a full default falls back to the planner's current automatic choice. `auto` always keeps that choice. Existing jobs and follow-ups retain their agent.

### `GET /v1/settings/jobs` and `PUT /v1/settings/jobs`

How many jobs run at once, as in `status.maxRunning`: `{"maxRunning": 6}`, 1 to 20 (anything else is `422`). The runner keeps it across restarts. Raising it starts waiting jobs at once; lowering it stops nothing, and new jobs wait until fewer than the new cap run.

obid also answers `accountFailover` (`{"maxRunning": 6, "accountFailover": true}`), kept across restarts and set with `PUT` alongside or instead of `maxRunning`. It controls conversation-turn failover; jobs at an account limit follow the owner decision in “obid: account-limit decisions” (#1563). A body with neither setting, or a value of the wrong kind, is `422`.

### `POST /v1/speech?device=<UUID>&utterance=<UUID>&final=false&language=auto`

Transcribes one utterance on the runner's CPU using whisper.cpp, independently of the iPhone and of any agent. It uses the same owner gate as the other `/v1` endpoints. This is the speech service only; a Watch gateway can call it later.

The body is binary audio: signed 16-bit little-endian PCM, 16 kHz, mono (`Content-Type: audio/pcm` or `application/octet-stream`), or a complete RIFF/WAV chunk containing that same format (`audio/wav` or `audio/x-wav`). Send sequential chunks of at most ten seconds; each WAV request has its own header. The HTTP body limit is 324,096 bytes including WAV metadata. Audio for an utterance may total at most 60 seconds (1,920,000 PCM bytes).

- `device`: stable UUID chosen by the app for this device. This is a concurrency key, not authentication.
- `utterance`: a fresh UUID for each utterance. Reuse it for every chunk of that utterance.
- `final`: `false` by default. Every non-final call appends the chunk and transcribes **all** audio so far with multilingual `base-q5_1`. A `true` call appends its optional last chunk and transcribes all audio with `small-q5_1`, then discards the utterance. An empty final body is allowed after earlier audio. Partial text can change.
- `language`: `auto` (default, detection while transcribing), `en` or `pt`. Use the same language on every call. Transcription keeps the spoken language; it does not translate. With `auto`, partials keep detecting the language from all audio so far. After at least five seconds of partial audio, the final pass reuses the latest detected English or Portuguese language to avoid a second encoder pass. Final-only requests, shorter partials and other detected languages still use automatic detection.

Returns `{"utterance":"<UUID>","text":"…","final":false}` (or `true`). Wait for each answer before sending the next chunk. Accepted chunks append exactly once per received call; this endpoint does not deduplicate retries. After an uncertain network result, let the session expire before starting again.

Only one utterance per device is open or transcribing at once (`409` for an overlapping call, a different utterance or a changed language). One resident server per distinct model and 32 open utterances are allowed across devices. Each model accepts one inference at a time (`503` when busy). A busy `503` consumes none of the rejected chunk and preserves earlier audio and its idle deadline; retry the same chunk with the same utterance UUID. Fast and final models can work independently. Idle utterances are discarded after 120 seconds following their last partial answer. `400` means invalid parameters/audio; `413` means a body or utterance is too large. Exceeding the utterance limit discards it. `415` means unsupported content type. `501` clearly identifies a missing server/model or invalid thread count. `500` means transcription failed or timed out: audio is discarded and the client starts a fresh utterance. Each inference has a 120-second deadline; a timeout kills that model’s server to release its audio. The next request or five-second maintenance pass restarts it.

Audio stays in memory throughout: normalized WAV is posted as multipart `file` data to whisper-server's `/inference` endpoint, with `language`, `prompt`, `response_format=verbose_json`, `no_language_probabilities=true` and `no_context=true`. Language probabilities are disabled because upstream otherwise encodes the audio again for that response. The runner consumes only the text and detected language; its public response is unchanged. Neither Oby nor the server writes audio or transcript files (`--convert` is never enabled). Servers bind only to `127.0.0.1`, have a random request-path prefix, receive no agent/login environment, and run directly under the runner outside `Agents.sandboxed`. Their stdout/stderr go to `/dev/null`; audio and transcripts never reach logs or agents. Startup removes legacy crash leftovers from `OBY_HOME/whisper/audio`. HTTP uses a fresh, verified loopback socket for each request, without cookies, cache or proxies. No previous utterance's decoder context is carried into another request.

The runner starts both model servers before serving requests and keeps them resident, restarting exited servers during maintenance or on demand. Startup warms each server with one second of generated silence (not microphone audio) using the short encoder window, and discards the result. Readiness and warmup share a 120-second startup deadline. A missing installation leaves the other API routes usable and returns `501` for speech. Runner shutdown kills its servers; systemd's default control-group cleanup also covers a runner crash. If both model paths are identical, they share one server. Reserve the configured loopback ports for this runner; an occupied port causes readiness to fail. Before sending any request bytes (including warmup), the runner verifies that the connected peer socket belongs to its spawned child: `/proc/net/tcp` and the child’s descriptors on Linux, `lsof` on macOS. Audio travels over that same verified socket, with no redirects or automatic reconnects, so a different listener cannot receive it even if the port changes hands. If ownership cannot be verified, speech fails closed.

Run `OBY_HOME=/srv/nvme/oby Runner/scripts/setup-whisper.sh` once as the runner's owner on tetrix-lab. It requires git, CMake, a C++ compiler, curl and flock (Ubuntu: `git cmake build-essential curl util-linux`). It pins whisper.cpp v1.8.2, builds static release `whisper-cli` and `whisper-server` binaries optimized for this CPU with GPU backends off, and atomically downloads multilingual `base-q5_1`, `large-v3-turbo-q5_0` and `small-q5_1`. Repeated runs reuse the checkout/build and completed downloads; interrupted downloads are retried. Existing unquantized models are retained but unused by default. See the [upstream server](https://github.com/ggml-org/whisper.cpp/blob/v1.8.2/examples/server/server.cpp) and [model distribution](https://github.com/ggml-org/whisper.cpp/blob/v1.8.2/models/download-ggml-model.sh).

Short audio no longer pays for Whisper's default 30-second padding. Each request sets `audio_ctx` to `min(1500, max(600, (ceil(seconds) + 1) * 50))`: at least 12 seconds, a second beyond the audio, and at most the full 30-second encoder window. The 11-second sample uses 600 frames. Utterances of 30–60 seconds use the full window and whisper.cpp's successive-window processing; the 60-second input cap is unchanged.

Measured on tetrix-lab (16 CPU cores), 2026-10-11, with whisper.cpp v1.8.2's 11-second `samples/jfk.wav`, CPU-only, eight inference threads, `no_context=true`, no timestamps, and prompt `oby, tetrix`. Timing covers the loopback inference request through the parsed response, excluding startup/model loading. Models were resident and warmed with generated silence. Loads are one-minute averages recorded before each request; concurrent work changed between runs, so this is not a controlled comparison with the earlier load-13.6 CLI measurements (base-q5_1 4.3 s, unquantized turbo 27 s).

| Model / language | Encoder frames | Inference seconds | Load before each request |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| small-q5_1 / en | 600 | 1.710, 2.083, 1.632 | 28.12, 27.15, 27.15 |
| large-v3-turbo-q5_0 / en | 600 | 13.129, 12.826, 13.715 | 48.08, 49.90, 46.28 |
| small-q5_1 / auto | 600 | 9.764, 2.991 | 27.15, 40.65 |
| large-v3-turbo-q5_0 / auto | 600 | 25.900, 24.094 | 49.00, 53.18 |

These first measurements used the text-only JSON response. A repeat with the production verbose response and language probabilities disabled measured small-q5_1 at **27.008, 10.515, 11.136 s**, at loads **76.72, 77.19, 74.21**. The same production-response repeat measured quantized turbo at **13.181, 12.641, 12.174 s**, at loads **75.15, 70.89, 60.78**. The returned word sequence matched in the response-format checks. Earlier full-window (1500-frame) resident runs were also slow: small 47.923/63.658 s (loads 75.91/83.18), quantized turbo 50.006/36.528 s (loads 71.16/73.02). Reducing threads to one, two or four did not improve the best eight-thread result; retain eight by default.

**Default: small-q5_1.** It reached about two seconds on the warmed English sample; turbo remains too slow. Startup warmup, the smaller encoder window and reuse of a sufficiently long partial's detected language avoid repeated setup and detection work in the live speech path. This is not a two-second guarantee under heavy contention: the current machine sometimes ran at loads above 100, and even the small model missed the target badly. Final-only automatic detection is also slower. Use `en`/`pt` when the device knows its language, or send live partials; longer utterances and Portuguese recognition still need real Watch measurements.

The runner reads this configuration from its environment at startup (restart after changes):

| Variable | Default |
| --- | --- |
| `OBY_WHISPER_SERVER` | `OBY_HOME/whisper/bin/whisper-server` |
| `OBY_WHISPER_FAST_MODEL` | `OBY_HOME/whisper/models/ggml-base-q5_1.bin` |
| `OBY_WHISPER_FINAL_MODEL` | `OBY_HOME/whisper/models/ggml-small-q5_1.bin` |
| `OBY_WHISPER_THREADS` | `8` per model server, allowing two inferences to share tetrix-lab's 16 CPU cores |
| `OBY_WHISPER_FAST_PORT` | `8171` (loopback only) |
| `OBY_WHISPER_FINAL_PORT` | `8172` (loopback only) |
| `OBY_SPEECH_NAMES` | Unset: `oby`, `tetrix` and the short names of the runner's current `Desk.repos` list. Set a comma-separated list to override these names. Duplicates are removed and the initial prompt is capped at 512 UTF-8 bytes, including for overrides. |

No GPU is needed. The sample timings above do not replace checks with real Watch recordings.

### `GET /v1/watches`, `POST /v1/watches` and `DELETE /v1/watches/{id}`

The Apple Watches paired with the runner, for [the Watch API](#the-watch-api). On this API, so only the owner's iPhone over the tailnet reaches them.

`POST /v1/watches` pairs one. The Watch makes its key in its Secure Enclave and hands the public half to the iPhone over WatchConnectivity; the iPhone signs the pairing with its approval key (the owner's tap) and sends it with the Cloudflare Access service token it gives the Watch:

```json
{"name": "Wellington's Apple Watch", "publicKey": "BK9…",
 "accessClientId": "4a1c….access", "accessClientSecret": "9f2e…",
 "approval": {"device": "d7c1…", "signedAt": "2026-10-11T09:12:30Z", "signature": "MEUCIQ…"}}
```

- `publicKey`: the Watch's P-256 key, X9.63, base64. `approval.signature`: ECDSA P-256 with SHA-256 (DER, base64) by the registered phone's key (`POST /v1/devices`) over `"oby-pair-watch\n<publicKey>\n<signedAt>"`, within 2 minutes, once. Agents on the runner's machine reach this API as the owner, but they can't make that signature.
- The service token goes to the runner's vault (`watch-access-client-id`, `watch-access-client-token`), where no agent sees it and the secret scan learns it, so nothing outgoing carries it. A newer pairing replaces it. No answer or event carries it.
- It returns `201` with `{"id": "5e0f…", "url": "https://oby-watch.example.com"}`: the Watch's id and the Watch API's address, which the iPhone hands the Watch with the token. A key the runner knows keeps its id. `409` when the runner has no `watch.json` yet, `403` for an approval that doesn't pass, `422` for a key or token that isn't one.

`GET /v1/watches` lists them, `[{"id": "5e0f…", "name": "Wellington's Apple Watch", "registered": "2026-10-11T09:12:31Z"}]`, never a key or the token. `DELETE /v1/watches/{id}` removes one (the iPhone's Settings): its requests fail from then on and its push tokens are dropped; with the last Watch gone, the service token leaves the vault. `404` for a Watch the runner doesn't know.

## The Watch API

The Watch works without its iPhone. While the phone is in reach, the Watch goes through it as before (WatchConnectivity, `Shared/WatchWire.swift`); otherwise it calls this API itself, and goes back to the phone once it's in reach again. watchOS has no Tailscale or VPN, and only plain HTTPS through `URLSession` (no WebSockets), so:

- **Where:** the runner listens on a second address, `127.0.0.1:7778` (`OBY_WATCH_PORT`), which serves only the endpoints below. A Cloudflare Tunnel (`cloudflared`) publishes it at a hostname of the owner's, and a Cloudflare Access application on that hostname lets through only requests carrying its service token (`CF-Access-Client-Id` and `CF-Access-Client-Secret` headers, a Service Auth policy).
- **Set up** on the runner's machine, in `$OBY_HOME/watch.json`, read again whenever it changes:

  ```json
  {"url": "https://oby-watch.example.com", "team": "example",
   "audience": "4714c1358e65fe4b408ad6d432a5f878f08194bdb4752441fd56faefa9b2b6f2",
   "keys": [{"kid": "…", "kty": "RSA", "alg": "RS256", "n": "…", "e": "AQAB"}]}
  ```

  `url` is the tunnel's hostname, `team` the Zero Trust team name, `audience` the Access application's AUD tag, and `keys` the `keys` list from `https://<team>.cloudflareaccess.com/cdn-cgi/access/certs`. Cloudflare rotates those keys every six weeks: paste the new list in. Without a usable `watch.json`, every request is `503`.
- **Never** `Tailscale-User-Login`: it means nothing here. Every request needs both of these, or it's `401` with the reason:
  1. **`Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion`**, which Access adds to every request it lets through: an RS256 JWT, checked against `keys` (by `kid`), with `audience` in `aud`, `iss` `https://<team>.cloudflareaccess.com`, and `exp`, `nbf` and `iat` current (a minute of clock difference allowed).
  2. **A signature from a paired Watch**, over the request:
     - `Oby-Watch`: the Watch's id from pairing.
     - `Oby-Time`: when it was signed, in seconds since 1970. At most 2 minutes behind the runner's clock and 5 seconds ahead of it.
     - `Oby-Nonce`: 16 to 64 letters, digits, `-` or `_`, new for every request. A nonce the runner has seen from that Watch is refused, so a replayed request fails, after a restart too: the runner keeps used nonces in `$OBY_HOME/watch-nonces.json` (0600) until their request is too old to get in. When it can't save or read that file, Watch requests are `503`.
     - `Oby-Signature`: ECDSA P-256 with SHA-256 (DER, base64), by the Watch's Secure Enclave key, over the UTF-8 bytes of `"oby-watch\n<METHOD>\n<path and query, as sent>\n<Oby-Time>\n<Oby-Nonce>\n<hex SHA-256 of the body>"` (the body's hash is that of no bytes for a `GET`).
- Answers and errors are as on the tailnet API, vault secrets taken out the same way.

The endpoints, each as on the tailnet API unless it says otherwise:

| Endpoint | |
|---|---|
| `GET /v1/jobs?limit=50` | The jobs. |
| `GET /v1/jobs/{id}` | One job. |
| `GET /v1/events` | The event stream. The Watch follows it only while Oby is on screen. |
| `POST /v1/plans` | What was dictated: the work it plans starts, and the answer carries the jobs. |
| `POST /v1/jobs` | A job described whole; a plan's draft from before #269 is `410`. |
| `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages` | A word to the job's agent. |
| `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/dismiss` | Dismiss. |
| `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/move` | Move to another tool, account or model. |
