- `leastThink`: for tests. The least time a brain is asked to think in, in seconds, from 1 to less than `turnBudget`; 1 when left out. With that much of the turn's budget left or less, no brain is asked (`budget_spent`, #615). Tests widen it so a busy machine can't miss the window (#763).
- `orchestratorDelay`: how long the event orchestrator waits for nearby events to come together before its turn, in seconds, 1 to 600; 30 when left out (obid only: the brain, "The event orchestrator").
- `gateway`: where paired phones reach obid, an address on `127.0.0.1` like `127.0.0.1:8810`; no gateway when left out (obid only: the gateway).
- `pushRelay`: the control plane the iPhone's sealed pushes go through, an `https://` origin (default Deskree's, `https://oby-control.deskree.com`; set it for a control plane of your own). An iPhone's registration with any other is refused ([`POST /v1/notifications`](#post-v1notifications), obid's part).
- `tetrixPresets`: the Tetrix servers Settings offers by name before a URL field (obid only: Tetrix), each `{"id", "name", "url", "client"}` (`client` optional: a client registered for Oby there; left out, obid registers one), for example `[{"id": "team", "name": "Our Tetrix", "url": "https://tetrix.example/mcp"}]`. Each `url` follows a connection's rule (HTTPS, no login, query or fragment; HTTP only on 127.0.0.1), and ids are unique. `[]` when left out: the open-source build names no one's server, and the owner types a URL.
- `vmHelper`, `vmImage`, `vmImageUrl`, `guestModelApi`, `guestRequestEvery`: retired with compute sharing (2026-10-27): ignored with a warning.
- `claudeKeychain`: `true` lets obid copy Claude Code's login from the macOS Keychain where a boxed run reads it, only what calls the API, and have the Mac's own Claude Code refresh the default login in its last 5 minutes ("The job sandbox", below), and the login of every Claude Code account obid added itself (`POST /v1/accounts`: its folder is obid's `home/accounts/<id>`), each its own Keychain item, the refresh run only while the folder's login files and their locks are the person's own and no link (#877). An account the configuration names with a `configDir` of its own isn't refreshed while the Mac app's Swift engine may still run on that folder (#655): its runs start while its token is far from its end; `false` keeps it off. Left out, obid decides when it starts (the owner's decision of 2026-10-18): on macOS it turns it on when Claude Code's default login is in the Keychain and no `~/.claude/.credentials.json` is there (it asks only whether the item exists, never for what it holds), and off otherwise, so a fresh Mac's Claude accounts don't read as signed out. `GET /v1/status` says which: `"claudeKeychain": {"on": true, "auto": true}`, `auto` false when the configuration set it. Only on macOS; elsewhere it does nothing (`on` false). obid reads the Keychain with `/usr/bin/security`; its own tests set `OBID_SECURITY_BIN` to an absolute path of a stand-in, so a test's obid never reads the person's real Keychain.
- `claudeOwnSandbox`: the Claude Code accounts, by `id`, whose jobs run in Claude Code's own sandbox rather than obid's box (the owner's decision of 2026-10-26); `[]` when left out, and every other account's jobs stay boxed with a copy of the login, as below. Such a job's Claude Code runs unboxed by obid, in a session of its own, with the account's own folder (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`: its `configDir`, or `~/.claude`) and its own Keychain login (`CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR`), which it refreshes itself: no copy, no Keychain read by obid, no keeper. Its own `HOME` (and `CFFIXED_USER_HOME`) is the job's `home/logins/<job id>`, which its commands can neither read nor write, holding only `Library/Keychains`, a link to the person's, where `security` finds the login; obid makes the link fresh before every turn, never following anything found at `Library` or `Keychains`. Its commands get the job's temporary folder as `HOME` (so what they build and cache is the job's) through `CLAUDE_ENV_FILE`, `env.sh` in that folder, which obid writes again every turn, as a new file, never through a link found there (`O_EXCL`): Claude Code lets commands write that one file whatever the settings say (not replace it with a link), and what it sets reaches only them. Its `--settings` turn on its sandbox for every command (`failIfUnavailable`, `allowUnsandboxedCommands: false`, `autoAllowBashIfSandboxed: false`, so obid still answers each command), let it write only where obid's box would (the worktree, the job's git folder, its temporary folder) and never the git pointers obid's box keeps as obid wrote them (the worktree's `.git`, the git folder's `commondir` and `gitdir`) or Claude Code's `Library` and its Keychains link (`sandbox.filesystem.denyWrite`, and `Edit(…)` deny rules), and read nothing in obid's home, the person's home or any account's folder (`denyRead`, whole, so a folder made there mid-turn is denied too) but what the box reads: the job's own paths and the tools' folders (`allowRead`). That kernel sandbox covers its commands only. Its Read, Edit and Write tools run in Claude Code's own process, outside any sandbox: they ask before going outside the worktree (links followed), and in this mode obid refuses that whatever the job's permission mode (`policy::names_outside`: a `file_path`, `notebook_path` or search `path`, and an absolute Glob pattern), and an edit of the worktree's `.git`; a boxed job's read outside its worktree may still ask the owner. A link swapped between Claude Code's check and its open of the file isn't stopped, which is why the mode is opt-in. obid's own git calls in a job's worktree run pinned to its git folders (`GIT_DIR`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`: the ones it checked, or for the Mac check's bundle and a permission's `git ls-files`, where obid makes them), never through the worktree's `.git` or `commondir`. An account whose `.claude.json` (in that folder) sets, in its `env`, a variable no agent gets or one this mode sets (`GIT_*`, `HOME`, `PATH`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` …) doesn't run: Claude Code applies that `env` whatever `--setting-sources` says, and the job fails saying which. Account failover never moves a job into this mode or out of it, since its session is in the account's own folder. git reads only the clone's settings (`GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` set to the job's), since Claude Code runs git itself outside its sandbox; with no global settings, and `credential.helper` set empty in the environment (`GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`), there's no credential helper either. Its commands never reach a code host (`sandbox.network.deniedDomains`: `github.com`, `gitlab.com`, `bitbucket.org` and their subdomains), whatever else is allowed: only Publish pushes. `own_sandbox_probes` (`cargo test -p obid --lib own_sandbox_probes -- --ignored`, on a Mac with Claude Code's login in the Keychain) runs the real Claude Code so: its commands can't read obid's token, reach the Keychain (its own login, obid's vault), get a password from git, push (`git push --dry-run` to GitHub) or call `gh`, read another job's folder or the vault's file when they were made after the settings, write outside the job, rewrite the git pointers, move Claude Code's `Library` or re-point its Keychains link, reach obid's API or start an app, while they still write caches in their own `HOME`; its Read tool can't read obid's home; and it commits in its worktree. `own_sandbox_env_and_settings` checks the environment and settings on every run of the tests. Only jobs: a conversation's and the brain's Claude Code stay boxed.
- `cursorKeychain`: the same for Cursor's own login (the account with no `configDir`), which cursor-agent keeps in the macOS Keychain by default there (with `AGENT_CLI_CREDENTIAL_STORE` left out: account `cursor-user`, items `cursor-access-token`, `cursor-refresh-token` and `cursor-api-key`). `true` lets obid copy its access token and API key into a boxed run's login file, `false` keeps it off; left out, obid turns it on when it starts on macOS when one of those two items is there and no `~/.cursor/auth.json` is (asking only whether the item exists). obid asks only for `cursor-access-token` and `cursor-api-key` (`security find-generic-password -a cursor-user -s <item> -w`), never for the refresh token: a refresh inside a box would rotate it away from the Mac's own cursor-agent, which keeps the login fresh when the person uses it. `GET /v1/status` says which: `"cursorKeychain": {"on": true, "auto": true}`. Accounts obid adds sign in to a file in their own folder (`AGENT_CLI_CREDENTIAL_STORE=file`), so this is only ever Cursor's own login.
- An account's `configDir` (any tool) is never one that holds obid's `home`, nor anywhere inside `home` but a folder of `home/accounts` (where the accounts API makes them): obid's home holds its database, clones and the jobs' worktrees.
- `conversation` (optional): how conversations run (obid only: the conversation harness). `agent`: `"claude"` (each conversation is a Claude Code session, the default), `"codex"` (each is a Codex thread); the retired `"plan"` value uses Claude Code with a warning. `model`: the agent's model for conversations, `claude-sonnet-5-5` on Claude Code and Codex's own on Codex when left out. `budget`: how long one turn may take, in seconds, 10 to 3,600; 600 when left out. `turnBudget` is retired.

**What Settings changes** is kept in `home` as files, not in the database: the permission rules and Settings › Permissions' default mode (`GET`/`PUT /v1/settings/permissions`, `/v1/permissions/rules`) in `home/permissions.json`, and `maxRunning` (`PUT /v1/settings/jobs`) in `home/settings.json`, both 0600 and kept across restarts.

The API token is the one in `home/api-token` (0600, at least 32 visible characters): obid makes it on first start, or takes the one put there before it starts. The shells on the same machine read it there. It is never in obid's environment or arguments, where any process of the same user can read it (on macOS through `KERN_PROCARGS2`, which no sandbox holds back; #439): obid refuses to start when `OBID_API_TOKEN` is set, saying to put the token in that file instead. The same holds for every secret obid holds: none is ever in its environment or arguments, nor in a child's. Every request sends `Authorization: Bearer <token>`; anything else is `401`. Every `/v1` answer carries `Cache-Control: no-store` (unless it sets its own, like the event stream's `no-cache`) and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. `GET /v1/status` adds `"engine": "obid"` to `runner`. **While it starts** obid answers as soon as it listens: until it has opened everything (the Keychain and vault probes, the accounts, the sandbox's self-check, the jobs), `GET /v1/status` with the token answers `503` with `Retry-After: 1` and `{"error": "Oby’s engine is starting.", "starting": {"ms": <since it started>, "done": [{"step": "listen", "ms": 0}, …], "now": "<the step under way>"}}`, and every other call waits until obid is ready, then answers as usual. A call without the token, or from a name other than loopback's, is refused at once (`401`, `403`), never held; an agent's own routes (`/v1/agent/mcp/<id>`, `/v1/agent/tetrix/<id>`, one Bearer token, from loopback or, on Linux, `10.0.2.2`) wait too, at most 64 at once (`503` starting beyond), and their tokens are checked once obid is ready. A start that fails answers the calls that waited `503` (`Oby’s engine didn’t start.`) and says why in `home/last-start-error`; a stop while starting answers them `503` (`Oby’s engine is stopping.`). On SIGTERM or SIGINT obid stops taking requests, leaves its jobs as a restart would (After any restart, above) and exits 0 within 10 seconds.

**A start that fails says why.** obid logs a line as each step of its start ends (`starting: vault done in 3 ms`), then one with them all (`obid started in 312 ms: …`), so a start that hangs names the step after the last one logged. A top-level key of the configuration it doesn't know (one a later obid retired, or an earlier one wrote), and a key retired from a section (`conversation.harness`), are ignored, with a warning in the log that names each (`conversation.harness isn't a key this obid knows: ignored`), so an update never stops on one; any other unknown key inside a section (`agents`, a tool, an account, `repos`, `vault`) is still refused, so a typo (`checks` for `check`) never quietly drops a check. Nothing optional stops a start either: a busy gateway, a Sphere key that can't be read (left out and said in the log and `GET /v1/obid/sphere`'s `lastError`; `POST /v1/obid/sphere/key` is then `409` rather than make a new key over it, which would end the Obi and its paired phones), or a folder for exchanges, the inbox or artifacts it can't make (each save then fails with its own reason). Its last words before it exits are on stderr with the time, like its log's lines. When it can't start (a configuration it refuses, a token it can't use, a home it can't make, the API's address taken), it writes the reason it logs to `home/last-start-error` (the configuration's `home` as its JSON names it, else Oby.app's `~/Library/Application Support/Oby/Runner`) and exits non-zero; the apps on the Mac show it in place of a wait. It's removed once obid listens. The `security` probes obid asks while it starts end within 5 seconds (60 for a value, which can wait on the Keychain's prompt), so a Keychain that doesn't answer can't hold its start up.

**The Mac app on the same Mac (#440).** For Settings › Runner › This Mac, set obid's `home` to
`~/Library/Application Support/Oby/Runner` (expanded to an absolute path in the JSON) and leave
`listen` out, or use `127.0.0.1:7777`. This is `LocalRunner.home`, also where the app opens device
pairing. The app reads `api-token` there directly: no token in a process's environment or
arguments, no copy in UserDefaults, and no change to how the app starts. The file must be a
regular file owned by the current user with no group or other permissions (normally 0600),
at most 4096 bytes, and contain at least 32 visible ASCII characters with only optional trailing
CR/LF. Links, directories and malformed or exposed files are ignored. The existing Swift runner
continues using its keychain item (`com.deskree.oby.runner`, account `token`); that credential is
the fallback when no usable obid file exists or the server refuses its token with 401. A refused
credential is refreshed, so a stale file does not prevent connecting to a running Swift runner,
and rotating obid's token does not require restarting the app. Other obid homes and ports remain
valid for other clients; This Mac mode uses the fixed home and address above.

**Installing it (#585).** `install/install.sh` makes `home` (0700) and `home/api-token` (0600), writes this configuration (or keeps one already there, and then uses its `home` and `listen`), and runs obid as a launchd agent (macOS: label `com.deskree.obid`, home `~/Library/Application Support/Oby/Runner`, the This Mac home above) or a systemd user service (Linux: `obid.service`). With `--service`, `--prefix`, `--home`, `--config` and `--listen` it installs a second obid beside the first without touching it. The token is never in an argument, an environment variable, the service file or the configuration. docs/install.md also says how to update, uninstall and migrate from the Swift runner. **On a Mac, Oby.app runs obid itself** (2026-10-18): `Contents/Helpers/obid`, registered as the login item `com.deskree.oby.obid` with "This Mac", started as `obid serve` with no `--config` (macOS only: this configuration's file, `~/Library/Application Support/Oby/obid/config.json`, written the first time with `listen` 127.0.0.1:7777, this `home`, the login's name as `owner`, Claude Code and Codex when found, `gateway` 127.0.0.1:8810 and `telemetry` false; its first start lists the tools' own logins it finds too, obid only: accounts, "Logins already on the Mac"; its log `~/Library/Logs/obid.log`). obid has no plug-ins and serves no web app any more, and has no Cloudflare Tunnel: `status.tunnel` is always `{"state": "off", "detail": null}`, for the apps that still decode it. An install.sh launch agent (`com.deskree.obid`) keeps running until the app moves it (docs/install.md).

### obid only: accounts

The owner adds, renames and removes the agents' accounts, and signs them in, from the apps (Settings › Accounts). The configuration's `agents.<tool>.accounts` are the seed. What these endpoints change is kept in `home/accounts.json` (0600) and laid over the seed at every start; the configuration file is never rewritten.

- **When a change applies.** Every change reaches routing, the brain and the gateway's scrubbing at once, and every app following `GET /v1/events` gets `accounts` (the whole list, as `GET /v1/status` has it).
- **Checks at start.** Each entry of `home/accounts.json` is checked as the API checks it, and one that isn't right is left out and logged: an id obid didn't make (eight lowercase hex digits: macOS ignores letter case, so two ids differing only in case would share a folder), a folder other than `home/accounts/<id>`, a name or payer the API would refuse, a name (added or changed) another account of the tool has, a change for an account obid doesn't have, or past 32 accounts.
- **A file that doesn't read.** obid logs why, runs with the configuration's accounts, and answers `503` to every change until the owner fixes or removes the file.
- **No tool comes back on its own.** A tool whose accounts were all removed is gone, rather than back on its own default login (the person's), which an empty `accounts` otherwise means. Its default login is listed, as `default` (Claude Code) or `codex` (Codex), once anything about the tool's accounts changed, and can be removed like any account.
- **Logins already on the Mac.** At every start, and again at every `GET /v1/status`, obid looks for each tool's own login (with `claudeKeychain` or `cursorKeychain` left out and off, a login that is now only in the Keychain turns its copy on then, as it would have at start; a tool's CLI is also looked for in `~/.local/bin`, `~/.bun/bin`, nvm's default node and `/opt/homebrew/bin` each time, so one installed after obid started is found): its CLI is found (the configuration's `bin`, else `claude`, `codex` or `cursor-agent`) and its login is there (Claude Code's `~/.claude/.credentials.json`, or its Keychain item with `claudeKeychain`; Codex's `~/.codex/auth.json`; Cursor's `~/.cursor/auth.json` on macOS, `~/.config/cursor/auth.json` elsewhere, which cursor-agent writes only with `AGENT_CLI_CREDENTIAL_STORE=file`; its default on macOS is the Keychain, where obid finds it with `cursorKeychain`). On a fresh obid (its first start: no `home/accounts.json` and no `home/jobs` yet), each such tool with no account at all (none configured or added; an empty `accounts` is Claude Code's or Codex's own login already) gets its own login listed: `default`, `codex` or `cursor`, with no folder, within the 32 accounts. An obid that ran before keeps its accounts as they are, so routing never moves to a login the owner didn't pick: what it finds is only offered, in `GET /v1/status`'s `availableLogins`, and listed with `{"agent", "default": true}` (below). Never one the tool lists already (by its id, an empty list, or any account without a `configDir`), nor one whose name another of its accounts has. Those obid listed are kept in `home/accounts.json` (`own`); one the owner removes stays removed across restarts, offered again, until it's asked back. A Cursor job on its own login runs as an account's does: in a folder of the job's own, with a copy of the login file (only `accessToken` and `apiKey`) and of `~/.cursor/cli-config.json`, obid's settings over it.
- **Not on the gateway.** None of these endpoints is on the gateway's allowlist: a paired phone's token must never add an account or sign obid into someone else's login, which would send the owner's code to that account. The Mac app calls them over loopback.

**`POST /v1/accounts`** adds one:

```json
{"agent": "codex", "name": "Personal", "payer": "Wellington"}
```

- `agent` is `claude`, `codex` or `cursor`. A tool the configuration doesn't name gets one while it has an account, run as `claude`, `codex` or `cursor-agent` found on the login shell's `PATH`. A tool whose configured `accounts` is empty keeps its default login beside what's added.
- `name` and `payer` are each one line of 1 to 60 characters, trimmed. `payer` is optional: null or empty means none.
- No two accounts of a tool have the same name, nor a name that is another one's id, letter case aside: jobs name an account by either.
- obid makes the folder, `home/accounts/<id>` (0700), and removes it with the account. The API names no folder: a body with `configDir`, or any other key, is `422`. obid makes the `id` (eight hex digits). At most 32 accounts, the configuration's among them.
- `201` with the Account; `422` with the reason for a wrong body.
- **The tool's own login back.** `{"agent": "claude" | "codex" | "cursor", "default": true}` and nothing else lists the tool's own login (`default`, `codex`, `cursor`) after it was removed, as it was, or when obid found it on this Mac and offers it (`availableLogins`): no folder, signed in with the tool itself. `201` with it; `409` while it's listed, or when it was neither removed here nor found. The Mac offers it as `Use Claude Code's Own Login` under Add an account once it was removed there. Any other account removed stays removed.

**`PATCH /v1/accounts/{id}`** with `{"name": "…"}`, `{"payer": "…" | null}` or both answers the Account. Errors are `422` as above, and `404` for an unknown account. The configuration's accounts can be renamed and given a payer too.

**`DELETE /v1/accounts/{id}`** answers `204`.

- A sign-in under way ends first; `409` (`Personal's sign-in didn't end within 5 seconds: try again in a moment.`) when it doesn't within 5 seconds.
- `409` while a job of the account is queued or running: `Personal has a job queued or running: stop it, or move it to another account, first.`
- Its folder is removed only when obid made it (a real folder, never through a link). A configuration account that is removed stays removed across restarts. Its id is never made again, and its folder (one obid didn't make stays) is still hidden from every box, kept in `home/accounts.json` (`retired`).
- What the tool itself keeps stays: on macOS, Claude Code's Keychain item for the folder (Keychain Access removes it), and the login on the provider's side (signed in with Anthropic, OpenAI or Cursor until the person signs it out there).

**`GET /v1/accounts/{id}/sign-in`** runs the tool's own login for the account, never obid's (AGENTS.md: obid never reads, stores or forwards the login), and answers what the person needs to finish it:

```json
{"account": "a1b2c3d4", "url": "https://claude.com/cai/oauth/authorize?code=true&…", "userCode": null,
 "pasteCode": true, "expiresAt": "2026-10-18T10:15:00Z"}
```

- **What the app does.** It opens `url` in the browser. `userCode` (Codex) is the one-time code the person types on that page. `pasteCode` (Claude Code) means the page ends by showing a code, which the app sends back with `POST`.
- **One at a time.** Asked again while it's under way, the answer is the same sign-in; there is one per account. It ends after 15 minutes (Codex's code lasts that long), or once it has signed in.
- **Jobs wait.** While a sign-in is under way, and while an account is being removed, no job is dispatched to the account (a job naming it is refused: `Personal is being signed in or removed: try again in a moment.`; routing passes it by) and none starts on it: a job queued on it in the meantime stays queued and starts once the change ends. That holds from the moment no job of it was queued or running, and both are looked at under the same lock. An abandoned sign-in is ended with `DELETE` (below), rather than keeping the account's jobs waiting until it runs out.
- **Where it runs.** The login runs in the account's own folder: no box ever gets that folder (#655, "The job sandbox"), so nothing a job wrote can steer the login. The tool writes the login there itself, and obid never reads or copies what it writes there except for the boxed runs' copies.
  - **Claude Code:** `claude auth login` with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set to the account's folder. On macOS the login goes to that folder's Keychain item, the one obid copies for a boxed run with `claudeKeychain`, so on macOS it's `409` unless `claudeKeychain` is on. Without a terminal, Claude Code prints a link whose page shows the code, and reads the code on its stdin.
  - **Codex:** `codex login --device-auth -c cli_auth_credentials_store="file"` with `CODEX_HOME` and `HOME` set to the account's folder. It prints a link and a one-time code, and listens on no port (its browser login needs `localhost:1455`). It ends by itself once the person has signed in.
  - **Cursor:** `cursor-agent login` with `NO_OPEN_BROWSER=1`, the account's own `HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR`, and `AGENT_CLI_CREDENTIAL_STORE=file`, so its login is a file under the folder rather than the macOS Keychain (jobs run with the same setting and get a copy of that file). Its caches (`NODE_COMPILE_CACHE`, `XDG_CACHE_HOME`) go in obid's folder for the sign-in, and its `cli-config.json` is rewritten first, as before a job. It ends by itself once the person has signed in.
- **How it runs.** Every login runs unboxed, since it must reach the Keychain and the network:
  - by its absolute path (`503` when obid can't find one);
  - with its environment made from scratch: the kept variables, `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin` plus the folder of the tool's own binary (links followed: Cursor runs the `node` beside it) and, for a script that starts `#!/usr/bin/env <program>` (a CLI installed with npm: `#!/usr/bin/env node`), the folder of that program as obid finds it on the login shell's `PATH`, no token, and no Claude Code or Anthropic variable;
  - with `BROWSER=true`, so nothing opens on the runner;
  - with `TMPDIR` and its working folder set to a new folder of obid's (`home/tmp/sign-in-…`, removed when it ends, and swept at obid's next start);
  - in a process group of its own, which obid kills when the login ends, when the sign-in runs out, is ended or the account is removed, when obid stops, and when the request that started it ends before the login gave its link (its folder removed with it).
- **What obid takes from its output.** What a login prints is read, at most 4,096 bytes of each line, only for the link and the code:
  - the link is the first `https://` link on the tool's own sites (`claude.com`, `claude.ai`, `anthropic.com`; `openai.com`, `chatgpt.com`; `cursor.com`, `cursor.sh`, or below them), with no login, port or space in it;
  - the code is Codex's, a line like `3U0G-DCVSU`.
  Nothing else it prints is kept, answered or logged. obid's log says only that a sign-in started and whether it signed the account in.
- **Errors.** `404` for an unknown account. `409` in four cases:
  - the tool's own default login (an account without a folder: it is the person's, signed in with the tool itself), and a folder whose login file or its lock (Claude Code's `.credentials.json…`, Codex's `auth.json…`, Cursor's `.cursor/auth.json` or `.config/cursor/auth.json` and the folders on the way) is a link or not the person's, since the login would write the new sign-in wherever it leads;
  - a job of the account is queued or running;
  - the account is being removed;
  - Claude Code on macOS without `claudeKeychain`.
  `502` when the login gave no link within 30 seconds.

**`POST /v1/accounts/{id}/sign-in`** with `{"code": "…"}` sends the code the page showed to Claude Code's login, on its stdin, once. The code is never kept or logged.

- It answers the Account once it's signed in.
- `422` when the code isn't one line of at most 4,096 characters, or when Claude Code didn't sign in with it (`Claude Code didn't sign in with that code. Start again for a new link.`: the sign-in has ended).
- `409` when no sign-in is under way, a code went to it already, or the tool takes no code (Codex, Cursor).
- `504` when it didn't end within 60 seconds.

**`DELETE /v1/accounts/{id}/sign-in`** ends the account's sign-in under way, with everything its login started: `204`, also when none was under way. `404` for an unknown account; `409` when it didn't end within 5 seconds.

Every refusal above carries its `reason` ([Sign-in reasons](#obid-only-sign-in-reasons)): `ownLogin`, `folderUnsafe`, `busy` (also on `DELETE /v1/accounts/{id}`'s job `409`) and `keychainOff` for the `409`s, `notInstalled` for the `503`, `didNotStart` for the `502`, and `codeRefused` for `POST`'s `422` of a code Claude Code refused.

When a sign-in ends, every app gets `accounts`, with `signedIn` as obid finds it (the login file, or the Keychain item with `claudeKeychain`, or Cursor's own login's with `cursorKeychain`).

### obid only: GitHub's sign-in

The owner signs obid's `gh` in to GitHub from the Mac (Settings › Connectors › GitHub, and the setup's GitHub step), with GitHub's own device sign-in; nobody types `gh auth login` (the owner's decision of 2026-10-18). obid never reads, holds or passes the token: gh keeps it in its own Keychain item, as `gh auth login` always does. None of these endpoints is on the gateway's allowlist: a paired phone never signs the Mac in to someone's GitHub.

**`GET /v1/github`** answers `{"gh": true, "signedIn": true, "user": "well-prado", "signIn": null, "reviewer": {"signedIn": true, "user": "oby-reviewer", "signIn": null, "error": null}}`:

- `gh`: whether obid has a GitHub CLI (the one Oby.app carries, else the login shell's).
- `signedIn` and `user`: the active github.com account in `gh auth status --hostname github.com --active --json hosts` whose `state` is `success` (never `--show-token`); signed out otherwise. `user` is a GitHub login name or null. Read again at most every 30 seconds, and after a sign-in ends.
- `signIn`: the sign-in under way, as `POST` answers it, or null.
- `reviewer`: the reviewer's login (below), read the same way from its own settings folder: `signedIn`, `user`, its sign-in under way (`signIn`), and `error`, why its last sign-in kept no login (the owner's own account), or null; cleared by the next sign-in or a sign-out.

**`POST /v1/github/sign-in`** starts gh's own sign-in, or answers the one under way: `{"url": "https://github.com/login/device", "userCode": "AB12-CD34", "expiresAt": "…"}`. The app opens `url` and the person types `userCode` there (gh also puts it on its machine's clipboard).

- It runs `gh auth login --hostname github.com --git-protocol https --web --skip-ssh-key`, unboxed (it must reach the Keychain and the network), by its absolute path, with obid's kept environment (no token, no agent variable), the person's `HOME` (where gh keeps its settings), `GH_BROWSER` and `BROWSER` set to `true` (nothing opens on the runner), prompts off, and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` a file in the sign-in's own folder (`home/tmp/github-sign-in-…`, removed when it ends), so gh never changes the person's global git settings. obid needs no `gh auth setup-git`: it gives git gh's login itself (obid only: publishing).
- obid reads what gh prints only for its one-time code (`! One-time code (AB12-CD34) …` or `! First copy your one-time code: AB12-CD34`); nothing else is kept, answered or logged. The page is always GitHub's device page.
- One at a time. It ends when gh does (signed in, or not), after 15 minutes (a device code's life), with `DELETE`, or when obid stops; its process group is killed with it. `502` when gh gave no code within 30 seconds; `503` without a gh.

The `503` carries `reason` `notInstalled`, the `502` `didNotStart`, and the `409` while a sign-in is starting `busy` (the reviewer's sign-in too). A sign-in that ended without a login shows as `signedIn` false and `signIn` null.

**`DELETE /v1/github/sign-in`** ends the sign-in under way, if any: `204`.

#### The reviewer

The reviewer is a second GitHub account that Oby posts its review of the owner's own pull requests as (the owner's decisions of 2026-10-24: Oby carries a task the whole way, and GitHub lets nobody approve their own pull request). Settings has no row for it yet; these endpoints are for the app to add one.

**`POST /v1/github/reviewer/sign-in`** starts the reviewer's sign-in, or answers the one under way, exactly as `POST /v1/github/sign-in` does (`{"url", "userCode", "expiresAt"}`, the same device page, one at a time, the same limits and errors), with these differences:

- `409` (`Sign in to GitHub first: …`) until the owner's gh is signed in: the reviewer must be another account.
- gh signs in to a settings folder of the sign-in's own (`GH_CONFIG_DIR` in `home/tmp/github-sign-in-…/gh`, 0700) with `--insecure-storage`, so its token is a file there, never a Keychain item, and is kept away from the keyring altogether: on macOS it runs under a Seatbelt profile that denies `/usr/bin/security` (how gh reaches the Keychain), and everywhere with a D-Bus session that isn't there (how it reaches a Secret Service). gh keeps a host's active token in the keyring's one shared slot whatever its settings folder, so a sign-in that reached the keyring would clear the owner's, and a call whose own file lost its token would go as the owner. Where neither holds, the reviewer's gh doesn't run. Inside a job sandbox, which denies the Keychain already and can't nest another, it runs as it is.
- When gh ends signed in, obid reads that folder's account (`gh auth status`, as above) and the owner's. When they're the same account (in any letter case), nothing is kept and `reviewer.error` says why (`<login> is your own GitHub account: …`); likewise when the owner's gh is signed out by then (`Sign in to GitHub first: …`). Otherwise its login is first kept for good among the reviewer accounts Oby has used (`home/github/reviewers.json`, 0600: obid only, merging), and then the folder becomes the reviewer's, `home/github/reviewer` (0700), in place of the one before. A login obid can't keep there keeps nothing either (`reviewer.error`: `obid couldn't keep the reviewer's login: …`): its approval could otherwise pass for a teammate's once it signed out.

**`DELETE /v1/github/reviewer/sign-in`** ends the reviewer's sign-in under way, if any, and signs the reviewer out: `home/github/reviewer` is removed, token and all, and `204`. Its login stays in `home/github/reviewers.json`. A sign-in that was still ending keeps nothing afterwards. obid never runs `gh auth logout` for it (it clears the keyring's shared slot, the owner's). As after `gh auth logout`, GitHub keeps the token valid until it's revoked in the reviewer account's settings (Applications › Authorized OAuth Apps › GitHub CLI).

What the reviewer's token can do and where it lives:

- It's gh's own OAuth token for the reviewer account, with the scopes gh's sign-in asks for (`repo`, `read:org`, `gist`): whatever that account may do in the repositories it can reach. Give the account write access only to the repositories Oby should approve in.
- It lives only in `home/github/reviewer/hosts.yml` (0600, in a 0700 folder): obid's home, which every job's and conversation's box hides. obid learns it as the secret `github reviewer` at start, after a sign-in and at every read of the reviewer's status (gh may refresh it), so it's taken out of every answer, log line and push like obid's other secrets, and the secret scan stops a push that holds it.
- It never reaches an agent: jobs keep their empty `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, no job's environment, prompt, file or argument has it, and the reviewer's gh is started only by obid itself, for one thing: posting Oby's review of the owner's own pull request (obid only: GitHub work, "Oby's verdict"). Every other GitHub call stays the owner's.
- None of these endpoints is on the gateway's allowlist.

### obid only: sign-in reasons

Why a sign-in didn't start, as one stable word beside the `error` of `GET` and `POST /v1/accounts/{id}/sign-in`, `POST /v1/github/sign-in` (and the reviewer's) and `POST /v1/tetrix/sign-in` (#1579, docs/contracts/sign-in.md): `{"error": "…", "reason": "notInstalled"}`. The apps show their own words for it under the row, never `error`, which can name a folder. Reasons are add-only: never renamed or removed, and an app shows its plain sentence for one it doesn't know.

| `reason` | When |
|---|---|
| `notInstalled` | the tool's CLI, or gh, isn't found by an absolute path (`503`) |
| `ownLogin` | the tool's own login, signed in with the tool itself (`409`) |
| `folderUnsafe` | the account's login file, its lock or its folder is a link or not the person's (`409`) |
| `keychainOff` | Claude Code on macOS without `claudeKeychain` (`409`) |
| `busy` | a job of the account is queued or running, it's being removed, or GitHub's sign-in is starting (`409`) |
| `didNotStart` | the login, or gh, gave no link or code, or couldn't start (`502`) |
| `codeRefused` | Claude Code didn't sign in with the code sent (`422`) |
| `badAddress` | a Tetrix URL that breaks a connection's rules, or an unknown preset (`422`) |
| `noSignIn` | that Tetrix's metadata, sign-in server or client can't be found or used (`502`) |

### obid only: repositories

A job works in a repository the configuration lists (`repos`) or in one of the GitHub repositories the owner's gh can push to ([obid only: GitHub's sign-in](#obid-only-githubs-sign-in)), so a fresh obid with no `repos` works in the owner's repositories once gh is signed in, with no file edited.

- **GitHub's list.** obid asks gh at start, every hour, and as soon as a GitHub sign-in ends signed in: `gh auth status`, then `gh api user/repos?per_page=100&sort=pushed&affiliation=owner,collaborator,organization_member --paginate`, keeping each repository whose `permissions.push` is true and that isn't archived, newest push first. The list is the signed-in account's only: when gh says it's signed out, or signed in as another account (at each read, and whenever `GET /v1/github` reads gh's status), the list is dropped at once, so no job starts in, or adds, a repository of an account gh no longer uses. A read that fails while the same account is signed in keeps the list. Each is cloned from `https://github.com/<owner>/<name>.git` with gh's login, as any GitHub remote is (obid only: publishing the owner's work).
- **A review request's repository, on the owner's tap.** A review requested of the owner in a repository neither listed nor among GitHub's above (one their login can read but not push to, say) is added only by the owner's signed Confirm (#776). A tap on its card in the feed (`POST /v1/github/work/{id}/start`) or `Review it` on its inbox offer makes a `confirm` item, `Add acme/other and review #12` (action `add_repository`), and answers `422` (obid only: GitHub work), so obid's API token alone (an agent, oby-mcp, a script) never adds one. On the Confirm, obid checks the name (`owner/name`), reads `gh repo view <repo> --json nameWithOwner,defaultBranchRef` with its own gh, and gives `{"repo", "remote": "https://github.com/<repo>.git", "defaultBranch"}`, with no `check`, to that review's job alone: no other job sees it before. The remote is built from the checked name, never taken from GitHub's answer. It's kept, as below, only once that job is made; a start that fails adds nothing. Never the brain, the event orchestrator or anything GitHub says.
- **The first job adds it.** A job in one of GitHub's (`POST /v1/jobs`, the brain, the conversation, GitHub work, a plug-in's event) adds `{"repo", "remote", "defaultBranch"}` to the configuration file's `repos`, every other key and entry kept (the file is written again as JSON through a temporary file, 0600, its keys sorted). From then on it's listed like the rest: conversations' rooms check it out, its clone is tidied, routines and rules may name it. When the file can't be written, obid logs why and lists the repository until it stops.
- **What the brain is told.** The conversation's orientation names every listed repository and up to 100 of GitHub's, newest push first; a job may start in any of GitHub's.
- **Letter case.** Names match letter case aside, as GitHub's do and the Mac's disk, where two would share one clone: a listed repository first, with its settings (its `check`), and the job takes that name; GitHub's spelling of a listed one is never added again.
- **Never for another Obi.** A review another Obi asks for (obid only: Obi-to-Obi review jobs) is taken only in a listed repository.

**`GET /v1/repos`** answers the repositories a job may start in, the listed ones first, then GitHub's not listed yet (`listed` false), newest push first:

```json
{"repos": [{"repo": "obi-e2e/fixture-app", "defaultBranch": "main", "listed": true, "check": "node --test", "noCheck": false},
           {"repo": "octo-cat/app", "defaultBranch": "dev", "listed": false, "check": null, "noCheck": false}]}
```

`check` is the command its jobs run (null: none), and `noCheck` whether the owner answered No check when Oby asked (Job, #1062). Older obids don't send them.

**`POST /v1/repos/check`** (obid only, #1062), `{"repo": "acme/app", "check": "make test"}`: the owner's own check for a repository, from Edit on Oby's ask or Change check on a task's page. It never keeps anything by itself: it asks, as Oby does, with the same `confirm` item (action `set_check`, `job` empty), in place of any open one for that repository (those are done, unanswered), and only the owner's signed Confirm of that item keeps the command. Its text says what Cancel keeps: with a check there, `acme/app's check is \`npm test\` now. With \`make test\` instead, every job there that starts after your answer runs it in its box, with no network, before anything goes out. Cancel keeps \`npm test\`.` `"check": null` starts over: a check kept there is taken out the same way, an item titled `Take \`npm test\` out as acme/app's check?` whose Confirm takes it out (the next job asks again); with none kept, the owner's No check is forgotten at once, so the next job asks again, and there's no item. Answers `200` with the repository as `GET /v1/repos` lists it and the item, `{"repo": {…}, "ask": {…} | null}`; `422` for a repository obid doesn't know, or a `check` that isn't one line of up to 200 characters with no control character. Not on the gateway: a paired phone doesn't change a check.

### obid only: Tetrix

The owner connects Tetrix once, in Oby, and every agent on every account (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor) uses it with no sign-in of its own (the owner's decision of 2026-10-18, AGENTS.md). obid is Tetrix's OAuth client and holds the login; agents reach Tetrix only through obid, with a credential that works for their own job and dies with it. None of these endpoints is on the gateway's allowlist, and no other Oby can reach them: a paired phone never signs the Mac in to Tetrix (the iPhone shows the state through the accounts' `tetrix` and the jobs' `tetrix`).

**`GET /v1/tetrix`** answers:

```json
{"connected": true, "url": "https://tetrix.example/mcp", "preset": "team",
 "presets": [{"id": "team", "name": "Our Tetrix", "url": "https://tetrix.example/mcp"}],
 "scopes": ["graph:read", "sessions:write", "hitl:write", "offline_access"],
 "connectedAt": "2026-10-18T14:02:11Z", "signIn": null, "error": null}
```

- `connected`: obid holds a login for `url` that it can use or refresh. `url` is the MCP server it signed in to (or last tried), `preset` the preset's id or null for a URL the owner typed. `presets`: what Settings offers before a URL field, `config.json`'s `tetrixPresets`; `[]` when it names none.
- `scopes`: what the authorization server granted, or null when signed out. `connectedAt`: when the sign-in finished, or null.
- `signIn`: the sign-in under way, as `POST` answers it, or null. `error`: why the last sign-in or refresh failed, in a sentence (`Tetrix signed Oby out: sign in again.`), or null; it clears on the next sign-in, or the next refresh that works. With `connected` still true, it's a refresh the sign-in server didn't answer (below): Settings › Connectors shows Tetrix as needing attention, with Reconnect.
- No token, code, verifier or client secret is ever in an answer, an event, a log line, a report or `config.json`.

**`POST /v1/tetrix/sign-in`** with `{"preset": "<id>"}` (one of `presets`) or `{"url": "https://<host>/mcp"}` starts a sign-in and answers `{"url": "https://<authorization server>/…/authorize?…", "server": "https://<authorization server>/…", "expiresAt": "…"}`. `server` is the authorization server's issuer (where the person signs in), as the MCP server's metadata named it and its own metadata confirmed; `url` is always on its host (obid refuses a server whose authorization endpoint is elsewhere, `502`). The app opens `url` in the person's browser; when they finish there, the browser lands on obid's loopback page, which says `Oby is signed in to Tetrix. You can close this tab.` The app follows it with `GET /v1/tetrix` (every 2 seconds while `signIn` isn't null) or the `tetrix` event.

- The URL follows a connection's rules (Connection, above): HTTPS, with no login, query or fragment; plain HTTP only on 127.0.0.1 or localhost. Anything else, or an unknown preset, is `422` naming what's wrong.
- How obid finds the authorization server (the MCP authorization spec): the server's protected-resource metadata (where an unauthenticated call's `401` says, `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer … resource_metadata="…"`, else `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/<path>`, then at the root) gives `authorization_servers`, `resource` and `scopes_supported`; the server's metadata is RFC 8414's (`/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server/<path>`), else OpenID discovery (`<issuer>/.well-known/openid-configuration`, then `/.well-known/openid-configuration/<path>`), whose `issuer` must be the one asked for. Redirects aren't followed. When none answers JSON, `502` says so.
- The client: a public client with PKCE (S256) and no secret. A preset may name its client id; otherwise obid registers one by dynamic client registration (`registration_endpoint`, `token_endpoint_auth_method` `none`, redirect `http://127.0.0.1/callback`) once per authorization server and keeps its id. Neither: `502`.
- The metadata must be for the server obid was given (RFC 9728 3.3): its `resource` has the URL's origin and its path, or a prefix of it at a `/`; else `502` and no sign-in. That `resource` is the resource indicator obid sends. Plain-HTTP endpoints on 127.0.0.1 are taken only when the URL itself is on 127.0.0.1. Answers from the sign-in server are read up to 256 KB.
- Scopes: the resource's `scopes_supported`, else `graph:read sessions:write hitl:write`; plus `offline_access`, so obid can refresh. The request carries `resource` (RFC 8707), the `state` and the PKCE challenge.
- The redirect is `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/callback` on a port obid opens for this sign-in alone, on 127.0.0.1 only. It answers that one path, takes the first request whose `state` matches, and then closes; a request whose `state` doesn't match gets `400` and changes nothing.
- One at a time: a second `POST` ends the one under way (and waits up to 5 seconds for its page to close, so a registered client's port is free again) and starts another. A sign-in ends when the token exchange does, after 10 minutes, with `DELETE`, or when obid stops.
- The login (access token, refresh token, expiry) goes into obid's vault as `oby.tetrix.access` and `oby.tetrix.refresh` (a connection can't name an `oby.` secret), so it lives where the vault lives (the login Keychain on the Mac app's runner home, else `home/vault/secrets.json`) and is taken out of every answer, log line and push like every vault secret (obid only: the vault). Everything else (`url`, the client id, the endpoints, the expiry, the granted scopes) is in `home/tetrix.json` (0600).
- Until a sign-in finishes, the login before it stays in use. One that finishes on another sign-in server than the login it replaces (another token endpoint: another Tetrix's) has the old one take that login back, as `DELETE /v1/tetrix` does (its `revocation_endpoint`, the refresh token), in the background once the new login is kept; a failure there is only logged. On the same sign-in server nothing is taken back: there the old and new logins can share one session (Keycloak's client session), and revoking the old would end the new.
- obid refreshes the access token itself when it has less than a minute left, or when Tetrix answers `401`, and keeps the rotated refresh token. A refresh Tetrix refuses (`invalid_grant`) signs Oby out (`error` says so). One the sign-in server doesn't answer (it can't be reached, or answers 5xx) while the token can't be used (it ended, or Tetrix answered `401`) keeps the login, fails that call, and says so in `error` (`Oby couldn't refresh its Tetrix login: Tetrix's sign-in server answered HTTP 503. It tries again at the next call.`); the next call refreshes again.

Its `422`s carry `reason` `badAddress` and its `502`s `noSignIn`. A sign-in that ended without a login shows as `connected` false and `signIn` null.

**`DELETE /v1/tetrix/sign-in`** ends the sign-in under way, if any: `204`.

**`DELETE /v1/tetrix`** signs Oby out: it asks the authorization server to revoke the refresh token when it offers `revocation_endpoint` (and goes on if that fails), removes both vault secrets, and keeps `url` and `preset`: `204`. Jobs that are running lose Tetrix at their next call (`Oby isn't signed in to Tetrix.`).

**The `tetrix` event** comes on `/v1/events` with the same body as `GET /v1/tetrix` whenever any of its fields change. The gateway leaves it out of a phone's event stream.

**What agents get.** While Oby is signed in, every owner's job's agent gets one MCP server named `tetrix` that is obid itself: `POST /v1/agent/tetrix/{job}` on obid's port (on Linux, through slirp4netns's 10.0.2.2, as for the broker), with the job's token (the broker's: Connection, above), which works for that job alone, only while it runs, and dies with it. Claude Code gets it in its `--mcp-config` file and one `--allowedTools` rule per tool (`mcp__tetrix__<tool>`); Codex as `mcp_servers.tetrix` with the token in its environment (`bearer_token_env_var`, as the broker); Cursor in `session/new`'s `mcpServers` when it says `mcpCapabilities.http`. The agent never sees Oby's Tetrix token, and Tetrix sees obid's token, never the job's.

- `tools/list` lists only the tools agents may call (`TOOLS` in core/crates/obid/src/tetrix.rs: the graph reads, the earlier sessions' digests and `save_session`); `tools/call` of any other (`resume_session`, admin, write and diagnostics tools) is refused before it leaves obid. Every call's arguments go through the secret scan first (`crate::scan`, as a push's do, in obid's own `home/tmp`, never the job's folder, which its agent can write): a finding, or a scan that can't run, is refused (`Oby didn't send this to Tetrix: the secret scan found something in it.`) and nothing leaves. Every result is scrubbed like a connection's.
- One Tetrix error never takes the tools away. obid keeps a job's listing only once Tetrix gave it whole: one that failed, or that a timeout cut off (a context question's 10 seconds), is asked again, in a new MCP session, at the job's next call. An agent lists its tools once, as it starts, so while Tetrix doesn't answer `tools/list` gets Tetrix's last listing for any job (else the allowed tools' names alone, with an open `inputSchema`), never an error or none. Each call tries Tetrix again (refreshing the login when it ended); one that can't reach it answers `isError` with `Oby couldn't reach Tetrix: <why> Ask again later: each call tries Tetrix again.`
- A session the agent saves (`save_session`'s answer) is listed in the job's `outgoing` as `{"action": "tetrixSession", "repo", "id": "<session id>"}`.
- Each call is recorded in the job's `connectionUses` as connection `tetrix`, secret `oby.tetrix.access`, host Tetrix's (never a value), and counted in the job's `tetrix`.
- Without Oby signed in to Tetrix, agents get no Tetrix: the plug-in that gave a Claude Code agent Tetrix on its tool's own login went with plug-ins (2026-10-27), and obid logs how to sign in when `home/plugins.json` still has it on.
- Reviews another Obi asked for get no Tetrix.
- **A conversation's turn** (obid only: the conversation harness) gets the same server while Oby is signed in, at `POST /v1/agent/tetrix/conversation-<id>` with its turn's token (the one its `oby` server takes, good only while one of its turns is under way): Claude Code in its `--mcp-config` file, Codex as `mcp_servers.tetrix`. Signed out, its session has no `tetrix` server, and its orientation says nothing of Tetrix. It lists and calls the reads only: `TOOLS` but `save_session` (a conversation keeps no `outgoing`; writing anywhere is a job's), and any other call is refused before it leaves obid. The same secret scan and scrubbing; every answer comes between the turn's fences with the line that says it's data, and a call that leaves obid makes the turn count as having read something untrusted (its jobs start marked `untrusted`, and the conversation keeps none of the owner's declared secrets). A Claude Code waiting for its next turn may `initialize`, `ping` and `tools/list` with its token; a `tools/call` answers a JSON-RPC error (`-32600`), and one from a turn that was stopped answers `isError` (`The turn was stopped: nothing more happens for it.`) and leaves nothing. A turn nobody watches (obid only: a stalled job goes back to its conversation) gets no `tetrix` server and no word of Tetrix in its orientation, and its token is refused there (`401`). Whether a turn gets Tetrix is decided once, as it starts, for both its server and its orientation. Oby's Tetrix login never reaches the turn. Each call goes in obid's connection-use log with job `conversation-<id>` (there's no job to list it on), and every conversation's calls share one upstream session with Tetrix.

**Context first.** Before a job's agent starts (its first turn, and the first after obid restarted it), obid asks Tetrix with its own login about the job: `search_entities` for the issue or pull request (`<repo>#<n>`, else the job's title) and `repo_sessions` for the repository, 10 seconds at most each, each through the secret scan first (one it doesn't pass isn't asked). After two questions in a row that can't reach Tetrix, the rest aren't asked, and the agent starts. What comes back is written to `.obi/context/tetrix.md` with the plug-ins' context (obid only: plug-ins), quoted as data, and the prompt says to start there, to ask Tetrix before reading many files, and to end with `save_session` and a `Tetrix-Session: <id>` trailer on every commit.

**Decisions before publishing** (Trail-Code's `trail check`, the idea only). When a job's check has passed and before it publishes, obid reads what the change does in its own clone, as the secret scan reads it (`git diff -U0` with the scan's options and configuration, never the worktree's git), and keeps the lines whose only change is a number (`retries = 4` to `retries = 6`), with the name it's assigned to (`MAX_RETRIES`, `"retries"`, `retry(4)`). A number inside a name (`u32`), a reference (`#12`, `main.rs:40`), a date or time (`2026-10-18`, `10:30`) or a version's later parts isn't one, and lock files and other generated files (`*.lock`, `*-lock.json`, `go.sum`, `*.min.*`, `vendor/`, `node_modules/`, `dist/`, …) are left out. It reads the first 8 MB of the diff, the first 10 files with such a change, and at most 200 changes in them. With none, Tetrix isn't asked.

Otherwise obid asks Tetrix with its own login, each question through the secret scan first (one it doesn't pass isn't sent), 10 seconds at most each and 30 seconds in all, and a stop ends it at once. The questions, as Tetrix's tools take them (deskree-inc/tetrix-collectors, `mcp_service/app/tools/`):
- `repo_sessions` with `{"name": "<owner>/<name>"}`: the sessions on the repository, `sessions[].session_id` (graph ids).
- `session_context` with `{"session": "<graph id>"}`, for the first 10 sessions of the first page. Tetrix orders them by edge type (`produced`, `references`, `worked_on`), then title, never by date, and which ones changed the files is only known from each one's digest, so on a repository with more than 10 sessions the rest aren't read and an older decision can be missed. It gives each one's `natural_key`, `session:<owner>/<uuid>`. One whose `digest_summary.decisions` is 0, or whose uuid is one of the job's own (`saved`, and the graph gaps obid saved for it), is skipped.
- `session_digest` with `{"session": "<natural key>", "format": "structured"}`: its `digest`. It is kept, at most 5, when its `projects[]` for this repository list one of the changed files in `paths_modified`. Its `decisions[].statement`s are read, not the `reverted` ones.

A decision that names the old number and not the new one, and a word of the assigned name (`retries` for `MAX_RETRIES`; the same stem, never a prefix), is a **conflict** (at most 20, one per line).

- **The author's round.** The first time there's a conflict, the author gets one more round, as for a push the secret scan blocked (even with no rounds left). Each conflict and its decision are quoted in `.obi/inputs/decisions.md`, fenced as data from Tetrix. The round's message names only the file, the line, the two numbers of the agent's own diff and that file, and tells it to put the value back and commit, or keep it and say why in its summary, which goes into the pull request's description. The job's step reads `Round 2 of 2: a change goes against an earlier decision`. Its check runs again after it, and the decision check once more. A job had its round once `decisions.asked` is true, after a restart too.
- **Never a block.** What's still a conflict after that round (or, when the check after it runs out of time, what the first one found) publishes all the same, listed in the pull request's description: ``- Earlier decisions: this changes 1 value(s) an earlier Tetrix session decided on: ` src/net.rs:12 ` (4 → 6), session 5b0e…. The agent's summary says why.`` The decision's words aren't in it. No second round.
- **On the job**, in its `tetrix`: `"decisions": {"sessions": ["5b0e…"], "conflicts": [{"file": "src/net.rs", "line": 12, "was": "4", "now": "6", "session": "5b0e…", "decision": "Keep retries at 4: …"}], "asked": true, "error": null}`, from the latest check:
  - `sessions`: the sessions whose decisions were read;
  - `conflicts`: what was found (`decision` at most 300 characters, scrubbed like every answer);
  - `asked`: whether the author got its round;
  - `error`: `Tetrix didn't answer within 30 seconds: nothing was checked.` when it ran out of time, else null.

  The whole field is null when the check didn't run.
- **Without Tetrix** (Oby not signed in, or the job's agent didn't get it through Oby) nothing changes. Questions to Tetrix aren't outgoing actions; the pull request that lists the conflicts is.
- What it doesn't catch yet: an approach an earlier session ruled out coming back, or a decision changed in other words. Those need a reviewer reading the digests whole: obid's own review (#525) doesn't read them yet.

**Jobs' `tetrix`** (Job, above) on obid is filled for jobs whose agent had Tetrix through Oby, and stays null otherwise:

```json
{"connected": true, "calls": {"search_entities": 2, "get_entity": 1, "save_session": 1}, "rounds": null,
 "reminded": null, "stamps": {"commits": 2, "stamped": 2, "sessions": ["8f2a…"]}, "author": null,
 "context": true, "saved": ["8f2a…"],
 "gaps": {"noResults": ["acme/shop#12"], "missing": ["repo:acme/legacy"], "unknownFiles": ["src/new.rs"],
          "failed": [{"tool": "code_impact", "error": "Tetrix said: …"}],
          "sent": "2026-10-18T14:40:02Z", "session": "c41d…", "notSent": null},
 "decisions": null, "unreachable": null}
```

- `calls`: every call the agent made through obid, by tool, counted by obid itself (it is the server). `rounds` and `reminded` are null on obid. `stamps`: as above, read when the job's last round ends. `context`: whether `.obi/context/tetrix.md` was written. `saved`: the session ids Tetrix's `save_session` answered the agent.
- `unreachable`: while Tetrix couldn't be reached the last time the job tried (the agent's listing or call, or obid's context questions), in plain words: `Tetrix wasn't reachable, so this job ran without the graph: Tetrix's sign-in server answered HTTP 503.`, or `Tetrix stopped answering during this job: <why>.` when it had answered the job before. An error Tetrix itself gave (`Tetrix said: …`, a tool it doesn't have) isn't one. Null once Tetrix answers again. The summary's Tetrix line says it too, after the calls.
- `gaps`, the report back to Tetrix of where the graph fell short (null until the job ends): `noResults`, searches (`search_entities`, `query_graph`) that found nothing, by their query; `missing`, entities `get_entity` (or another lookup) didn't find, by what was asked; `unknownFiles`, files the job changed that no Tetrix answer named; `failed`, calls that failed, with the error's first 200 characters. At most 20 of each, each at most 200 characters.
- When the job ends with any gap, obid sends them to Tetrix with its own login as a `save_session` of its own (`client` `oby`, `title` `Oby: graph gaps in <job title>`, `repo_hint` the repository, `summary` the counts, `state` `{"kind": "oby.graphGaps/1", "job": "<id>", …the four lists, "agentSessions": […]}`), after the secret scan passes on exactly what goes; a finding, or a scan that can't run, sends nothing (`notSent` says why). `sent` and `session` say when and as which Tetrix session; the send is listed in the job's `outgoing` as `{"action": "tetrixGaps", "repo", "id": "<session id>"}`.
- The summary's last line (not on review jobs) reads as above, followed by `Graph gaps sent to Tetrix: 1 search with no result, 1 file it didn't know.` when there were some.

**Accounts' `tetrix`** (Account, above) on obid is `true` for every Claude Code, Codex and Cursor account while Oby is signed in to Tetrix, else null; `tetrixJobs` and `tetrixUsed` count the account's latest 50 ended jobs with a `tetrix`, and those whose agent called it.

### obid only: the gateway

obid serves the LAN gateway's pairing and forwarding (LAN gateway (#284), above) as the Swift runner does, with these differences (`core/crates/obid/src/gateway.rs`, #446):

- **Where it listens.** Only where the configuration's `gateway` says, an address on `127.0.0.1` like `127.0.0.1:8810`; with no `gateway` there's none. Any other address is refused when obid starts (`gateway is "0.0.0.0:8810": it must be an address on 127.0.0.1 with a port…`), and a busy one doesn't stop obid: it serves the API without a gateway, logs `no gateway: can't listen on 127.0.0.1:8810: …`, and `GET /v1/status` says `"gateway": {"state": "down", "address": "127.0.0.1:8810", "detail": "can't listen on 127.0.0.1:8810: Address already in use (os error 48)"}` until it starts again (`{"state": "listening", "address": …}` while it listens; the key is left out with no `gateway` configured). Once it listens, obid prints a second line on stdout after its first, `obid gateway listening on http://127.0.0.1:8810`. It isn't advertised over Bonjour: phones reach it through the owner's tailnet, with `tailscale serve --bg --https=8443 http://127.0.0.1:8810` (never `tailscale funnel`, which would put it on the internet), and pair at `https://<the Mac's tailnet name>:8443`.
- **Invitations** come from `POST /v1/pairing/invite` on obid's API with its API token, as above. The Mac app adds `"approval": {"device", "signedAt", "signature"}`, its registered key's signature (ECDSA P-256 with SHA-256, DER, base64) over `"oby-pair-invite\n<signedAt>"`, within 2 minutes, once, made on the owner's click: only the phone such an invitation pairs may add its publishing key (`POST /v1/devices`). One without an `approval` pairs a phone that can read and answer through the gateway but approves nothing; an `approval` that doesn't hold is `403` and makes nothing. `name` defaults to the machine's name and `hostname` to the `url`'s host and port. A Tailscale hostname is a link's `hostname` as it is, port included (`mac.tail0000.ts.net:8443`). While obid is a member of an Obysphere, every invitation is one through it too, and `url` is optional ([the phone relay](#obid-only-the-phone-relay), below); with no Obysphere, an invitation needs a `url`, and without one it's `409` (`Join or host an Obysphere first (Settings › Obysphere).`).
- **Paired devices** are kept, by their token's SHA-256 digest only, in `home/gateway-devices.json` (0600), not in `devices.json`: a phone paired with the Swift runner pairs again with obid.
- **Forwarding** happens inside obid: an allowed request with a paired device's token goes to obid's own API router with obid's API token and a loopback `Host`, carrying only its method, path, query, body (at most 4 MiB) and `Content-Type`. The answer has passed through the API's own layers, so the vault's secrets are already out; the gateway then nulls `session` and puts `[home]` for the accounts' `configDir` paths and for `home/logins` (where a job's copy of its login lives), as above, and sends only the status, `Content-Type`, `Cache-Control`, and an artifact's `Content-Security-Policy`, `X-Content-Type-Options` and `Referrer-Policy`. An answer that isn't text (not `text/…`, JSON or `…+json`: an artifact's image or PDF) goes byte for byte, as the API's own layer sends it. An answer of known length goes whole; any other (the event stream, `GET /v1/jobs/{id}/wait`) goes chunk by chunk as obid sends it, so a wait's first space reaches the phone at once. A stream ends at its first chunk after its device is revoked.
- **Redeeming** takes only what a phone sends: a request with an `Origin` (a browser) is `403` and one that isn't `application/json` is `415`, before it counts as a try, so a web page can't use an invitation up. A device's name keeps no control characters.
- **The allowlist** is Runner/Routes.swift's, plus `GET /v1/conversations`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /v1/conversations/{id}` (obid only: conversations), `GET /v1/artifacts`, `GET /v1/artifacts/{id}` and `GET /v1/artifacts/{id}/{version}/{path}` at any depth (obid only: artifacts), `GET /v1/jobs/{id}/report`, `GET /v1/jobs/{id}/transcript`, `GET /v1/jobs/{id}/transcript/{n}`, `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/proposals/{index}/file`, `GET /v1/github/work`, `POST /v1/github/work/refresh` and `POST /v1/github/work/{id}/start` (obid only: GitHub work), and `GET /v1/repos` (obid only: repositories). Routes obid doesn't serve yet answer `404` through the gateway as they do on loopback.

### obid only: the phone relay

A phone reaches obid through its owner's own Obysphere (the obi-sphere spec, `sphere-api.md` section 9), so the Mac opens no port and needs no tunnel: the Sphere passes frames between them, and every frame is an envelope the phone and obid seal end to end (#1277; `core/crates/obid/src/relay/`). Deskree's Obysphere relays for its members only; a Sphere anyone hosts works the same way.

- **When.** While obid is a member of an Obysphere (`GET /v1/obid/sphere` says `joined`) and at least one phone is paired through it or invited to, obid keeps one WebSocket on `GET <the Sphere's URL>/v0.1/relay/{relay}` with its member Obi's access token, and makes the Sphere's list of that relay's devices the paired ones: when the socket opens, when a phone is invited, paired or forgotten, and every minute (an invitation or a device whose time is up is taken off). After a drop it opens again after 1 second, doubling to 60, or when the Sphere's `Retry-After` says. 45 seconds before its token ends it opens a new socket with a new token, which replaces the old one (the Sphere closes that one with `4003`) and the phones see nothing: first a `pong` on the old socket says the Sphere has had all obid sent on it, and what the old socket still holds is read before anything on the new one, so nothing is lost or reordered either way. Once obid leaves its Sphere, or learns its membership was revoked, the socket ends. It sends `ping` every 30 seconds, and keeps under 4,000 frames and 56 MiB in any minute, below the Sphere's limits. It opens no listener for this: what comes in goes to the gateway's router inside obid.
- **What obid keeps.** `home/keys/relay.json` (0600): the relay's id (32 random bytes, base64url) and the Mac's X25519 private key, made the first time a phone is invited through a Sphere. The key never leaves obid: no answer, event or log has it. In `home/gateway-devices.json`, a device paired through the relay has `relay`, its relay token's SHA-256 (hex). The relay token itself is only in the QR code and on the phone.
- **Inviting.** While obid is a member of an Obysphere, `POST /v1/pairing/invite` makes a relay token for the phone (32 random bytes, base64url: 43 characters), and registers the relay and the token's SHA-256 with the Sphere (`PUT /v0.1/relay/{relay}`, then `PUT /v0.1/relay/{relay}/devices/{hex}`) before it answers, so the phone's first call can come through. The answer has everything the gateway's has, plus `relay`:

  ```json
  {"url": "https://obysphere.deskree.com", "name": "Studio", "hostname": "studio.local", "secret": "…", "code": "123456",
   "expiresAt": "2026-10-23T21:02:00Z",
   "relay": {"sphere": "https://obysphere.deskree.com", "id": "<relay id>", "token": "<the phone's relay token>", "key": "<the Mac's X25519 public key, base64url>"},
   "link": "{\"hostname\":\"studio.local\",\"name\":\"Studio\",\"relay\":{…the same…}}"}
  ```

  `link` carries `relay` too, so the QR code the Mac already makes from the answer (`{"url", "link", "secret"}`) has it as it is. Such an invitation pairs only through the Sphere: its `url` is the Sphere's (a `url` asked for is left out) and `hostname` the machine's name unless given, and its secret and code are refused on the gateway's listener (`401`, a wrong try), so a phone can't fall back to plain HTTP. `504` when the Sphere can't be reached, `502` when it refuses (`Your Obysphere relays 16 phones for this Mac at most: revoke one first.` at its limit); nothing is invited then. An owner with no Obysphere pairs as before, through Remote access or the tailnet, with a `url`; once obid is a member of one, every phone pairs through it.
- **Pairing through it.** The phone opens `GET <sphere>/v0.1/relay/{id}` with `Authorization: Bearer <relay token>`, starts a session (the envelope, below) with the QR's `key`, and sends `POST /v1/pairing` sealed in it: `{"credential": "<the secret>", "name": "<the phone's name>"}` with `content-type: application/json`. The answer is the gateway's. The device it pairs is bound to that relay token: an invitation made with a relay token is redeemed through the relay only on that token's socket (any other is `401`, and counts as a wrong try).
- **Requests.** Every other request is sealed the same way, with `authorization: Bearer <device token>`, and goes to the gateway's router in process: the same allowlist, device tokens, scrubbing and streams as on the listener; `POST /v1/hooks/{id}/deliver` is `404` through the relay. A device paired through the relay is reached only so: its token on the gateway's listener is `401` (`This phone reaches obid sealed, through its Obysphere.`), and inside another phone's envelopes `401` (`That device token isn't this phone's.`): which phone sent a request is its device token, sealed, and that device's relay token must be the socket's; the device id the Sphere routes by is never trusted for it. At most 32 requests open at once per phone (`429`); its requests' bodies at most 4 MiB until each is whole (`413`). Each phone's answers wait in a lane of their own, at most 64 frames (a refusal past that is dropped), and the lanes take turns, so one phone's download doesn't hold up another's events. A hello from a device the Sphere wasn't told of (forgotten, or made up) starts nothing, and a phone's new hello within a second of its session's start is ignored.
- **Forgetting.** `DELETE /v1/pairing/devices/{id}` ends that phone's session at once (its event stream too), and deletes its token's SHA-256 at the Sphere, which closes its socket (`4001`): from then on its relay token opens nothing.

#### The envelope (version 1)

Each WebSocket binary message between the phone and the Sphere is one envelope, at most 262,144 bytes; obid gets it after the 32 bytes of the phone's device id and sends its own after them (`sphere-api.md` 9.3). X25519 is RFC 7748, HKDF is HKDF-SHA256 (RFC 5869) with 32 bytes out, and seal is ChaCha20-Poly1305 (RFC 8439) with its 16-byte tag after the ciphertext. `‖` joins bytes; `nonce(n)` is 4 zero bytes and then `n` as 8 bytes, big-endian. In CryptoKit: `Curve25519.KeyAgreement`, `HKDF<SHA256>.deriveKey(inputKeyMaterial:salt:info:outputByteCount: 32)`, `ChaChaPoly.seal(_:using:nonce:authenticating:)`.

| Envelope | Bytes |
|---|---|
| hello, phone to Mac | `01 01` ‖ E ‖ seal(k_hello, nonce(0), aad: `01 01` ‖ E, the versions offered) |
| welcome, Mac to phone | `v 02` ‖ M ‖ seal(k_welcome, nonce(0), aad: `v 02` ‖ M, `v`) |
| sealed, either way | `v 03` ‖ n ‖ seal(k, nonce(n), aad: `v 03` ‖ n, a frame) |

- **Keys.** S is the Mac's public key, the QR's `key`, pinned at pairing. E is a new X25519 public key the phone makes for each session, M one the Mac makes for its welcome. es = X25519(the phone's new private key, S) = X25519(the Mac's private key, E); ee = X25519(the phone's new private key, M); either all zero is refused. k_hello = HKDF(salt: S ‖ E, ikm: es, info: `oby relay v1 hello`). The rest are HKDF(salt: SHA-256(the hello as sent ‖ M), ikm: es ‖ ee, info): k_welcome with `oby relay v1 welcome`, the phone's key with `oby relay v1 phone>mac` and the Mac's with `oby relay v1 mac>phone`.
- **Versions.** The hello seals one byte per version the phone speaks (1 to 16); this obid speaks `01`. The Mac picks the highest it speaks as `v`, in the welcome's header and sealed in it. The phone refuses a welcome with a `v` it didn't offer, or one that doesn't open (not from the Mac with the pinned key). Every sealed envelope carries `v` under its tag, and one with another `v` than its session's doesn't open: no downgrade, and nothing unsealed is ever taken from a phone paired this way.
- **Counters.** `n` is 8 bytes, big-endian: each side's first sealed envelope is 0, then 1, 2…, and each side takes exactly the next one, every time. One already taken (replayed, older) and one that doesn't open (changed, made up, sent back the other way, another session's) are dropped and spend nothing. One that opens with an `n` past the next proves an envelope before it is missing: nothing is skipped silently, and the session is over. When obid sees that, it sends the phone, sealed in that session, a `reset` on stream 0 (`The session is over: send a new hello.`) and keeps nothing of the session; when the phone sees it in obid's envelopes, or gets that `reset`, it sends a new hello. A new hello from the phone replaces its session: nothing of the old one opens in the new one. obid answers nothing to a hello or an envelope it can't open; a phone whose request goes unanswered for long sends a new hello, on a new socket if it must.
- **Frames.** Stream 0 is the session's own: only obid's `reset` that ends it. What a sealed envelope holds is a frame of the job channel's shape (obi-sphere `protocol.md` section 13): a stream (4 bytes, big-endian), a kind (one byte: `0` open, `1` data, `2` end, `3` reset) and its payload. A request opens a new stream, whose id is above every earlier one in the session (the first is 1), with `{"method", "path", "headers"}` (the path with its query; header names lower case), then its body in `data` frames and an `end`. obid answers on the same stream: `open` with `{"status", "headers"}` (the gateway's: `content-type`, `cache-control`, an artifact's `content-security-policy`…), the body in `data` frames of at most 65,536 bytes (an event stream's as its events come), then `end`, or `reset` with one line when the answer broke off. The phone sends `reset` on a stream to stop its answer (an event stream it no longer shows).
- **Known answer.** With the Mac's private key the bytes 0 to 31 (`00 01 … 1f`), the phone's new private key 32 to 63 and the Mac's 64 to 95, a hello offering version 1, then the phone's request on stream 1 (`open`, `{"method":"GET","path":"/v1/status","headers":{"authorization":"Bearer test-device-token"}}`, as its first sealed envelope) and the Mac's answer (`open` `{"status":200,"headers":{"content-type":"application/json"}}`, `data` `{"ok":true}`, `end`), every value in hex. obid's `relay::envelope` tests give exactly these, and CryptoKit on macOS 26 makes the same hello and request and opens the welcome and the answer.
  - S: `8f40c5adb68f25624ae5b214ea767a6ec94d829d3d7b5e1ad1ba6f3e2138285f`
  - hello: `0101358072d6365880d1aeea329adf9121383851ed21a28e3b75e965d0d2cd166254a2b39ce6bc8467ffdd094cfa4ea2258b59`
  - welcome: `010279a631eede1bf9c98f12032cdeadd0e7a079398fc786b88cc846ec89af85a51a0944396b30ca3b9834e5d232b1f803e05b`
  - the request: `01030000000000000000f5727141453120bc5930b45fc6041dd63ff38849669a6597b32e0126a0a47e5b8fe24aa92a12c8cb0b0d802d00b9b406da400b17143ce5eb2c2ec1d382aea99104e3927783310d114581bd85e058d1ebfe3852a2b3daf14baac186d5bb8a9459923c8d5f80fc14bc380ab04ace824f10`
  - the answer's `open`, `data` and `end`: `010300000000000000000a35fcb1ed7cde7ec716ca66cb4bfe2f07395d39e87ad3465de5430d568548a6ff91920033e8ba5f0286bafa8e7381285da738f3788c0b3889dd22cf8b188f542334f708ec77c3267f435ac2e4c58b4d64`, `0103000000000000000104872ef5d6738d2ec970fec24a408375a3ad546947fe63f62b4aac3868fe9137`, `010300000000000000020715d08c652dc9cad1f99f67006dbe715414875ed5`

#### What the Sphere sees

- **The Sphere** sees the relay's id and its Mac (the member's Sphere and Obi id), each phone's relay token (in its socket's `Authorization`; it keeps only the SHA-256), and for each frame its size, its time and which phone it comes from or goes to. It never sees a request, an answer, an event, a device token, the pairing secret, the Mac's public key or a login: those are only in the QR code and inside the envelopes. Dropping or changing an envelope ends that phone's session (it starts over); it never goes unnoticed.
- **Someone with a phone's relay token alone** (the Sphere itself, or whoever copied the token) can open a socket as that phone, which closes the phone's own (`4003`) until its person opens it again, send frames obid drops, use up that phone's share of the Sphere's limits, and see the size and timing of what obid sends to that socket. They can't read or change anything, act as the phone (that needs its device token, which never leaves an envelope), pair (that needs the QR code's one-time secret within its two minutes), or reach any other phone. Forgetting the phone ends all of it.
- **The Mac's private key** (`home/keys/relay.json`), if it were stolen, would let its holder answer as the Mac to new sessions; sessions recorded before stay closed to it (ee: each session's keys die with it), and a hello holds only the versions it offers.

### obid only: GitHub work
