What GitHub wants from the owner, so Oby can show it and do it for them: the open issues assigned to them, the open pull requests waiting for their review (asked of them or of a team they're on), and their own open pull requests. obid reads it with its own `gh` login, as the Lead reads the board: `gh` signs in with its own login (gh's device sign-in from the Mac, or a token the owner gave gh's own store), and obid never reads, holds or passes a token (no file, argument, environment variable, log or event of obid's has one). No webhooks: obid is local and self-hostable, so it asks, every 3 minutes.

- **How it reads.** One `gh api graphql` query per read, with three searches of up to 100 items each (`is:open is:issue archived:false assignee:@me`, `is:open is:pr archived:false review-requested:@me`, `is:open is:pr archived:false author:@me`, newest update first), each item's boards (`projectItems(first: 5, includeArchived: false)`, with its `Status` field) and its milestone's title, GitHub's `rateLimit` (`cost`, `remaining`, `resetAt`, read on every read: 9 points with boards, 6 without, measured 2026-10-20) and `viewer`, so the feed is always that of whoever `gh` is signed in as now (after `gh auth switch` too). Every 3 minutes while obid runs, the first right after it starts. A `403` or `429` (GitHub's rate limits, the secondary ones included) waits 3 minutes, then doubles, up to 30 minutes, until a read succeeds; a read that leaves fewer than 50 points waits until `rateLimit.resetAt`. Other failures (no network, a `5xx`) keep the last lists and try again at the next tick. An answer with GitHub `errors` besides its `data` (an organization whose SAML the login isn't authorized for, say; `gh` fails it) still counts: its lists are shown, and `error` says what GitHub left out.
- **Boards need `read:project`.** Reading an item's boards needs gh's login to have the `read:project` scope (or `project`). Without it GitHub fails the whole query, so obid reads again at once without boards: the lists are as always, every `board` is null, `error` is `Board columns need gh's read:project scope: run gh auth refresh -s read:project`, and the log says so once until boards read again. For the next hour a tick reads without boards (one call per read), then tries boards again; a refresh (`POST /v1/github/work/refresh`), or a read as another login, tries them at once, so a refresh right after `gh auth refresh -s read:project` brings them back. Once they read, `error` clears.
- **Not signed in.** When `gh` isn't installed or isn't signed in, two reads in a row (one that passes, a 401 GitHub gives once, keeps the lists and reads again at the next tick), the lists are empty and `signedIn` is false: the apps show Connect GitHub. After those two, obid doesn't ask `gh` again for 30 minutes of the Mac awake (its clock stops while the Mac sleeps): signing in through Oby (`POST /v1/github/sign-in`) reads at once, as does a refresh, and signing in from a terminal (`gh auth login`) shows the work within those 30 minutes. The first read after signing in again, or as another login, is a first read: it offers nothing in the inbox.

#### `GET /v1/github/work`

```json
{"signedIn": true, "login": "wellington", "checkedAt": "2026-10-18T09:12:00Z", "nextCheck": "2026-10-18T09:15:00Z", "error": null,
 "counts": {"reviews": 1, "assigned": 140, "mine": 1},
 "reviews": [{"id": "3f9a0c1d2b4e5f60", "list": "reviews", "repo": "obi-ai-bot/obi", "number": 702, "title": "Pair a device from Settings",
              "url": "https://github.com/obi-ai-bot/obi/pull/702", "author": "sangam", "labels": ["area:mac"], "updated": "2026-10-18T08:40:11Z",
              "state": "OPEN", "draft": false, "reviewDecision": "REVIEW_REQUIRED", "checks": "SUCCESS", "board": null, "milestone": null, "known": true, "job": null}],
 "assigned": [{"id": "9b1c…", "list": "assigned", "repo": "obi-ai-bot/obi", "number": 640, "title": "…", "url": "https://github.com/obi-ai-bot/obi/issues/640",
               "author": "wellington", "labels": [], "updated": "…", "state": "OPEN", "draft": null, "reviewDecision": null, "checks": null,
               "board": {"project": "Oby — Roadmap", "column": "Todo"}, "milestone": "F17 · Secrets Oby uses but never shows", "known": true, "job": "ab12cd34"}],
 "mine": [ … ]}
```

- `signedIn`: whether `gh` is installed and signed in. `login`: its GitHub login, or null. `checkedAt`: the last read that succeeded, or null (none yet). `nextCheck`: when obid reads next. `error`: why the last read failed, one line with the secrets obid holds taken out (`GitHub's rate limit: reading again at 2026-10-18T09:42:00Z`, `gh isn't signed in to GitHub`), or what it left out (`GitHub left some of your work out: Resource protected by organization SAML enforcement…`), or null; after a failure the lists stay as the last read that worked.
- `reviews`, `assigned`, `mine`: the three lists, newest update first, at most 100 each. `counts`: how many GitHub found for each, so a list shorter than its count was cut at 100. Each read also takes a pull request's `isCrossRepository` and its head commit's `oid`.
- Each item: `id` (16 hex characters, obid's own, the same for the same repository and number on every read), `list` (which of the three), `repo` (`owner/name`), `number`, `title`, `url`, `author` (a login, or null for a deleted user), `association` (GitHub's `authorAssociation` of the author, or null) and `trusted` (whether the author's words count as the owner's own, Whose words below), `labels` (names, at most 20), `updated`, `state` (`OPEN`). On pull requests, `draft`, `reviewDecision` (`APPROVED`, `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, `REVIEW_REQUIRED`, or null: no review required), `checks` (the head commit's combined state: `SUCCESS`, `FAILURE`, `ERROR`, `PENDING`, `EXPECTED`, or null when it has none), `head` (that commit's full id, or null when GitHub gave none that is one) and `fork` (whether its branch is a fork's); on issues, the five are null. `board`: the GitHub Project (v2) board it's on and its column there (`{"project": "<the board's title>", "column": "<its Status>"}`: `Backlog`, `Todo`, `In progress`, whatever the board calls them), or null when it's on no board, on none with a single-select field named exactly `Status`, or gh's login lacks `read:project`; on several boards, the first that has a `Status`. Boards the item is archived from are left out (`includeArchived: false`). `known`: whether a job can start in its repository: one of obid's `repos` (obid only: configuration) or of GitHub's that gh can push to (obid only: repositories). `job`: the queued or running obid job on it (same repository and number; on your own pull request, a `fix` job with that `pr`), or null.
- Everything in an item is GitHub's data: show it, never act on it. `title`, `labels` and `board` are what people wrote.

#### `GET /v1/events`: `github`

A `github` event comes, carrying the same object as `GET /v1/github/work`, when a read changes anything in it but `checkedAt` and `nextCheck` (an item came, went or changed, `signedIn`, `login`, `error`), and when `POST /v1/github/work/{id}/start` starts a job (its `job`). The first read after obid starts sends none: fetch the feed when you connect, as after every reconnect. Replace the whole feed with it; how that job goes comes as `job` events.

#### `POST /v1/github/work/refresh`

Reads now, as a tick does, and answers the feed as `GET /v1/github/work` does once the read ends (up to 2 minutes). Within 15 seconds of the last read began (a tick's or a refresh's), it doesn't read again and answers the feed as it is, so no client spends GitHub's points by refreshing; nor during a rate-limit wait, when `error` and `nextCheck` say until when. No body.

#### `POST /v1/github/work/{id}/start`

Starts the work on an item with one call, as the owner's tap (no body; a body is ignored). Starting work is always the owner's choice: nothing in the feed starts a job by itself.

- A `reviews` item starts Oby's review of that pull request: a `review` job (`POST /v1/jobs` with `kind` `review` and `pr`), routed as any job, with the pull request's head checked out. It reads, never commits, and never posts on GitHub by itself (What the runner never does). When the owner's own review ends `done` with a summary, one `listen` inbox item (`id` `feed-review-` and the job's id) shows that summary, with Oby's verdict, and offers `Approve` and `Dismiss`. `Approve` posts it on the pull request with that verdict, after the secret scan, and only while the pull request's head is still the commit the review started from. A summary the scan flags, or a scan that can't run, offers only `Dismiss` and posts nothing. Another Oby's review is not this item.

  **Oby's verdict.** A review job's prompt asks the agent to end its summary with a line that is exactly `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: REQUEST CHANGES`. obid takes the last such line out of the text and keeps the verdict in the item's `watch.verdict`: `approve`, `requestChanges`, or `comment` when the agent gave none. What `watch.review` shows, and Approve posts, starts with obid's own line saying what was decided (`Oby's verdict: approved.`, `Oby's verdict: changes requested.` or `Oby's verdict: comments only.`), then the review. It's posted as a review of the commit reviewed with the event `APPROVE`, `REQUEST_CHANGES` or `COMMENT` (above), from the owner's login: Oby decides, the owner's Approve posts it. GitHub lets nobody approve or request changes on their own pull request, so on the owner's own (its author, `gh pr view --json author`, is the owner's gh login, in any letter case) an approval or a change request goes as the reviewer when one is signed in (obid only: GitHub's sign-in, "The reviewer"): obid first checks with the reviewer's login that it can push to the repository (`gh api repos/<repo>` → `permissions.push`), because an approval from an account without write access doesn't count toward the required ones. Without it nothing is posted and the item stays open (`502`, `Nothing was posted: the reviewer, <login>, can't push to <repo>, so its approval wouldn't count: …`); a reviewer signed in as the owner's own account is never used, nor one whose login gh can't read now or that isn't signed in any more (`Nothing was posted: obid couldn't read the reviewer's GitHub login …`), so it's tried again rather than posted another way. Likewise when obid can't read the owner's login or the pull request's author. With no reviewer signed in it goes as the owner's comment, its first line still saying the verdict, as it does wherever GitHub says `your own pull request`. A comment is always the owner's. An item from before this has no `watch.verdict` and posts a comment.

  **Posting it.** Once a review's job ends `done`, its `listen` item (`Review ready: obi #835`) shows the review, its job's summary after the secret scan, under `watch.review`, offers `Approve` and `Dismiss` (`job` the review job, `watch.pr` the pull request and the head commit reviewed). A review the scan flags isn't shown and offers `Dismiss` only. `Approve` (the owner's own answer, on the Mac, where the review is shown: the conversation's brain is refused, the event orchestrator never takes a `listen` item (the kind keeps its name from the Slack listener this flow outlived), and a phone's `Approve`, through the gateway, is `403`, `Answer it on your Mac, where what it sends is shown`, until the phones show the review: the answer endpoint checks the item itself, found by its id as decoded, so no spelling of the path gets by; their `Dismiss` and later go through) posts exactly that review on the pull request with Oby's verdict, signed `by Oby: Oby wrote this review.` (Whose words, below), with obid's `gh` (`gh api -X POST repos/<repo>/pulls/<n>/reviews -f commit_id=<the commit reviewed> -f event=APPROVE|REQUEST_CHANGES|COMMENT -F body=@…`, from a folder of its own each time; see "Oby's verdict" below), after the secret scan again and only while the pull request's head is still the commit reviewed: obid reads the head before it decides who posts, and again after, just before posting, and the review names the commit reviewed (`commit_id`), never whatever the head is when GitHub gets it (`gh pr review` can't name one), so a push while obid asks around never gets an approval meant for the commit before it. A moved head (`out of date`), a finding, or a scan that can't run posts nothing and the item stays open (`502`, `Nothing was posted: …`). The claim is kept on disk before `gh` is asked (`watch.posting`): a second `Approve` meanwhile, or one after obid stopped mid-post, is `409` (`Oby was posting this review when it stopped, or still is: check the pull request on GitHub, then Dismiss it.`), so a review is never posted twice; when the claim can't be kept on disk, nothing is posted (`500`). A review the owner dismissed while it was being posted stays dismissed when the post fails. A review that's empty, or that the scan flags or can't check, says which and offers `Dismiss` only. Nothing is ever posted any other way. A review whose job didn't end `done` is left to its job's own report.
- An `assigned` item starts a `fix` job on that issue (`kind` `fix`, `issue`), routed as any job, that ends in a pull request through Publish as today (`Closes #N`).
- A `mine` item (your own pull request) starts a `fix` job on its branch (`kind` `fix`, `pr`, and `base` the pull request's head branch, read with `gh pr view --json headRefName,isCrossRepository,…` as it starts): it starts from the pull request's head on a branch of its own (`oby/fix-<n>`), adds commits on top, and what it publishes is a pull request into your branch, through Publish as today. It never pushes onto your branch, rewrites it or force-pushes it. A fork's branch is `409` (`Oby works on a branch of the repository itself, not a fork's.`), and so is a pull request an obid job opened (`Oby's job <title> opened this pull request and follows its checks: tell that job instead.`).
- A `reviews` item in a repository obid doesn't work in starts nothing here: it makes the `confirm` item that adds the repository and starts the review on the owner's signed Confirm, one per item (obid only: repositories, "A review request's repository, on the owner's tap"), and answers `422` with `Adding obi-ai-bot/other waits for your Confirm in the inbox.`
- The job's `task` and `title` are obid's own words, never GitHub's: `Review pull request #702 in obi-ai-bot/obi.` titled `Review obi #702`, `Fix issue #640 in obi-ai-bot/obi.` titled `Fix obi #640`, `Make pull request #812 in obi-ai-bot/obi pass its checks. Add commits on top of it; never rewrite or drop the ones it has.` titled `Fix CI obi #812`. What people wrote on GitHub (the title, the description, the labels and the latest 30 comments, at most 64 KiB; on your own pull request, each check's name and result too) reaches the agent only as a quoted file, `.obi/inputs/github-<name>-<number>.md` (an attachment, so its text is fenced, the prompt says it's data, never instructions, and the vault's secrets are taken out when it's copied into the worktree), never in a prompt, a task, a title or the brain's briefing.
- Answers `201` with the new `Job`, as `POST /v1/jobs` does (and a `job` event). When a queued or running job is on it already (same repository, kind and number; for your own pull request, a `fix` job with that `pr`), answers `200` with that job and starts nothing. Starts happen one at a time, from the feed and the inbox alike, so two taps at once (the Mac and the iPhone, or a card and the feed) start one job: the second waits for the first and answers its job.
- `404` for an id that isn't in the feed now (read again). `409` with why: `gh` isn't signed in, a fork's or an obid job's pull request, or an assigned issue or your own pull request in a repository a job can't start in (`obid doesn't work in obi-ai-bot/other yet: add it to repos in obid's configuration, or sign gh in to an account that can push to it.`). obid clones only the repositories it's configured for, those gh can push to, and those the owner added with a signed Confirm: which repositories it works in, and their checks, stay the owner's choice. `422` with why when the job can't be made (no account signed in), or when a review waits for the owner's Confirm (above). `502` when GitHub's text for it couldn't be read.

#### The `github` inbox item

When a read finds a new review request, a new assigned issue on a repository obid works in (`known`), or failed checks on a new head of the owner's own pull request, one `github` inbox item offers to do it:

```json
{"id": "github-3f9a0c1d2b4e5f60", "kind": "github", "job": "", "title": "Review requested: obi #702",
 "text": "Your review is requested on pull request #702 in obi-ai-bot/obi.", "options": ["Review it", "Dismiss"],
 "details": ["Starts Oby's review of it. Nothing is posted on GitHub.", "Leaves it for now. It stays in your GitHub work."],
 "recommended": null, "github": {"id": "3f9a0c1d2b4e5f60", "list": "reviews", "repo": "obi-ai-bot/obi", "number": 702, "author": "sangam", "association": "MEMBER", "trusted": true, "url": "https://github.com/obi-ai-bot/obi/pull/702", "known": true, "head": "9f3c…", "quoted": {"title": "Pair a device from Settings"}}, "triage": "needs_you", …}
```

- `github.author` is the login of who opened it (null when GitHub names nobody), for sorting it (obid only: sorting the inbox). `github.association` and `github.trusted`: GitHub's `authorAssociation` of that author and whether their words count as the owner's own (Whose words, below). `github.known`: whether obid worked in its repository when it came. `github.head`: the pull request's head commit, or null.
- A review request in a repository obid doesn't work in says so: `Your review is requested on pull request #12 in acme/other. Oby doesn't work in acme/other yet.`, and offers `Review it` (`Adds acme/other to the repositories Oby works in, with no check, then starts Oby's review of it. Nothing is posted on GitHub.`). Answered with obid's API token alone, it adds nothing: obid makes a `confirm` item, `Add acme/other and review #12` (action `add_repository`, one per offer), answers `422` with `Adding acme/other waits for your Confirm in the inbox.`, and the offer stays open. The owner's signed Confirm adds the repository and starts the review, and the offer is done with `Review it`. A tap on the item's card in the feed does the same. Oby's brain never answers a `github` item either: its answer is a `confirm` item too (obid only: confirm items).
- Failed checks on your own pull request (`checks` `FAILURE` or `ERROR` on a head that wasn't red at the last read, or a pull request that wasn't there) are titled `CI failed: obi #812`, say `Checks failed on your pull request #812 in obi-ai-bot/obi.` and offer `Fix CI` (`Starts a fix job on your pull request's branch. It never pushes onto your branch: when its check passes, it opens a pull request into it.`) and `Dismiss`. Its `id` is `github-ci-`, the item's `id`, `-` and the head's first 12 characters: one offer per red head, across restarts too. None for a draft, a fork's branch, a repository obid doesn't work in, a pull request an obid job opened (that job follows its checks), or one with a queued or running fix job on it. No offer of any kind comes while the job it would start is queued or running already (a review on that pull request, a fix job on that issue). Only typed fields decide (list, repository, number, head, checks, draft, fork): no title, label or check name.

- An assigned issue's item is titled `Assigned to you: obi #640`, says `Issue #640 in obi-ai-bot/obi is assigned to you.` and offers `Fix it` (`Starts a fix job on it, which opens a pull request when its check passes.`) and `Dismiss`. `title` and `text` are obid's own words; GitHub's title is quoted under `github.quoted`, for the card only.
- `Review it`, `Fix it` or `Fix CI` starts the work as `POST /v1/github/work/{id}/start` does, and the item is done, its `job` the job's id (or the job already on it). The answer claims the item first, so a second answer meanwhile is `409` (`That item is done already.`) and nothing settles it by itself under way; when it can't start, the answer is the same error and the item is open again. `Dismiss`, done or later settle or put it off; nothing is sent to GitHub.
- One per item, ever: its `id` is `github-` and the item's `id` (`github-ci-…` per head for failed checks), so an item that's done never comes back, across restarts too. It's done by itself, with no `answer`, once its item leaves the feed (reviewed, merged, closed or unassigned), and a `CI failed` one once its pull request's head moves or its checks pass (a re-run's pending checks keep it, so a second failure on the same head is still that offer); a new red head is a new offer. Nothing settles by itself after a read GitHub cut or left something out of.
- No flood, none lost: the first read with no earlier read on record (a new obid, one that never read GitHub, or the first after signing in again or as another login) offers nothing, it only notes what's there. After that, at most 5 items per read, the newest first; the rest wait for the next reads (kept in `home/github-work.json`, at most 100, across restarts), each while it's still in the feed and still offered, so a burst arrives 5 a read. Assigned issues on repositories obid doesn't work in, and your own pull requests whose checks didn't fail, get no inbox item: they're in the feed only.

#### Who reaches it

- On loopback, with obid's API token, like every endpoint.
- Through the gateway, a paired phone can `GET /v1/github/work`, `POST /v1/github/work/refresh` and `POST /v1/github/work/{id}/start` (obid only: the gateway). The list is read-only, and a start does no more than `POST /v1/jobs`, already on the allowlist, would, with obid's own task. The phone gets the feed scrubbed as any answer is.
- Another Obi never does: nothing in Obi-to-Obi exchanges reads the feed or starts work from it, and its typed actions are unchanged.

#### Whose words: the owner's, or untrusted

Issues, pull requests and comments written by the owner or by members of the owner's GitHub organizations count as the owner's own words, not as untrusted (the owner's decision of 2026-10-24); text from anyone else stays untrusted. Either way it's still quoted data, never instructions, and trust changes only whether a turn is untrusted (so whether the work it starts may run in Bypass), never what an agent is told (`github_work.rs`, `Owner::trusts`, `issue_trusted`).

- **Who counts.** The owner's own login, on any repository; or `OWNER` or `MEMBER` of a repository whose owner is the owner or one of the owner's organizations. Never `COLLABORATOR`, `CONTRIBUTOR`, `FIRST_TIME_CONTRIBUTOR`, `FIRST_TIMER`, `MANNEQUIN` or `NONE`, and never a member or the owner of another organization's repository (a `MEMBER` of `acme/app` is a member of `acme`, not of the owner's organizations).
- **The owner.** obid's own `gh` login (`gh api user`) and its organizations (`gh api user/orgs --paginate`; gh's `read:org` scope shows private memberships), kept for 10 minutes, and only while GitHub says the read at hand was made as that login: after `gh auth switch` the old login's organizations never count; organizations that couldn't be read are read again next time.
- **Never the owner's words.** What Oby wrote, under whoever's login: a pull request an obid job opened, and any text carrying Oby's signature `by Oby:` (what a teammate's Oby publishes under a member's login too; text that only forges it is untrusted, which fails closed). Every review obid posts on GitHub (Oby's review the owner approves, the reviewer account's approval, a teammate Oby's approval) carries it: one that doesn't already ends with `by Oby: Oby wrote this review.` Every issue Oby files under the owner's login with a model's or agents' words is signed so: an `oby-failure` issue (`by Oby: Oby's post-mortem of a failure, filed by the runner.`), a review follow-up issue (`by Oby: Oby's reviews of [pull request #N](…) found these items worth doing later:`) and a research job's proposed issue (its body, then `by Oby: a research job proposed this issue, and it was filed from Oby.`). A pull request's lookup (`view_pr`), whoever opened it: anyone who can push to its branch writes its files, branch and commits under its author's name. Labels, which whoever triages sets: a trusted issue's lookup leaves them out.
- **What an issue's lookup checks** (`view_issue`), with one more query after gh's answer (`gh api graphql`: who wrote, edited and renamed it, and as which login gh read it), so an edit made in between counts: its author and every comment's count; every edit of its description and of each comment (`userContentEdits`) was made by that text's author or the owner (a collaborator can edit a member's words, which keep the member's association); every rename of its title (`RenamedTitleEvent`) was made by its author or the owner; and no text carries `by Oby:`.
- **Fail closed.** An association, editor or renamer obid couldn't read, a list GitHub didn't give whole (more than 20 edits of one text or renames, more than 100 comments), a comment gh showed that the check didn't read, organizations it couldn't read (then no organization's member counts), a login it couldn't read, or a check that didn't end within the lookup's 10 seconds (gh's view and every read of the check share them): untrusted.
- **Where it shows.** Each feed item has `association` (GitHub's word, or null) and `trusted` (its author counts, as of the read that found it, and no obid job opened it), and its `github` inbox item carries both under `github`. The quoted file a job gets (`.obi/inputs/github-<name>-<n>.md`) names each author as `@login (MEMBER)` (just `@login` when GitHub didn't say), inside the same `GitHub, written by people` fences: never whether their words count as the owner's.
- **What it changes.** A conversation's `oby_inbox` doesn't taint the turn when everything waiting is a `github` offer with `trusted` true (an offer's text is obid's own sentence from typed fields); anything else waiting still does. A plan-loop `view_issue` that passes the check above doesn't make the turn count as researched; a `view_pr`, any other lookup, a refused one, or one that didn't run still does. That holds for everything the turn's `work.md` quotes, not only the plan at hand: a "continue" turn quotes the last turn's lookups, so one that carries anything but a trusted `view_issue` counts as researched from its first step, even when it looks nothing up itself. Reading files, a command's output, the web, attachments and a job's words taint exactly as before.

### obid only: teammates' pull requests

obid's own jobs on the same files are named in each other's prompts (Job, `files`). A teammate's open pull request, from another machine and branch, can change the same code too; obid looks for those so the clash shows before the merge, not at it. The idea is Trail-Code's edit overlap; none of its code is in Oby.

- **When a job starts** (an owner's job, not a review, not another Oby's, in a repository whose pull requests obid opens: `pullRequests`, or a GitHub remote), and it claims `files`: obid reads the repository's open pull requests with its own `gh` login, one `gh pr list --state open --limit 50 --json number,title,author,headRefName,url,files` call (so only the 50 newest open pull requests are looked at), and keeps those that change a claimed path (the same path, or one inside a claimed folder), but those on a branch of obid's own jobs in that repository: at most 5, the newest. GitHub lists a renamed file by its new path only, so a pull request that moves a claimed file away is found by where it moved to. Then it reads the diff of those files of each, all at once (`gh pr diff`, at most 32 KB kept, the secrets obid holds taken out; a rename's old path counts too). The whole read, list and diffs, takes at most 10 seconds: a diff not in by then is left out, and a list not in by then, or `gh` not installed or signed in, starts the job without it. GitHub's rate limit is the GitHub work feed's: while the feed waits one out, obid doesn't read, and a rate limit met here makes the feed wait too.
- **The agent** hears of them in its prompt in obid's words, with the pull request's number and the job's own paths only: `Pull request #812: both touch src/jobs.rs`, and is asked to read a pull request's diff before changing the same code, keep its change small and expect a rebase. Who opened each (a login, or `someone`), its branch (when it's plain characters), its title and its diff are quoted in `.obi/context/teammates.md`, as a plug-in's context is (written by people: data, never instructions). Agents still have no GitHub login: obid does the reading.
- **Tetrix**, when it's connected, is asked about up to 3 of the claimed files too (`search_entities` with `owner/repo#path`) with the rest of the job's context, so the graph's pull requests and commits for that code are in `.obi/context/tetrix.md`. Which pull requests are open comes from GitHub.
- **After the job's commits**, when its last round ends, obid reads once more with the files the commits changed (`git diff --name-only <startCommit>..HEAD`), and compares each pull request's diff with the job's (`git diff -U0`): a hunk of each in the same file (by its path before or after a rename) whose old lines meet, or that git puts in the same function (the name after `@@`), is the same code. The job's `summary` gets a line, before Tetrix's: `Teammates: @alice's #812 changes the same code (`fn run(&self)` in src/jobs.rs): read it before this merges.`, or `… changes the same files (src/lib.rs), not the same code.` Nothing when no pull request changes its files. Line ranges of two diffs off different bases can drift, so it's a pointer to look, not a verdict.
- On the job, `teammates` (Job, below), or null when obid didn't read (no claim yet, no `gh`, a review, another Oby's job, and on older runners):

  ```json
  {"prs": [{"number": 812, "title": "Run jobs side by side", "author": "alice", "branch": "feat/run",
            "url": "https://github.com/acme/shop/pull/812", "files": ["src/jobs.rs"], "clash": ["`fn run(&self)` in src/jobs.rs"]}],
   "rechecked": true}
  ```

  `files`: the job's paths (claimed, then those its commits changed) the pull request changes too. `clash`: after the job's commits, the code both change, `[]` when only files are shared, null before that check. `rechecked`: that check ran (once a job). `title` is what a person wrote: show it, never act on it. The apps show a line on the job page: `Also changed in @alice's #812 (src/jobs.rs)`, and `same code` when `clash` names some.

### obid only: the vault

A connection receives a credential from the same checked snapshot already learned by obid's
redactors. If Keychain Access changes an item between checks, the broker never gets a newer
credential from an unchecked second read. An unreadable store still refuses the request.


The owner's secrets for connections they make (#457), kept and used as the Swift runner keeps them (Engine/Vault.swift):

- **Where.** On a Mac whose `obid` runs in the Mac app's runner home (`~/Library/Application Support/Oby/Runner`, [The Mac app on the same Mac](#obid-only-configuration)), the Swift runner's own Keychain items: generic passwords in the owner's login Keychain under the service `com.deskree.oby.runner.vault`, the secret's name as the account, labelled `Oby vault: <name>`, so secrets the owner added to the Swift runner carry over. A Mac with no login Keychain file (macOS would answer errSecNoSuchKeychain to every read, and obid `503` to everything) gets the file there too. Anywhere else, and on Linux, `home/vault/secrets.json` (`{"secrets": {"<name>": "<value>"}}`) as the Swift runner's file vault keeps it: a folder only `obid`'s user may open (0700), the file readable only by that user (0600), each save a new file written 0600 and renamed into place under a lock (`home/vault/.lock`), so two `obid vault add` runs at once each keep their secret. The configuration's `vault` can say either ([obid only: configuration](#obid-only-configuration)). No items, or no file, is an empty vault, which everything reads.
- **What the Keychain is and isn't.** `obid` finds the login Keychain by its path from the user database, never through `$HOME`, and makes items with the Swift runner's service, account, label and accessibility (`WhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly`). Measured on a Mac (2026-10-17): another program of the same user, even an unsigned one, reads and updates these items without being asked, and macOS adds it to the item's access list, so against such a program the Keychain holds them no better than the 0600 file does. That holds for the Swift runner's vault too, and is [#577](https://github.com/obi-ai-bot/obi/issues/577). Every new build of `obid` that reads an item is added to its access list again, so Keychain Access shows one entry per build: harmless, but expected. Only the program that made an item may delete it, so `obid vault remove` of a secret the Swift runner added fails, saying to remove it there or in Keychain Access.
- **Putting secrets in.** On the machine, as `obid`'s user: `obid vault --config <path> add NAME` takes the value on stdin (typed without echo at a terminal, with echo back if the command is stopped, or piped in), `obid vault --config <path> list` prints the names, `obid vault --config <path> remove NAME` removes one. A value is never an argument (`add NAME VALUE` is refused with the usage line) or an environment variable, and nothing the command prints holds one. A name is 1 to 64 letters, digits, `.`, `_` or `-`, starting with a letter or digit; a value is 8 bytes to 64 KB, with the whitespace around a paste dropped. A common word or a weak pattern (`postgres`, `changeme`, `00000000`: fewer than 6 different characters, a common word with or without digits after it, or under 50 bits by its length and kinds of characters) is refused, since every push holding it would be refused and every answer would have it taken out. No endpoint takes or returns a value.
- **While obid runs.** `obid` reads the vault at start and again whenever it changed (the file's inode, size or time; each Keychain item's name and modification date), so a secret `obid vault add` puts there is taken out from then on, without a restart. A Keychain item's date has whole seconds only, so the Keychain's values are also read again at least every 2 seconds: a second change in the same second is taken out within that. A removed secret stops being taken out. At start the log says which store the vault is and how many secrets it holds, never a name or a value.
- **Answers.** Every `/v1` answer has the vault's secrets taken out of every string, keys too, plain and in every encoded form `obid` knows (base64, standard or URL-safe, at any offset in a longer run, or wrapped over lines as GNU base64 writes it, also behind a diff's `+` or a quote's `> ` and with the line breaks written as `\n` inside one JSON string; hex in either case; URL-encoded, a form's `+` for spaces too), each as `[vault: <name>]`; a string that still holds one some other way becomes that marker whole. An answer with no secret in it goes as it was, byte for byte. The event stream and `/wait` are scrubbed a frame at a time. When the vault can't be read, a request isn't carried out: nothing reaches an agent, the brain, a plug-in or another Oby (no job is made, no words join the conversation). The exceptions only stop work and send nothing anywhere: `POST /v1/stop-all`, `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/stop`, `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/dismiss`, and `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/permission` denying without words (`message` absent, null or blank); those are carried out, so the owner can always stop work. Either way the answer is then `503` with `{"error": "The vault can't be read, so obid answers nothing until it's fixed: <why>"}`, `<why>` naming the file, folder or Keychain service, never a value, and what to do (`run chmod 600 on it`, `move it aside, then add the secrets again with obid vault …`, `unlock the login Keychain`). Two answers start no work and come even then: `GET /v1/status`, which says why in `vaultProblem` (`the vault can't be read: <why>`, left out while it reads), and, once obid has read the vault at least once, `GET /v1/events`, scrubbed of the secrets it last read (before any read it's `503` like the rest, since nothing could be taken out of it). An open event stream goes on the same way. A check's bundle (`application/octet-stream`) goes as it is.
- **Agents.** Every prompt `obid` hands an agent's turn (a job's task, the owner's words for it, a publish-fix round, an Obi-to-Obi review) has the vault's secrets taken out the same way, and so do the files an Obi-to-Obi review hands its agent (the request and the diff in `.obi/inputs/`). When the vault can't be read, the agent gets nothing: the job fails with `The vault can't be read, so obid gave the agent nothing: <why> Once it's fixed, tell the job to go on.`
- **What goes out.** The publish gate and every scan (`crate::scan`: publishing, Obi-to-Obi replies and review results) look for the vault's secrets in those same forms, in every file a push sends, read whole (base64 wrapped over lines joined), and in each commit message; when the vault can't be read, the scan can't run and nothing goes out (`publishError` `Not published: the secret scan couldn't run (the vault can't be read: …).`).

### obid only: secrets a check declares

A repository says which secrets its check needs in `secretspec.toml` at its root ([SecretSpec](https://secretspec.dev)), names only, never values (oby-plan E1, "Secrets you declare"). The owner keeps each value in their vault ([obid only: the vault](#obid-only-the-vault)) as `check.<NAME>`: `obid vault --config <path> add check.STRIPE_TEST_KEY`, the value on stdin. Only names starting `check.` ever reach a check, so a connection's secret or obid's own never does.

- **Which names.** The keys of `[profiles.default]` (`NAME = { description = "…" }`, one inline table on one line) and the tables `[profiles.default.NAME]`, at most 32. A name is an environment variable's (letters, digits and `_`, not starting with a digit, at most 64), never one obid sets for every check (`PATH`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, …) or starting `GIT_`, `LD_`, `DYLD_`, `OBY_`, `OBID_`, `OBI_`, `CLAUDE`, `ANTHROPIC_`, `CODEX_`, `SSH_` or `BASH_FUNC_`. Other profiles, defaults and every other part of the file are left to the repository. A file obid can't read this way (a multi-line string, a name twice, a line it can't read in that profile) gives the check nothing, and says why.
- **From the base branch only.** The file is read from the job's base branch (its `base`, else the repository's `defaultBranch`) as obid's own clone has it (`refs/remotes/origin/<base>`, in a git folder every sandbox reads only), never from the worktree or the job's own branch: a commit that adds a name, the agent's or anyone's, changes nothing until it is merged.
- **Only into the check.** The values go into the check process's environment, after everything obid sets there, and nowhere else: never an agent's environment, prompt, transcript, a message, the chain, a log line, an answer or a pull request. The log says how many of the declared names the check got, never a name's value.
- **Taken out at once.** Each value, plain, JSON-escaped (`\"`, `\\`, `/` as `\/`, `\uXXXX` in either case) and in every encoded form obid knows (as for the vault), is taken out of what the check printed before obid keeps any of it (`check.log`, the job's `checks` and their failing tests, the agent's next prompt), as `[check secret: <NAME>]`; when only the end of a long output was kept, its first line, which a value may have been cut in, goes too. This catches a value a check prints by accident, not one its code turns into something else on purpose: that is why only the owner's own code gets values (below).
- **In a checkout of its own.** A check that gets values runs in a checkout obid makes from the commit the gate passed (`git worktree add` in obid's clone at `home/checks/<job>-<id>`, the job's commits since its base copied into the checkout's own object folder as one pack), with a temporary folder (`HOME`, `TMPDIR`) of its own, neither anywhere the job's sandbox can write, both removed after it. What the check writes never reaches the job; what the job left outside its commits (a file left uncommitted or hidden by a `.gitignore`, a rewrite after the gate) never reaches the check. A check without values runs in the job's worktree as before. Its builds start without the job's caches.
- **The threat model.** The values protect against other people's code reaching the check: teammates', pull request authors', received work, and content that steered the agent. An agent that never read foreign input counts as the owner's own, as the owner running the tests on their own laptop would. Commit metadata can't tell where a tree came from, so obid controls the inputs, and **fails closed**: a job is eligible only if every input it ever received came from an allowlisted channel. The channels:
  - **The owner's own words typed in Oby's apps**, signed by the app ("Words the owner typed", below): a job started through `POST /v1/jobs`, the owner's words sent to it (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages`, a permission's words), and what Oby's conversation plans from them while it's clean. Anything else holding obid's token (oby-mcp's `dispatch`, `message` and `ask`, a script, an agent, an older app or one that doesn't sign, such as the Watch, Android and the web app) sends words that aren't: a job they start, and one they reach, gets nothing. Not a job an event, a routine, the Lead or an inbox offer started, nor words from an event, the orchestrator or a Confirm of what Oby proposed (obid's own Try again aside).
  - **Oby's conversation, while clean.** Each conversation keeps the first thing it read or heard that isn't on this list, for good (kept in its file; one kept before this, or one obid doesn't keep, counts as marked). It stays clean on the owner's signed words, Oby's own answers, its room's files outside the repositories' checkouts and attachments, its own task list, Oby's tools that answer in obid's words (`oby_dispatch`, `oby_offer`, `oby_tasks`, `oby_routine`, `oby_check_in`, `oby_artifact`), the ones that name a job that is clean throughout (`oby_tell_job`, `oby_stop_job`, `oby_continue_job`, `oby_archive_job`, `oby_delete_job`, `oby_change_job`), `oby_jobs` while every job it lists is, and a clean job's end. Anything else marks it: words no app signed, turns its client wrote, attachments, a supervisor's turn, a recap or check-in, a routine's run, `oby_job`, the inbox, past conversations, a command, the web, a helper, and any tool it doesn't know. A job a marked conversation starts is someone else's code from the start and runs in a clone apart; its words (a tell, a continue, a permission's no) make the job they reach someone else's.
  - **The text of an issue or pull request the owner alone wrote**: its body, title and every comment, edit, rename, review and review comment, every list read whole, read as the job starts and again at its check (what's deleted in between still reached it). What obid posts under the owner's login is signed `by Oby:` and never counts as the owner's, but a pull request's own description that obid wrote only for jobs that are clean throughout (the jobs whose record says they opened or described it).
  - **The base repository's code.**
  - **Tool calls of kinds obid knows are safe**: a file read, write, edit or search, a command inside the job's sandbox, its to-do list, obid's own question to the owner.

  Anything else, a channel nobody listed included, makes the job someone else's code the moment it arrives, and marks the owner's clone: a vendor's own web search or fetch, any MCP tool or resource, a helper agent, an MCP server's tool descriptions given at launch, any tool kind an agent adds later or obid doesn't parse (Claude Code's unparsed blocks, Codex's helper threads and unknown items, Cursor's unknown kinds and its `search`, which its web search reports as too), and egress past the tool's API and the default registries. A check gets the owner's values only when all of these hold too, none otherwise:
  1. **The machine is a Mac.** On Linux a job's agent still has a network of its own until oby#616, so it could fetch anyone's code itself (oby-plan E1: macOS only until then). On a Mac every agent's only way off the machine is obid's egress proxy, and every turn's sandbox watcher kills what the turn started, a process that called `setsid` included, when it ends.
  2. **obid's clone holds only what obid knows is the owner's.** A clone is made with only the default branch (`git clone --single-branch`; it carries `obid.narrow`); a clone made before holds every branch of its remote and gives no values until it's moved aside and made again. Only the owner's own jobs write to it: a job that's `untrusted` or someone else's code when it's made keeps to a clone apart (`home/apart/clones/<repo>`, its follow-ups too), and a review another Oby asks for to its own (`home/received/clones/<repo>`). Anything else comes into the owner's clone only by obid's own fetch: a job's base, a pull request's head. One that isn't a reviewed branch (3) and whose commits aren't all in obid's record of the owner's jobs (4) notes it in the clone's own settings (`obid.foreign`, which no sandbox can write), as does a job there that turns someone else's code; from then on no check in that repository gets values. An agent can't fetch anything itself: it has no code host's login (an empty `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, an invalid push URL), and the proxy lets its tool's own services through and decides every other site. The job turns someone else's code when its agent reaches a vendor host past its tool's API (Claude Code: `api.anthropic.com`, `statsig.anthropic.com`, `console.anthropic.com`; Codex: `api.openai.com`, `auth.openai.com`, so a Codex job on a ChatGPT login, which calls `chatgpt.com`, gets no values; Cursor: `api2.cursor.sh`, `api3.cursor.sh`, `api4.cursor.sh`): a vendor's other hosts serve what people share there. It does too for any site the proxy lets through but the default registries (`egress::REGISTRIES`: npm, Yarn, Maven Central, Gradle's plugins and services, CocoaPods' CDN), which serve only published, immutable versions on an edge that answers only the host the TLS hello named. The proxy reads that name, not the HTTP `Host` inside the tunnel, so a registry whose edge serves whichever of its sites `Host` names (`egress::ROUTED_BY_HOST`: Fastly's PyPI, crates.io and Rust's toolchains, RubyGems; Google's Go module proxy and checksum database, which also serve any public commit) could carry anyone's code: those no longer go through by default, and when a rule or an owner's card lets one through, the job turns someone else's code. And it does for every channel that brings content past the proxy: its vendor's own web search or fetch, or any MCP server's answer (obid's connected apps, Tetrix's), as a tool call in its turn; a connected app's answer through obid's broker; the context obid gives it in `.obi/context` (Tetrix's, teammates' pull requests). A marked owner's clone is made again before the next job uses it, once no job of the repository runs in it: the old one moves to `home/marked/<when>` with the jobs whose worktrees belong to it, and their follow-ups.
  3. **The base is reviewed, and so is the default branch:** a branch the repository lets in only through a pull request with at least one approving review that a later push can't slip past, that no one can bypass, as GitHub says through obid's own `gh`: an `active` ruleset whose `pull_request` rule has `required_approving_review_count` 1 or more and `require_last_push_approval` or `dismiss_stale_reviews_on_push`, that the login can't bypass (`current_user_can_bypass` `never`, which counts its teams), and whose `bypass_actors` (obid's login must be able to read them) is empty or holds only teams with no members (a break-glass team kept empty), or classic protection with `required_approving_review_count` 1 or more and `require_last_push_approval` or `dismiss_stale_reviews`, `enforce_admins` on, and no bypass allowance for the login, any team or any app. Never an `oby/` branch or the job's own branch. The repository's default branch, in every clone, must be one too.
  4. **Defence in depth:** every commit between that base and the commit is in obid's own record of what the job (and the follow-ups it continues) made, kept in the job's file in obid's home: after each of an owner's job's turns, the commits it added, each made then (author and committer the same, both no more than 5 minutes before the turn began) and none with the same change (`git patch-id --stable`) as a commit on any other branch the clone has, other jobs' own included; obid's rebase of recorded commits; and its "work left uncommitted" commit. A turn that brings in a commit made elsewhere makes the job someone else's code.
  5. **The job is the owner's:** started from the owner's own words, signed, through the jobs API or a clean conversation, and not another Oby's review, not a follow-up of a job that got none (carried to every follow-up), not `untrusted`, and not one that works from an issue or pull request anyone but the owner wrote in (members of the owner's organisation still count as the owner for what a conversation trusts, not for this). A job that's someone else's code (one of these, or marked above) marks the owner's clone; a job that's only not the owner's own words (a brain's, an event's) gets no values but leaves the clone as it is, and obid records its commits as it does the owner's.

  Its output starts `obid: secretspec.toml on <base> declares <names>; obid gave this check none of its owner's secrets: <why>.` Out of scope, and said so: a package anyone may publish to a registry the job installs from (the owner's own tests would install the same), and foreign code an agent writes out, as its own new commit, from what its model already knows.
- **What's missing.** A declared name the vault doesn't have as `check.<NAME>` is left unset, and the check's output starts with a line saying which (`obid: secretspec.toml declares X, which the owner's vault doesn't have as check.X: obid left it unset.`). When the vault can't be read, the check gets none of them and says so.
- **Words the owner typed.** Oby's Mac and iPhone apps sign every request but a read (`GET`, `HEAD`) with the device's approval key, the one a Confirm uses (it signs on the owner's click, asking for no Touch ID or Face ID), in the header `X-Oby-Words: <device> <signedAt> <signature>`: ECDSA P-256 with SHA-256, DER, base64, over `"oby-words\n<METHOD>\n<path>\n<SHA-256 of the body, hex>\n<signedAt>"`, the path from `/v1/` on, without its query, and an empty body's hash when it has none. obid takes the words as the owner's own, for these secrets only, when the device is registered, the signature is its key's over exactly that request, `signedAt` (to the second, or to the millisecond as the apps sign, so two of the same request in one second differ) is within two minutes, and that signature was never used before (kept with the approvals, ten minutes). The gateway and the phone relay pass the header on. It decides nothing else: a request without it does everything it did, and only its job, its follow-up or its conversation gets no values. The routes that read it, and what words without it do:

  | Route | Words without it |
  | --- | --- |
  | `POST /v1/jobs` | the job isn't the owner's own words |
  | `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages` | the job that hears them, or the follow-up they start, isn't; files attached make the follow-up someone else's code, signed or not, as they mark a conversation |
  | `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/permission` (an answer, a no's words) | the job isn't |
  | `POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer` on a job's question, permission or plan (an answer, a no's words) | the job isn't |
  | `PATCH /v1/jobs/{id}` (a title, which Oby's conversations read) | the job isn't |
  | `POST /v1/plans` | the conversation is marked |
  | `POST /v1/memory/suggestions/{id}/remember` | the memory is marked |

  The rest put no words into a job or a conversation: a Confirm has its own tap (`oby-confirm`), and what it carries out is marked as Oby proposed it (a Start's planned words a working follow-up hears carry the plan's mark, and one kept before obid marked plans isn't the owner's own words); an offer's start (GitHub's, a failure's fix) and a routine's run start jobs that are never the owner's own words; a conversation's or an artifact's title reaches no model; the settings, accounts, devices, sharing and the Obysphere routes carry no words to either.
- **Memories.** Each memory keeps where it came from: a suggestion found in a reviewer's words, or in a conversation that was marked, is marked, and so is one a Remember no app signed saved (one kept before, without `clean: yes` in its file, counts as marked). A job that's given a marked memory isn't the owner's own words any more, and a conversation's room with one marks the conversation. obid's own sentences (a repository's workflow, who reviews where) are the owner's.
- **What obid described.** Every pull request obid opens or describes is noted, with its job, in `home/described.json`, kept after the job is deleted: its description counts as the owner's only while every job noted for it is still there and clean.
- **An answer from another conversation.** Words that answer Oby's question (`POST /v1/plans` with `answer`, as the Watch sends it) mark the conversation they join unless Oby asked that question there.

### obid only: conversations

Many conversations with Oby instead of one thread (#493; decided 2026-10-16, D2): each thing asked on Home can start its own, and every device sees them all. The Swift runner keeps its one thread.

A conversation, as `GET /v1/conversations` lists it and the `conversation` event sends it:

```json
{"id": "5b0e2c4d-…", "title": "Review oby PR #33", "pinned": false,
 "created": "2026-10-16T09:12:00Z", "lastActivity": "2026-10-16T09:14:07Z", "cursor": "0-42",
 "tasks": [{"id": "t1", "title": "Fix the login test", "status": "in_progress", "job": "ab12cd34", "note": null}],
 "folders": ["/Users/alex/Projects/api"]}
```

- `folders` (#1572): the working folders its work is in, canonical paths in the order picked (obid only: working folders, below). Always a list, empty when it has none. A change sends the `conversation` event.

- `tasks`: the task list the conversation's agent keeps for itself (`oby_tasks`, obid only: the conversation manages its own work), in its order. Always a list, empty when it keeps none. A change sends the `conversation` event.

- `id`: a lower-case UUID. `title`: the first line the user said in it, as Sangam's design names conversations (`design/app/src/renderer/src/chats.ts`): at most 48 characters (the first 45 and `…` when longer), its first letter upper-case, any other control character a space. A rename replaces it. `created`: when it started. `lastActivity`: its latest turn's `at`, or `created`. Renaming or pinning isn't activity.
- `cursor`: its place in the list right now (pinned or not, and how recent its latest turn is), for paging (below). It changes with every turn and every pin. Treat it as opaque.
- Each keeps its last 500 turns, the oldest pruned, in a private file of its own (`home/conversations/<id>.json`, 0600).
- Every turn names its conversation: `GET /v1/conversation` and the `turn` event carry `"conversation": "<id>"`.
- **At most 200 are kept.** When a new one starts past 200 (and when obid starts with more), obid forgets the oldest that isn't pinned (the one whose latest turn is oldest), as `DELETE` would, with its `conversation` event. Pinned conversations and the latest one are never forgotten, so pinning more than 200 keeps them all. The jobs a forgotten conversation started stay.

**`GET /v1/conversations?after=&limit=`**: the conversations, the pinned ones first, then the others, each newest first (by their latest turn; obid orders turns exactly, not only to the second). `limit` is as for turns: 50 unless asked, 1 to 500. For the next page, send the `cursor` of the last conversation you show as `after`: the list goes on from that place. A cursor is a place, not a conversation: a conversation that stayed where it was is listed once, never skipped. One that moved since (a turn or a pin moves it up, an unpin down) can be missed or listed again; its `conversation` event already told you where it is, so keep one per `id`. `422` for an `after` that isn't a cursor. Fewer than `limit` means there are no more; `[]` before anything was said.

**`GET /v1/conversation?id=&before=&limit=`**: one page of the conversation `id`'s turns, as above; `404` for an `id` that isn't there. Without `id`, a `before` turn names the conversation (the one it's in); with neither, it's the latest conversation (the one with the latest turn), `[]` when there's none. So an app from before #493 shows the latest conversation and pages back through the one it shows.

**`GET /v1/conversations?q=watch target`** finds conversations by what was said in them (#593): a plain text match, no model. `q` is split on spaces, each word lower-cased with the punctuation around it taken off (`target?` is `target`, `off-limits` stays whole), and a conversation matches when its title, or one of its turns, holds every word, in any case. The list is in the same order as without `q`, with `after` and `limit` as above, and each conversation has one more key, `turn`: the latest turn that holds the words, `{"id", "who", "at", "text"}`, its `text` cut to 500 characters around the first word (`…` where it's cut), or `null` when only the title holds them. Only the conversations obid keeps are searched, so a deleted one never appears, and neither do turns pruned past the 500 kept. `422` for a `q` with no word in it or longer than 200 characters.

**`PATCH /v1/conversations/{id}`** with `{"title": "Vault questions"}`, `{"pinned": true}`, `{"folders": ["/Users/alex/Projects/web"]}` or more than one renames or pins it, or replaces its working folders (an empty list clears them), and answers the conversation. A folder it has already stays as kept, even one gone from disk since, so it can be taken off; any other is checked as `POST /v1/plans` checks it (`422` naming it and why). Through the gateway or the phone relay, `folders` may only leave out folders it has: one more is `422`. The title is trimmed: one line of 1 to 100 characters, with no line break (`\n`, `\r`, U+2028, U+2029) or other control character. `422` for none of the keys or a title that breaks those rules; `400` for a body that isn't that JSON (another key, a `title` that isn't a string, a `pinned` that isn't `true` or `false`, a `folders` that isn't a list of strings); `404` for an id that isn't there.

**`DELETE /v1/conversations/{id}`** forgets it and its turns: `204`, `404` for an id that isn't there. The jobs it started stay, and keep its id in their `conversation`: show such a job's card on its own.

**`POST /v1/plans`** takes `conversation`, an `id` (the words join it; `404` if it isn't there), or `"new": true` (they start a new conversation, titled from them); both is `422`. With neither, the words join the latest conversation, or start the first one: the apps from before #493 (the Mac, the iPhone, the Watch) send neither and show one thread, and the Watch and the talk key carry on the latest conversation (#493). A screen that starts a conversation, like Home, sends `"new": true`. The brain reads only that conversation's turns (or the `turns` the app sends). Every answer, whatever its `kind`, carries `"conversation": "<id>"`, the one the words joined. The body may carry `"id"`, a lower-case UUID the app gave the words (anything else is `422`): the user's turn takes it, so the app's own copy of the words gives way to obid's turn of the same id. Words sent again with the id of the user's turn of the same words, where they go (the conversation named, the latest, or with `"new": true` the one those words started; an app's Try again after an answer it lost), add nothing and start nothing: the answer is `{"kind": "none", "repeated": true, "conversation": <that one>}`. An id any other turn has (other words, or another conversation) is `409`.

- A plan obid refuses is never a `422` (#546): obid tells the brain why and lets it try once more, then Codex plans, then keyword rules, which never act on the user's first words (they reply "Oby couldn't think that through: say it again", or ask which repository; to "continue", "go on" or "keep going" they reply "Oby couldn't think that through: say “continue” again in a moment.", never asking which repository, #632). The conversation keeps the words and that answer.
- When no brain answers at all (each one asked timed out or failed, and none sent a plan obid refused), rules don't guess: the reply says which brain and how, and that nothing started, as `"Oby's brain couldn't answer: Claude Code and Codex both took too long, so nothing was started. Try again."` (`Claude Code failed, then Codex took too long`, and so on). When the Mac is very busy it says so instead of "Try again.": `The Mac is very busy (load 512): try again once it calms down.` After a step that did or looked at something, it says that first and ends `Say “continue” for the rest.` (or `… to pick up from there.`). The turn ends `brain_failed`, its record's `by` is `none`, and the conversation keeps the reply like any other.
- **One answer at a time in a conversation** (Sangam's design, docs/mac-design.md 1.6). Words said in a conversation while a turn is under way in it wait for that turn, which goes on to its own answer. When it ends, one turn answers every word said meanwhile, as the latest words' turn: they're one after another with a blank line between them, their attachments together, and the brain or the session reads the whole conversation as it is then, that answer included. The latest words' request carries the answer, and the jobs it starts name the latest words' `turn`. Each earlier request answers `{"kind": "none", "jobs": [], "drafts": [], "conversation": "<id>"}` once that turn takes its words, and no turn starts for them alone. Stop (`POST /v1/conversation/stop`, or a bare "stop") still interrupts the turn under way, and the words waiting then get their turn. `POST /v1/plans/cancel` drops the turn under way and the words waiting for it: their requests answer `none`, and the words stay in the conversation unanswered. A turn obid started there by itself (obid only: the conversation supervises its jobs) gives way to the owner's words: it's cancelled, and comes again after their turn. A turn in another conversation goes on.
- A conversation deleted while its turn runs stays deleted: the answer still comes, with its `conversation`, and Oby's words aren't kept.

**Jobs remember the conversation that started them.** A job a plan started has `conversation`, the id of the conversation whose words started it, kept when that conversation is deleted, and every `job` event of it carries it, up to the last one (how it ended): show its events in that conversation. It also has `turn`, the id of the user's turn whose words started it, from its very first `job` event, so the apps put its card under those words the moment it starts, before Oby's answer lists it in `jobs`; A follow-up the owner's words start in its parent's own conversation, and a job the Lead starts, have that turn too. `turn` is null for a job no turn started directly (a follow-up told from its page or from another conversation, a Start or Confirm in the inbox, an event turn's); a job Oby offered (`oby_offer`) has the turn it was offered in, whenever the owner's Start starts it, so its card goes under the answer that offered it. The apps count it only for a job of the conversation on screen. A job from `POST /v1/jobs`, or another Obi's review, has `conversation` null. An inbox item names its `job`, so it belongs where its job does. A routine's runs go to the routine's conversation ([obid only: routines](#obid-only-routines-395)): `conversation` is the routine's, `turn` is null (no user's turn started it, Run now from chat included, which starts on the owner's Confirm), and the turn that says the run started lists its job, so its card goes there. Work that comes in from elsewhere (another Oby, GitHub) doesn't yet start a conversation of its own.

**A job tells its conversation how it ended (#351).** Each time a job a conversation started goes from `queued` or `running` to an end, obid adds one Oby turn to that conversation, with the job's card (`jobs` is `[<id>]`) and `"ended": true`, a key only these turns have. No model writes it: its first line is obid's own words, from the job's fields: `<title> is done: <pull request URL>` when it was published, `<title> is done, but it wasn't published: <why>` when its publish failed, `<title> is done: Pushed, but <why>` when the push went out and only its pull request failed, `<title> is done and pushed.` for a remote with no pull requests (#627), `<title> is done.`, `<title> failed.`, `<title> stalled: <stalled.text>. Its branch is kept and nothing was pushed.` (a stalled job, whose conversation then decides what comes next: obid only: the conversation supervises its jobs), `<title> was stopped.` or `<title> was interrupted.`, the title as `title` reads, cut to 80 characters. When the job's `summary` has a line, its first one (cut to 300 characters) follows on a second line: for a failed job, why it failed (not for a stalled one, whose summary starts with the same stall). Every secret obid holds is taken out of both, as `[redacted: <name>]`. The summary may be its agent's words, so the brain's briefing reads only the first line of such a turn; what the agent wrote stays in the quoted `work.md` (obid only: the brain). A job that ends again after Try again or one more round says so again. A job from `POST /v1/jobs` or another Obi's review (no `conversation`), a dismissed job, and what had ended when obid started add nothing; a conversation deleted meanwhile isn't told. This turn is apart from the event orchestrator's (obid only: the brain), which still reads a failed job and acts in its own conversation, `orchestrator`.

**Events.** A `conversation` event comes with the conversation as listed whenever that changes: when it starts, with each turn (before the `turn` event, so a new conversation is known before its first turn), and when it's renamed or pinned. Replace the conversation with that `id` and sort again. When one is deleted or forgotten, it's `{"id": "<id>", "deleted": true}`: take it out. An app from before #493 adds every `turn` to the one thread it shows, whichever conversation it's in, until it fetches again.

**From one thread to many.** When obid starts, the single thread it kept before #493 (`home/conversation.json`) becomes the first conversation, with all its turns. Its `id` is the thread's first turn's id when obid made that id (a lower-case UUID), and a new one otherwise; its `title` comes from the first thing the user said in it, and its `created` is the first turn's `at`. The conversation is written, the folder's entries are synced to disk, and only then is the old file moved aside to `home/conversation.json.pre-493`, unchanged, for a rollback to an obid from before #493 (rename it back). It isn't read again, and deleting the conversation doesn't delete it: delete it once a rollback is no longer wanted. A move cut short is finished at the next start, never made twice.

**Through the gateway.** On obid's gateway (obid only: the gateway, below), `GET /v1/conversations`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /v1/conversations/{id}` are on the allowlist, so a paired phone lists, renames and deletes conversations as the web app does. The Swift runner's gateway (Runner/Routes.swift) doesn't list them; `GET /v1/conversation` and `POST /v1/plans` are on both.

**On the Mac.** When the runner on this Mac is obid (`GET /v1/conversations` answers), the window's side panel lists the conversations, Pinned above Recent, and searches, renames (`PATCH`), pins (`PATCH`) and deletes (`DELETE`) them. A row opens that conversation (`GET /v1/conversation?id=`) and the box in it sends `conversation`. Home's box sends `"new": true`, and so do the first words of a New conversation, which opens empty. The talk key's words send neither, so they carry on in the latest conversation, unless the window is in front on the conversation page, where they join the one shown. Each conversation shows only its own turns. A job's page, and a task's menu, lead to the conversation named by the job's `conversation`, while that conversation is listed; deleted, the job has none to go to. The Mac follows the `conversation` event, and shows only the `turn` events of the conversation on screen. When the runner answers `404` to `GET /v1/conversations` (the Swift runner), or the runner isn't the one on this Mac (the Mac's own brain plans then), the Mac keeps today's single thread, hides the list and sends neither `conversation` nor `new`.

**A decision is shared between conversations only once it's remembered.** What the user decides in one conversation ("never touch the Watch target") should hold in all of them (#493). obid's brain is briefed on the work (obid only: the brain, below), but the conversation it reads is only that conversation's own turns, and it has no memory that could carry a decision. Its remembered decisions (below) are the owner's answers to other Obis' requests, not what they say to Oby. obid keeps memories now (obid only: memory, below), and every conversation's session reads them: a decision the owner has Oby remember reaches all of them. One said in a conversation and not remembered still doesn't reach the others.

### obid only: working folders (#1572)

The message box's Select working folder… (Sangam's design, #1564): the owner points Oby at folders on this Mac, kept by the conversation. The contract is docs/contracts/folders.md; what's built so far is the boundary below. Jobs don't work in folders yet: the brain isn't told about them, and words in a conversation with folders start jobs in repositories as before (the contract's "Which kind of folder" and landing come next).

- **Who adds one.** Only the owner's Mac app, over loopback with the API token: through the gateway or the phone relay, `folders` with a path on `POST /v1/plans` is `422` (an empty list is none), and so is a `PATCH` that adds a folder (`Folders are added only on the Mac: from here you can take one off.`). A paired device's `PATCH` may take folders off. Whether adding one also needs the owner's signed words is open (#1572).
- **Every path is checked, every time it's added.** It's absolute, canonicalized (links resolved, `..` gone; the canonical path is what's kept), there, and a folder. It isn't `/`, the person's home folder itself, `/Volumes` itself, inside `~/Library`, inside `/System`, `/Library`, `/Applications`, `/usr`, `/bin`, `/sbin`, `/etc`, `/var`, `/private`, `/opt`, `/cores` or `/dev` (on Linux also `/proc`, `/sys`, `/root`, `/run` and `/boot`), and doesn't hold or sit inside obid's home, an agent's login (`~/.claude`, `~/.codex`, `~/.cursor`, every account's `configDir`) or the person's keys and logins (`~/.ssh`, `~/.config/gh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.gnupg`, `~/.docker`, `~/.kube`), each resolved through links first. A folder on another volume (`/Volumes/X/…`) is fine. A folder in the person's home is checked against that home's own guards (its `Library`, the logins and keys), never against the system folders the home may sit in. The person's homes are the one the password database names and obid's `HOME`; `OBID_PERSON_HOMES` (paths joined as `PATH` joins them) names them instead, for obid's tests, whose folders are made in a home of their own. On macOS paths are compared ignoring case (`~/LIBRARY` is `~/Library`). The `422` names the folder by its name and why (`"Library" is where your Mac keeps its own settings, so Oby doesn't work there.`; words to come from Sangam).
- **What a conversation keeps.** At most 20 folders, in the order picked, the same folder once, in its file (`home/conversations/<id>.json`); more is `422` (`A conversation works in at most 20 folders.`). `POST /v1/plans` adds; `PATCH` replaces, merged with the folders as they are when it's written, so a folder taken off meanwhile comes back only if it passes its own check.
- **What the window shows.** obid's `422`s are the error lines the Electron app shows as they are. Their words are obid's placeholders until Sangam writes his: `"<name>" isn't a folder's full path.`, `"<name>" isn't at <path>.`, `Oby can't read "<name>": macOS hasn't let it in. Allow it in System Settings › Privacy & Security › Files and Folders.`, `"<name>" is a file, not a folder.`, `"<name>" is your whole disk: pick a folder inside it.`, `"<name>" holds your Mac's disks: pick a folder on one.`, `"<name>" is your whole home folder: pick a folder inside it.`, `"<name>" is where your Mac keeps its own settings, so Oby doesn't work there.`, `"<name>" is where your Mac keeps its own files, so Oby doesn't work there.`, `"<name>" is where the system keeps its own files, so Oby doesn't work there.`, `"<name>" holds Oby's own files, so Oby doesn't work there.`, `"<name>" holds an agent's login, so Oby doesn't work there.`, `"<name>" holds your keys or logins, so Oby doesn't work there.`, `"<name>" has a name Oby can't read.`, `A conversation works in at most 20 folders.`, `Folders are added only on the Mac.` and `Folders are added only on the Mac: from here you can take one off.`

### obid only: memory (#378)

What Oby remembers about the owner and each repository (docs/onboarding.md section 5, "Learned workflow: memory"). This part keeps memories, lists and deletes them, finds what the owner keeps repeating, and keeps how each repository merges, typed (`workflow`, below); each owner's job's agent and each conversation's session get them as quoted data (**What reaches the work**, below); the job follower merges by a remembered workflow (obid only: merging). The Swift runner has none: `404`.

**Where.** One Markdown file per memory, 0600, in 0700 folders: `home/memory/user/<id>.md` (about the owner) and `home/memory/repos/<owner>/<name>/<id>.md` (one repository). Frontmatter of one-line values, then the text: `id` (`m` and 8 hex digits), `scope` (`user` or `project`), `repo` (`project` only), `kind` (`preference`, `instruction`, `fact`, `feedback`, `workflow` or `review`), `source` (`found`: a suggestion the owner said yes to; `user`: a workflow or a review the owner said yes to), `title`, `from` (where it came from, in obid's words: `You said it in 3 conversations; reviewers asked for it on #12 and #40`), `created`, `updated`, `used`, and on a `workflow` memory only, `workflow` (below). A file that doesn't read as a memory is skipped and logged, never fatal; keys obid doesn't know are dropped.

**How a repository merges (#854).** A `workflow` memory is one repository's (`project`), with one typed value obid reads strictly, one line of JSON: `workflow: {"base":"dev","method":"squash","rebaseFirst":false,"review":"reviewer","checks":"mac"}`. `base` is a branch name, never `main`, `master` or `trunk`; `method` `squash`, `merge` or `rebase`; `rebaseFirst` a boolean; `review` who approves first, `oby` (Oby's own review of the commit), `reviewer` (Oby's reviewer account), `teammate` (a teammate's Oby) or `person`; `checks` the checks that pass on the head first, `mac` or `github` (no `none`: nothing merges without a check that passed on that commit). A key or a value obid doesn't know, a `workflow` on another kind, or a `workflow` memory without one, and the file isn't a memory. Its `title` (`How obi merges`) and `text` are obid's own words made from it (`Squash-merge into dev after Oby's reviewer account approves and your Mac's checks pass on the same commit.`), never a model's or a suggestion's. It's the only memory a merge reads, and a merge checks every fixed rule whatever it says (obid only: merging). Oby asks "Can I remember that?" about it with a suggestion of `kind` `workflow` carrying the `workflow` (and no `sources`), which replaces one waiting for the same repository and stays when Find runs; only the owner's Remember on it saves it (or their `Merge, and remember` on a merge card, which asks the same question, or `Ask a teammate, and remember`, which changes only the `review` of a workflow already taught), and it replaces the repository's workflow before it. Nothing else saves one: not Find (its suggestions are never `workflow`), the brain, an agent, a reviewer's words or another Obi, and saving a `workflow` memory any other way is refused. Deleting it puts the repository back on asking.

**Who reviews a repository (the owner's decision of 2026-10-25).** A `review` memory is one repository's (`project`), with no typed value: it says the owner said yes to a teammate's Oby reviewing that repository's pull requests where GitHub requires another person's approval (obid only: merging). Its `title` (`Who reviews obi`) and `text` (`Where GitHub requires another person's approval on <repo>, Oby asks a teammate's Oby to review it. It still asks you before it merges.`) are obid's own words. Only the owner's `Ask a teammate, and remember` on a merge card saves one, and it replaces the repository's `review` before it; saving a `review` memory any other way is refused, and Find never suggests one. It never says how the repository merges: a `workflow` does, and once the repository has one, the `review` memory isn't read. Deleting it puts the repository back on asking.

**Nothing is saved without the owner's yes.** The only way a memory is written is `POST /v1/memory/suggestions/{id}/remember`, the owner's Remember on "Can I remember that?"; obid never saves one by itself, not on a first run either. On the way in it's checked again whatever made it: a title of 1 to 60 characters, a text of 1 to 500, at most 100 memories per scope, and the secret scan (the publish gate's, gitleaks included) on its title, text and `from`: a finding saves nothing (`422`, `That looks like a secret; Oby doesn't remember secrets.`), and a scan that can't run saves nothing (`503`). A memory never widens a rule AGENTS.md keeps fixed. obid reads it as words, sentence by sentence (NFKD with its combining marks dropped, so fullwidth, styled and accented letters read as plain ones; lower case; every character but letters, digits and apostrophes a space, so `origin/main`, `HEAD:main`, `+main` and `refs/heads/main` all hold `main`; commas, colons, dashes, brackets, slashes and line breaks end a clause, and so does a hyphen unless it joins two words ("force-push"); sentences end at `.;!?`). A text holding a character that shows nothing or changes the one before it (a format character, a combining mark typed as one, a Hangul filler, an emoji's variation selector) is refused, and so is one with a word mixing Latin with Greek or Cyrillic letters, each with its own reason.
