- **Known gaps:** on macOS a box can read the arguments and environment of the person's other processes (`kern.procargs2`, which Seatbelt doesn't mediate: obid tried `deny process-info*`, `deny sysctl*` and a `kern.procargs2` rule on macOS 26, and none stopped it), so a command could print the token obid gives a job's or a Codex conversation's agent (`OBY_MCP_TOKEN`). obid learns every such token as a secret, so what a command prints reaches the agent and the apps with it redacted, and a command can't use one: it has no network, or with the network no loopback, where those endpoints are. On Linux the box has its own process namespace. Passing those tokens through a file only their process reads is [#777](https://github.com/obi-ai-bot/obi/issues/777). On a Mac whose `git` is Xcode's `/usr/bin/git` (an `xcrun` shim), git can't run in the shell: the box doesn't read the developer folder (a git from Homebrew runs). Matching a command's words is best-effort, as for a job: a script that opens a socket itself isn't caught by the rules, but the box has no network, so it fails. A command the owner allowed once has the whole network for that run (no host allowlist).

### obid only: the conversation manages its own work

The conversation's agent manages Oby's work as Claude Code manages its own (TaskStop, TaskCreate, TaskUpdate, TaskList; Gemini CLI's `write_todos`), with Oby's tools in the `oby` MCP server above. What the owner says ("cancel that", "archive the done ones", "delete the failed job", "switch it to Codex") becomes one of these calls.

**The gate.** These tools act only on the owner's own jobs: a review another Obi asked for is refused (`That's a review another Oby asked for: it isn't yours to change.`). What can't be undone, what widens what a job may do (Bypass counts as wider only where it isn't the owner's own default: there, every job runs in it anyway), and anything done on another conversation's job (#631) or in a turn that read something untrusted (the harness's taint, above) becomes a `confirm` item, which only the owner's Confirm carries out; the tool then answers what waits, not an error (`<what> waits for your OK: confirm it in your inbox.`). Its title is obid's own words, never the agent's; words the agent added are in `payload`, and `text` shows them as the owner's words and what Oby adds for its agent, `> ` lines, never the agent's quoting fences. `payload` also holds the job's `snapshot` (Inbox item, above): a Confirm after the job changed is refused. So text that agents or other people wrote can never delete, widen or continue a job by itself. Each call lands in the turn (its answer is part of the reply) and in the job's `changes`.

| Tool | Arguments | At once | Waits for the owner's Confirm |
|---|---|---|---|
| `oby_continue_job` | `{id, words?}` | the conversation's own job, in a clean turn | a tainted turn, another conversation's job, or a job in Bypass mode where Bypass isn't the owner's default (`continue_job`) |
| `oby_archive_job` | `{id, archived?}` | always: it's undone by `archived: false` | never |
| `oby_delete_job` | `{id}` | never | always (`delete_job`) |
| `oby_change_job` | `{id, mode?, agent?, account?}` | the job's own mode or a narrower one, or another tool or account, on the conversation's own job in a clean turn | any mode that asks less than the job's (Plan, Ask, Accept edits, Sandbox, Bypass, from narrowest; Bypass for a job in Bypass already changes nothing and waits for nothing); a tainted turn; another conversation's job (`change_job`) |
| `oby_tasks` | `{tasks?}` | always: it's the conversation's own list | never |

- **`oby_continue_job` `{id, words?}`** continues a job that failed, was stopped or needs you (`interrupted`, a stalled one too, with `stalled` set) in its own agent session, worktree and branch, as Try again does (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages`): the agent hears Try again's words, then the owner's words this turn and the agent's `words` (at most 2,000 characters in all), quoted as `oby_tell_job`'s are. The job keeps its `id`, gets a new `round`, and `dismissed` and `archived` are cleared. It counts against the turn's 10 `oby_tell_job` calls. Refused: a job that's queued or running (`It's still running: tell it something with oby_tell_job.`), one that's done (`It's done: oby_tell_job starts a follow-up of it.`), one that never started, or one whose follow-up carries its branch on. To start again from a clean session instead, `oby_tell_job` on the job starts a follow-up that's told what the first job did; to split the work, `oby_dispatch`.
- **`oby_archive_job` `{id, archived?}`** archives a job that ended and needs nothing (`archived`, on the Job above), or unarchives it with `archived: false`, as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/archive` does. A job that needs you is refused (`Dismiss it first, or publish it.`). `oby_jobs` leaves archived jobs out, and lists only them with `archived: true`.
- **`oby_delete_job` `{id}`** always waits for the owner's Confirm (`delete_job`), whose title says what goes: `Delete <title> (its worktree and local branch go)`, or `(its worktree goes; its branch stays, another job uses it)` when the branch is shared, and when it has commits that aren't published, `; <n> commits that aren't published are lost`. Confirmed, it's `DELETE /v1/jobs/{id}`. A job obid would refuse to delete (running, its pull request open, a follow-up carrying its branch on) is refused at once, saying why, with no Confirm.
- **`oby_change_job` `{id, mode?, agent?, account?}`** changes how much the job asks (`mode`: `ask`, `acceptEdits`, `plan`, `sandbox` or `bypass`), as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/mode` does, and obid's rules for modes stay: a review job stays in Plan. A change to another tool (`agent`: `claude`, `codex` or `cursor`) or account (by id or name; `auto` picks as a new job's does) is for a job that ended: it's added to `moves`, its next round starts a new session on its new choice, which hears the task, that Oby moved it, and what it's told, and a model of the old tool gives way to the new tool's own. A running job is refused (`Stop it first, then change it and continue it.`); so is an account that isn't one of that tool's. Every change is a line in the job's `changes`.
- **`oby_tasks` `{tasks?}`** is the conversation's own task list, kept in its file (`home/conversations/<id>.json`, so a restart, a new session or a summarized one keeps it) and shown in the API (`tasks`, obid only: conversations). With `tasks`, the list is replaced whole, as `write_todos` does: at most 50, each `{id?, title, status, job?, note?}`, `title` one line of 1 to 200 characters, `status` `pending`, `in_progress`, `completed`, `cancelled` or `blocked`, `job` a job's id (1 to 40 characters on one line), `note` at most 500 characters (why it's blocked, say); an `id` left out is given (`t1`, `t2`, …). Without, it answers the list. While the list has a task that isn't `completed` or `cancelled`, every turn's prompt starts with it, quoted as data, so the agent keeps it after its session is summarized. A list written in a turn that read something untrusted keeps that with it (in the conversation's file): every turn it's fed to, a new session's too, is treated as one that read something untrusted, and `oby_tasks` answers it quoted, so text an agent or a web page wrote can't come back as the owner's own plan.

### obid only: Sphere membership

The Obi's place in one Sphere: its key, its seal key, its owner-signed agent card, its owner's delegation, and the token it exchanges them for (spec `identity.md`). One Sphere per obid in this version.

**`GET /v1/obid/sphere`** answers the membership:

```json
{"state": "joined", "url": "http://127.0.0.1:8787", "sphere": "e2e", "obiId": "obi-wellington", "owner": "wellington",
 "keyId": "ed25519:…", "publicKey": "…", "sealKeyId": "x25519:…", "sealPublicKey": "…", "sealing": "on",
 "accepts": ["review_request", "question"], "card": { …the signed agent card… },
 "delegation": {"scope": "send:review_request send:question reply receive directory:read audit:read", "expiresAt": "2026-11-13T12:00:00Z"},
 "tokenExpiresAt": "2026-10-14T13:00:05Z", "lastPoll": "2026-10-14T12:00:40Z", "lastError": null}
```

`state` is `none` (no Sphere yet; the other keys null, except `owner`, and `keyId`/`publicKey` once the key exists), `joined` or `revoked` (the Sphere refused its token as revoked). `owner` is who obid acts for in its Sphere: while it keeps a membership, the owner its card names; after it leaves one, the owner it joined as (`home/owner`, 0600, kept across a leave and a restart, since a Sphere refuses that person's rejoin under another id); until it first joins, configuration's `owner`, except that while that is still the Mac's login name (what obid writes at its first start), this call makes it the GitHub login `gh` is signed in as, lower-cased (#1214: a Sphere binds one owner key per owner id, and two people's Macs often share a login name, or have one like `john.doe` that isn't an id). `lastError` is the last thing that went wrong talking to the Sphere, one line, or null. `accepts` is the request types the card accepts. `sealing` is `on` when the card obid holds carries its seal key, so what teammates' Obys send it is sealed for it ("Sealed words", below); `card_needed` when the owner should sign a new card; `waiting` until the Sphere or a teammate can take one with a seal key; null with no membership. A card with a seal key is refused by an obid from before sealing (its schema closes the card's `params`, so that member would vanish for its teammates) and by a Sphere from before it, so obid goes in two steps (spec `identity.md` section 2). Its card's `version` said `0.2.0` from the release that read seal keys (`0.3.0+card-runtime` now, below), and a card from before (`0.1.0`) is `card_needed`: the new one says so. Its seal key goes in a card only once the Sphere's `GET /v0.1/health` says `seal: true` and every other active member's card says `0.2.0` or later (a joining Obi's card never has one); then it's `card_needed` again, and the next card carries it, as does every card after (teammates know this Obi seals, whoever joins later). The Mac app looks every 5 minutes, and seeing `card_needed` signs a new card (`POST /v1/obid/sphere/card`, then `PUT /v1/obid/sphere/membership` with it, the same `accepts`). It signs one the same way when `card`'s `name` isn't the one the owner gave in Oby (`<name>'s Oby`, else `<owner>'s Oby`), at once when they change it; teammates' Macs show that name without its `'s Oby`, and the owner id where they compare keys. Once a team seals, a newcomer's Oby must be this release or later to read its teammates' cards. From this release its card's `version` is `0.3.0+card-runtime`: the build metadata `card-runtime` says, the spec's way, that it reads a card with `features`, `runtime` and `vendors` (spec `identity.md` section 2.3), which a card from before can't list yet. Its card then says what runs it: `features` (the additions obid takes: `["cancel", "card_classes", "card_runtime", "card_topics", "follow_up", "introduction"]`), `runtime` `{"id": "oby", "version": "0.3.0"}` (the version its card says, without the build metadata) and `vendors`, the vendors whose models write what obid sends another member: `anthropic` always (a review, and a question it answers by itself, run on Claude Code), and `openai` too when the conversation runs on Codex, whose answer the owner may confirm. A card that carries them but says other `runtime` or `vendors` than obid would now (the owner changed the conversation's agent) is `card_needed` too. They go in the same two steps as the seal key: a card from before (`0.2.0`) is `card_needed`, to say `0.3.0+card-runtime`; then, once the Sphere's `GET /v0.1/features` lists `card_runtime` (asked only once every other active member's card lists it or says `+card-runtime`, then every 10 minutes until it does; a Sphere from before answers `404`) and every other active member takes it, `card_needed` again, and the next card carries them, as does every card after. The Sphere refuses such a card sooner (`409`). `sealing` is `card_needed` while either step waits on a card, even when the card carries its seal key; the owner's signature declares them, and nothing proves them. An obid from before checks the whole directory against its schema, so once any card carries them it can read no member at all: a newcomer's Oby must be this release or later.

**Once revoked** (the directory lists this Obi `revoked`), nothing more comes in from the Sphere or goes out to it, and what was open is settled here, its peers not told. obid stops following its deliveries, and a request delivered after the revoke (in the same poll, or a poll alongside) is dropped: it reaches neither the inbox nor a remembered decision. It sends nothing more: replies built before stay unsent. Each incoming request still open ends `declined` with no decline built or sent and `error` `obid's membership was revoked: settled here, and its peer wasn't told.`: one in the inbox (its item done, with no `answer`), a question being answered by itself, and an accepted review (its job stopped, and no result built). The clock still runs: what this Obi sent, and anything else still open, expires at its `expires_at` as before, and a decline the clock builds then never goes. An obid that starts with its membership revoked settles what's open the same way. With no membership at all, the sender and the clock don't run.

**`DELETE /v1/obid/sphere`** leaves the Sphere, joined or revoked, so obid can join another or the same one again (#1214): what's open is settled as at a revoke, every exchange's waiting replies are dropped (they were for that Sphere, never the next), `home/sphere` goes (the membership, the delegation, the chain obid saw, the pinned owner keys) and so do the Obi key and the seal key (`keys/obi.key`, `keys/seal.key`), since a Sphere never takes a key or an Obi id twice; the exchanges and the inbox stay, and the phone relay's socket ends. obid can't remove itself from the Sphere's directory: that takes the owner's own token, so the Mac app first calls `DELETE /v0.1/spheres/{sphere}/members/{obi}` with one (scope `directory:read`), when the Sphere answers. Answers the membership, `state` `none`; the same with nothing to leave. A Mac joining with a new open link tries `obi-<owner>`, then `obi-<owner>-2` to `-9` while the Sphere answers that the id was a member before.

**`POST /v1/obid/sphere/key`**, no body: makes the Obi's Ed25519 key, and its X25519 seal key (spec `protocol.md` section 9.1), if there are none, and answers `{"keyId": "ed25519:…", "publicKey": "<base64url>", "sealKeyId": "x25519:…", "sealPublicKey": "<base64url>"}`, the same every time after. They are kept in `home/keys/obi.key` and `home/keys/seal.key` (0600), which no job's sandbox can read; neither private half ever appears in an answer, an event, a log or an agent's reach.

**`POST /v1/obid/sphere/card`**: the card the owner is to sign, built by obid around its own key:

```json
{"url": "http://127.0.0.1:8787", "sphere": "e2e", "obiId": "obi-wellington", "name": "Wellington's Obi",
 "ownerKey": "<owner public key, base64url>", "accepts": ["review_request", "question"]}
```

`accepts` names `review_request`, `question` or both: the request types obid takes in this version (anything else is `422`). `description` is optional (one line; else `A personal delegate for an engineer.`). Answers `{"card": { …the agent card without signatures… }}`: `url` is `<url>/v0.1/spheres/<sphere>/members/<obiId>`, `version` obid's, `card_seq` 1 (one more than the current card's when it has one), `issued_at` now, `skills` one per accepted type, and `seal_key_id` and `seal_public_key` obid's seal key (made here when there is none yet) once the Sphere and every other member can take a card with one, and always once its card carries it (`sealing`, above). It reads the directory first. The owner signs it as the spec says (identity.md 2.1); obid never holds the owner key. `409` before the key exists, `422` for a field that isn't valid. `url` is the Sphere's base URL with nothing after the host and port: `https://`, or plain `http://` only on this machine (`localhost`, `127.x.x.x`, `[::1]`, as the e2e harness's local Sphere), so the delegation and tokens never cross a network in the clear; the same holds for `POST /v1/obid/sphere/join`. obid follows no redirect from a Sphere. Once the Sphere and every other member take `card_runtime` (above, `sealing`), or once the card obid holds lists it, the card also carries `features` (the six above), `runtime` and `vendors`; a card whose `features` list differs from obid's is signed again (`card_needed`); `PUT /v1/obid/sphere/membership` keeps no card that drops `card_runtime` from the one it replaces, or names a runtime other than `oby` (`422`).

**`POST /v1/obid/sphere/join`**: joins with an invitation:

```json
{"url": "http://127.0.0.1:8787", "sphere": "e2e",
 "invitation": {"id": "<invitation_id>", "secret": "<secret>"},
 "card": { …the card, signed by the owner… },
 "delegation": "<owner assertion JWT with may_act>"}
```

For an invitation limited to a Google Workspace (obi-sphere spec `sphere-api.md` section 4, an open invitation's `domain`), `invitation` also carries the owner's sign-in with the Sphere, `"signin": {"state", "code"}`, which the Mac app gets by signing the owner in with Google for the owner key (section 3.1). obid passes it to the redeem as it is, and keeps it nowhere; anything else in its place is `422`.

obid checks the card (identity.md 2.2, checks 1 to 5 and 7), that it carries obid's own `key_id` and `public_key` (and obid's own seal key, when it carries one: a card without one joins, with `sealing` `card_needed`), `sphere` and `obiId` as given and `owner` its configured owner; and the delegation (signed by the card's `owner_key`, `iss` and `sub` the owner, `aud` `obi-sphere:<sphere>` or the Obysphere's own URL `<origin>/v0.1/spheres/<sphere>`, `may_act.sub` its `obi_id`, `exp` ahead, `scope` including `receive` and `reply`). Then it redeems the invitation (`POST /v0.1/spheres/{sphere}/invitations/{id}/redeem`), exchanges the delegation and an actor assertion of its own for a token (`POST /v0.1/oauth/token`; the assertion's `aud` is the Obysphere's own URL when its `GET /v0.1/features` lists `sphere_url_aud` (read again at most every 10 minutes while obid runs, an answer that fails counting as none), so the assertion names the server too, and `obi-sphere:<sphere>` otherwise, or, once the Obysphere has refused its URL and taken that instead, from then on while obid runs; a token whose `token_type` isn't `Bearer` is refused), reads the directory, and starts following its deliveries (`GET …/deliveries?wait=25`, again as soon as each answers; a new token when less than 60 seconds are left; after an error, again after 1 second, doubling to 30). It keeps the card and the delegation in `home` (0600) and never shows the delegation again. Answers `200` with the membership, `state` `joined`.

`409` when already joined; `422` with why for a card or delegation that fails a check; `502` `{"error": "The Sphere refused it: invitation_invalid: …"}` when the Sphere answers an error (its `code` and `message`, quoted); `504` when the Sphere can't be reached.

**`GET /v1/obid/sphere/members`**: the other active members of the Sphere whose cards obid trusts (its pinned owner keys, below), for picking whom to ask (oby-mcp's `sphere_members`, docs/mcp.md): `[{"obi": "obi-ernesto", "owner": "ernesto", "accepts": ["question", "review_request"], "sealed": true, "verified": false}]`, sorted by owner. `accepts` is what the member's card takes, `sealed` whether obid seals its words for it (its card has a seal key), `verified` whether the owner compared its owner's key. Typed fields only, never a card or a key. The directory is read fresh for every call, so a teammate who just joined shows; when the Sphere can't be reached, the last read answers and `lastError` says why. `409` before joining.

**Pinned owner keys** (spec identity.md, "Pinning, in every Obi"). obid trusts a member's card, and so its Obi key and a job channel's hello, only when the card verifies with the owner key obid pinned for that owner the first time it saw one (`home/sphere/pins.json`, 0600), whatever key the Sphere bound. The Obi key obid uses for a member is the one its card signs: a member whose current key in the directory isn't the card's (`key_id` and `public_key`) is left out, and logged; keys the Sphere lists with a `valid_until_seq` are used only when a card obid trusted once signed them (obid keeps every such key, by Obi, in `pins.json`), and only to check old chain entries: a live message is checked with the sender's current key alone. A member whose owner key changes is left out of the directory obid uses (its messages dropped, no channel) until the owner compares the new key's fingerprint with that person and accepts it. A member's seal key is the one its trusted card carries, so a Sphere can't swap or strip it either. **`GET /v1/obid/sphere/pins`**: `[{"owner", "fingerprint": "ed25519:…", "firstSeen", "changedTo": "ed25519:…" | null, "verified"}]`; `verified` is false for a key trusted on first sight, until the owner compares it. **`POST /v1/obid/sphere/pins/{owner}`** with `{"fingerprint": "<the new key's, as compared>"}` trusts the changed key, or with the pinned key's own fingerprint verifies it (`422` for any other fingerprint), and reads the directory again. The Mac asks the owner first whether that person read them this fingerprint.

**`PUT /v1/obid/sphere/membership`** with `{"card"}`, `{"delegation"}` or both, signed by the owner: a new card (a higher `card_seq`, the same Obi, key, Sphere and owner, checked as at joining, its seal key obid's when it has one; it goes to the Sphere first, `PUT …/members/{obi}/card`) and/or a new delegation (checked as at joining). The Mac app sends a card with obid's seal key when `sealing` is `card_needed`, a card that accepts `run_request` when the owner turns sharing on, and a delegation with `send:run_request` so the owner's jobs can go to a teammate's Mac. `409` before joining, `422` with why, `502` with the Sphere's refusal.

**Renewing the delegation.** `GET /v1/obid/sphere`'s `delegation` is the scope of the delegation obid holds and when it ends (its `exp`), never the JWT, or null. A delegation lasts 30 days, so the Mac app signs a new one with the same scope and sends it here at launch, and every 6 hours once 5 or fewer days are left (#1215).

**`GET /v1/obid/sphere/audit`** (#810): the Sphere's audit chain, read whole and checked here against the directory's keys (spec `sphere-api.md` 7.1), and this Obi's own entries in it, as typed fields only: `{"verified": true, "problem": null, "head": {"seq", "hash"}, "entries": [{"seq", "recordedAt", "type", "from", "to", "correlationId", "repo", "reason", "state", "outcome", "costCents", "grant", "share", "usedCents", "limitCents"}]}` (each field only when the message has it). No message's text, ever. It also keeps (`home/sphere/chain.json`) the last head it verified and the seq and hash the Sphere answered for each of its own sends, and answers `"verified": false` with `"problem": "rollback"` for a chain shorter than that head, or `"fork"` for one with another hash there or without one of its sends (spec sphere-api.md 7.1), and `"head_mismatch"` when the head the Sphere names isn't the chain's last entry. It reads at most 100,000 entries (`502` past that). Every member reads the whole chain, costs included: it is the Sphere's common record. `409` before joining, `502`/`504` from the Sphere. Owner and teammate each check the entries about them this way; a Sphere admin exports the chain with the Sphere's own `GET …/audit`.

### obid only: Obi-to-Obi exchanges

An **exchange** is one request and its replies (spec `protocol.md`, "Exchanges"), as this Obi sees it.

**`POST /v1/obid/sphere/requests`**: the owner's Obi asks another Obi:

```json
{"to": "obi-wellington", "type": "review_request", "body": { …the action body… }, "expiresIn": 3600}
```

`type` is `review_request`, `question` or `handoff` (obid reads an `introduction` and a request's `follows`, but sends neither in this version: `422`); `body` must match the spec's schema for it (a question is `{"text": …, "about": [refs], "choices": […]}`, `about` and `choices` optional; to ask several Obis, send one question to each: each is its own exchange, answered on its own); `to` must be an active member whose card accepts `type`; `expiresIn` is 60 to 2,592,000 seconds. Every text field of `body` goes through the secret scan before obid seals, signs or sends: a finding, or a scanner that can't run, is `422` and nothing is sent. obid builds the envelope (a new UUID for `id` and `correlation_id`, `created_at` now, `expires_at` now + `expiresIn`, its `key_id`), seals its words when `to`'s card has a seal key ("Sealed words", below), signs it and sends it (`POST /v0.1/spheres/{sphere}/messages`). Answers `202` with the exchange, `state` `sent`. `422` with why for a body, recipient, expiry or secret scan that isn't valid (nothing sent); `409` when not joined; `502` with the Sphere's code and message when it refuses (`policy_denied`, …), and no exchange is kept.

Only the owner sends requests, through this endpoint (from the apps, or the e2e harness). Agents can't reach it: their tokens answer only `/v1/agent/…`.

**`POST /v1/obid/sphere/council`**: the owner's Obi convenes a Council review panel (obi-ai-bot/oby-sphere `spec/council/review.md`, section 5): one `review_request` per seat, the same for every seat.

```json
{"seats": ["obi-ernesto", "obi-maria", "obi-tomas"], "review": { …a review_request body… }, "expiresAt": "2026-10-14T14:00:00Z"}
```

Only with `"council": true` in the configuration: `403` otherwise. `seats` is 1 to 15 different obi ids, never this Obi's own (the convener never sits on its own panel). Who sits is the draw's (`spec/council/draw.md`), made outside obid in this version: obid sends what it's given. `review` must match the spec's `review_request` and must not carry `approve_on_host`: a panel never approves on the code host in a person's place. `expiresAt` is the motion's `deadlines.reviews`, 60 to 2,592,000 seconds from now: every seat's request expires at that same instant.

Everything obid can check is checked before anything is sent, so none of it leaves a seat out: the review's words pass the secret scan once (`422` for a finding or a scanner that can't run), and every seat must be an active member whose card accepts `review_request`, whose seal key obid trusts when it seals (`409` otherwise), that isn't this Obi's owner's, and no two seats may share an owner (`422`). A directory that can't be read is `502`/`504`. Then each seat's request is sealed for that seat when its card has a seal key, signed and sent as `POST /v1/obid/sphere/requests` sends one, and is its own exchange, so no seat sees another's request or result, and obid never passes one seat's result to another. A seat obid already sent this motion's request (the same seat, repository, `head_sha` and `expiresAt`) is answered with that exchange, never sent a second request, so a repeated call is safe: it checks and sends only the seats not sent yet, and refuses (`409`, nothing sent) a seat whose request for the motion had other words. A seat may not share an owner with any seat already sent the motion's request, by this call or an earlier one. One call runs at a time, and it runs to the end even when the caller hangs up. Answers `202` with `{"seats": [{"seat", "exchange"} | {"seat", "status", "error"}]}`, in the order given. A seat whose send failed on the way says why and the others still go: `422`, `409`, `502` (the Obysphere refusing, `429` included), `504` (it couldn't be reached: the Obysphere may have recorded the request all the same, so obid keeps that seat's exchange, its `error` saying it isn't confirmed, and the envelope as signed; calling again posts the same bytes, which an Obysphere that recorded them answers with its first receipt, never a second request. After obid restarts, look for that seat's request in the chain instead) or `500` (obid couldn't seal or sign). When none went, the first seat's error. `409` when not joined or without an Obi key. The exchanges are listed as any other (below); counting the panel's verdict is the clerk's, from the chain.

**`GET /v1/obid/sphere/exchanges?limit=50`** lists exchanges, the latest first (`limit` 1 to 500). **`GET /v1/obid/sphere/exchanges/{correlationId}`** answers one, `404` for one obid doesn't have:

```json
{"correlationId": "5f0e8c1a-…", "direction": "incoming", "type": "review_request",
 "peer": "obi-ernesto", "peerOwner": "ernesto", "state": "done",
 "decidedBy": "inbox", "rule": null, "inboxItem": "obi-5f0e8c1a-…", "job": "9f08fd10",
 "publishScan": {"ran": true, "clean": true},
 "messages": [
   {"direction": "received", "seq": 1, "at": "2026-10-14T12:00:11Z", "envelope": { …review_request… }},
   {"direction": "sent", "seq": 2, "at": "2026-10-14T12:00:15Z", "envelope": { …accept… }},
   {"direction": "sent", "seq": 3, "at": "2026-10-14T12:00:19Z", "envelope": { …result… }}],
 "result": { …the result body… }, "error": null,
 "created": "2026-10-14T12:00:11Z", "updated": "2026-10-14T12:00:19Z"}
```

- `direction`: `outgoing` (this Obi asked) or `incoming` (it was asked). `peer` and `peerOwner`: the other Obi and its owner, from the Sphere's directory.
- `state`: outgoing `sent`, `accepted`, `done` (a `result` came, or for a question an `answer`: either ends the exchange), `declined` or `expired` (neither by the request's `expires_at`); incoming `inbox` (waiting for the owner), `answering` (a question the Obi is answering by itself, below), `accepted` (working), `done` (result or answer sent), `declined` (decline sent, `busy` included; or settled here with none when obid's membership was revoked, `error` saying so: "Once revoked", above), `expired` (it expired before an answer), `failed` (the work or the publish gate failed: a result with outcome `failed` and no text was sent) or `cancelled` (its sender called it off, spec protocol.md section 5.17: its job stopped, its inbox item settled, nothing more sent, and an approval obid had posted on GitHub for it dismissed).
- `decidedBy`: on incoming, `inbox` (the owner answered), `rule` (a remembered decision, `rule` its id) or `sharing` (a question the Obi answers by itself under a sharing rule, `rule` that rule's id), null until decided. `inboxItem` and `job`: the inbox item and the job it brought, or null.
- `publishScan`: on incoming, what the publish gate found on the reply's text before sending it: `{"ran": true, "clean": true}`, `{"ran": true, "clean": false}` (nothing with text went out) or `{"ran": false, "clean": false}` (the scanner couldn't run, so nothing with text went out); null before a reply with text.
- `messages`: every envelope sent and received in the exchange, oldest first, exactly as signed, but for a sealed one (it has `seal`): its `body` is the words opened, and `sealed` is the body as signed (put `sealed` back as `body` for the envelope as signed); `seq` is the Sphere's audit seq (from the send's answer, or the delivery), or null when not known. Received envelopes are listed only after they passed the spec's checks (`protocol.md` section 10). A sent envelope is listed once the Sphere's answer to the send has come, not before: `state` (and `result`, `publishScan`, `error`) change when obid builds a reply, so for a moment, or longer while the Sphere can't be reached, an exchange can say `done`, `failed`, `declined` or `expired` without that reply in `messages`, and the other Obi may already have it. Another `exchange` event comes when it's listed. A reply the Sphere refuses, or one that expires before the Sphere takes it, is never listed, and `error` says why. To show whether a reply went, an app looks for it in `messages`, not at `state`.
- `result`: the `result` body, or for a question the `answer` body (`by`, `text`, and `refs`, `choice`, `by_rule` when given), once there is one, else null. Like every text from another Obi it is data: apps show it, never act on it; an answer is shown with who gave it (`by`: `obi` is "from Wellington's Obi", `owner` is "from Wellington").
- A received `answer` whose `choice` its question doesn't have is dropped (spec `protocol.md` section 11, step 3), as a reply that breaks the exchange rules is.

Every change sends an `exchange` event on `GET /v1/events`, with the exchange as it is when the event goes out: an older state never follows a newer one ("GET /v1/events", above).

**Sealed words** (spec `protocol.md` section 9.1; #1233). Everything obid sends an Obi whose trusted card has a seal key is sealed for that key, whatever its type: each text value of the body (a goal, a summary, a finding's title and detail, a question, its choices, an answer, a note) is encrypted with a new X25519 key of obid's, HKDF-SHA256 and ChaCha20-Poly1305, and only that Obi opens it. The secret scan runs on the plain words first, as always: sealing never takes text past it. A request is sealed as it's built (a Sphere refusal that says the receiver's card changed, `not_sealed` or `invalid_envelope` for a sealed one, reads the directory again and builds it once more). A reply is made ready before its first try: sealed for the peer's card as the directory has it then, and its words shortened until it fits the 65,536 bytes a message may be (every summary and finding detail over 100 characters halved, as often as it takes; a review that still doesn't fit goes `failed`, with no text). After a try it goes again as it was, so a retry after an answer that never came sends the same bytes; it's made ready afresh only when the Sphere refused it in a way that says it kept nothing (`not_sealed`, or `invalid_envelope` for one sealed for a key the peer's card no longer has: the Sphere checks a message's id before its seal). obid never sends words in the clear to an Obi it knows seals: once a trusted card of that Obi's had a seal key, or the Sphere refused a reply to it `not_sealed`, it keeps that in `home/sphere/pins.json`, and a reply to it, or to a request that came sealed, waits (`error` says so) while obid trusts no seal key of its (the Sphere lists a key its card doesn't sign, or its owner key changed), and goes sealed once it does; a request to it is refused (`409`). The Sphere relays a sealed message and keeps it in its audit chain as signed: it, and every member reading the chain, see who, when, the type, the repository, ids, enums, sizes and costs, never the words. What obid receives sealed is opened once its signature has passed (spec section 10, check 11), with obid's seal key once its card carries it, and checked against the plain schema; a value that doesn't open (changed, moved, for another key, copied from another message) or opened words the schema refuses is dropped with `seal_invalid`, its code alone logged, and never answered. Words in the clear from an Obi whose card has a seal key, while obid's card has one too, are dropped with `not_sealed`, unless they were written (`created_at`) before both cards had one: a message sent while the receiver's or the sender's card had none is taken. Between an Obi that seals and one whose card has no seal key yet, words go in the clear: the second can't be sealed for, or seal. The inbox's `obi.quoted`, the files an agent reads under `.obi/inputs/`, the result and the brain's briefing read the opened words, as before. The audit listing (`GET /v1/obid/sphere/audit`) shows typed fields only, sealed or not. A Sphere that doesn't know sealed messages yet refuses them (`400 invalid_envelope`), and the exchange says so: the Sphere's deploy goes first.

### obid only: Obi-to-Obi requests in the inbox

An incoming request that no remembered decision covers (spec `protocol.md` section 11) waits in the owner's inbox as an item of kind **`obiRequest`**, one per exchange:

```json
{"id": "obi-5f0e8c1a-…", "kind": "obiRequest", "job": "", "title": "Review request from ernesto",
 "text": "ernesto's Obi (obi-ernesto) asks for a review of obi-e2e/fixture-app: feature/average into main at 6d20146. Conditions: tests pass, no blocker findings.",
 "options": ["Accept", "Accept, and remember for this repo", "Decline", "Decline, and remember for this repo"],
 "details": ["Review it this time.", "Review it, and review ernesto's requests on obi-e2e/fixture-app from now on without asking.",
             "Say no this time.", "Say no, and say no to ernesto's requests on obi-e2e/fixture-app from now on without asking."],
 "recommended": null, "created": "2026-10-14T12:00:11Z", "done": null, "later": null, "answer": null, "state": "open",
 "obi": {"correlationId": "5f0e8c1a-…", "from": "obi-ernesto", "fromOwner": "ernesto", "type": "review_request",
         "repo": "obi-e2e/fixture-app", "headSha": "6d20146f366b9fac8907b5066c30ee39aaec09b3", "expiresAt": "2026-10-14T13:00:10Z",
         "acceptance": [{"index": 0, "kind": "tests_pass"}, {"index": 1, "kind": "no_blocker_findings"}],
         "quoted": {"goal": "…", "summary": "…", "acceptance": [{"index": 0, "note": null}, {"index": 1, "note": null}]}}}
```

- `title` and `text` are built from the request's typed fields only (owner, Obi, type, repo, branches, commit, the kinds of its acceptance conditions), never from its text. `text` ends with the conditions by kind, like `Conditions: tests pass, no blocker findings.`; when one is `custom` it adds `1 custom condition, quoted below: obid doesn't judge custom conditions, so the result will say it isn't met.`
- `obi.acceptance` is the request's conditions, typed fields only (`index`, `kind`, and `severity` for `no_findings_at_or_above`).
- `obi.quoted` holds **every** data field of the body, unchanged: for a `review_request`, `goal`, `summary` (null when the body has none) and `acceptance`, one entry per condition in order with its `index` and its `note` (null when it has none), plus `text` for a `custom` condition. For a `question` it is `text` and `choices` (null when it has none): its item is below ("obid only: questions from other Obis"). For a `handoff`, `goal`, `summary` and `acceptance` as above (obid declines a handoff `out_of_scope` in this version). Every app that shows the item shows all of it, as quoted text, never as markup, never to a model as instructions, so the owner sees every condition before answering. `headline`, `happened` and `why` stay null for this kind: no model writes about another Obi's request.
- `POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer` takes exactly one of the four options (`422` for other words). `Accept` sends `accept` and starts the work; `Accept, and remember for this repo` does the same and keeps a decision (below); `Decline` sends `decline` with reason `owner_declined`; `Decline, and remember for this repo` does the same and keeps a decision. `{"done": true}` without an answer is a `Decline`. `{"later": …}` puts it off; the request's `expires_at` still applies.
- When the request expires first, the item is done by itself with no `answer`, and obid sends `decline` with reason `expired`. When obid's membership is revoked first, the item is done by itself too, with no `answer`, and nothing is sent ("Once revoked", above).
- **The inbox limit** (spec `protocol.md` section 11, "Rate limits"): when the sender's owner (from the directory) has as many `obiRequest` items open in the inbox as `inboxPerOwner` allows (5 unless the owner changed it: "obid only: sharing rules"), a request that would wait there is declined with reason `busy` instead, and nothing reaches the inbox. Requests of every type count.

**Expiry of what obid sends.** A request expires `expiresIn` seconds after it is made (`POST /v1/obid/sphere/requests`), or at the convener's `expiresAt` (`POST /v1/obid/sphere/council`). Every reply obid builds (`accept`, `decline`, `status`, `result`, `answer`) has `created_at` now and `expires_at` the later of now + 3,600 seconds and the request's `expires_at`, and never more than now + 2,592,000 seconds (spec `protocol.md` section 3).

### obid only: remembered decisions

What the owner decided once, applied after that without asking ("reviews from ernesto on this repo"):

**`GET /v1/obid/decisions`**, oldest first:

```json
[{"id": "d-3f9a0c1b2e4d", "type": "review_request", "fromOwner": "ernesto", "repo": "obi-e2e/fixture-app",
  "decision": "accept", "created": "2026-10-14T12:00:15Z", "source": {"inboxItem": "obi-5f0e8c1a-…"},
  "uses": 1, "lastUsed": "2026-10-14T12:00:30Z"}]
```

`id` is `d-` and 12 lower-case hex. `repo` is the exact repository, or `*` for every repository (only a question's decline makes one: below). `decision` is `accept` or `decline`. `uses` counts the requests it decided without asking (the answer that made it isn't one), `lastUsed` the latest, or null. A request is covered by a decision with the same `type`, the sender's owner (from the Sphere's directory, never from the message) and its `repo`; an exact repository beats `*`. Only an inbox answer makes a decision; nothing another Obi sends can.

A question has no `repo` (spec `protocol.md` 5.2 and section 11, step 5): its repositories are those its `about` references name, compared ignoring ASCII case, a `job` reference counting only for a job that is obid's own and runs in the repository it names (5.9; else it names nothing obid knows). `Decline, and remember for this repo` on a question keeps a `decline` with `type` `question` for **each** of its repositories, or one for `*` when it is about none. A later question from the same owner is declined by them only when **each** repository it is about has one (its own, or `*`); one about no repository only by a `*` one. `by_rule` names the first repository's. Only a decline applies to a question: what lets obid answer one by itself is a sharing rule (below), never a remembered accept.


A standing permission to approve a teammate's pull requests on GitHub ([teammate approval](#obid-only-teammate-approval)) is listed here too, with `type` `host_approval`, `fromOwner` the teammate's owner id (from the directory), `repo` the exact repository or `*`, `decision` `accept`, and `uses` counting the approvals it let obid post. It lets obid approve by itself only next to approvals of that commit by the people who own every file it changes ([teammate approval](#obid-only-teammate-approval)); otherwise obid asks the owner each time. It is kept only on the owner's own tap on a `confirm` item (#776), never by an inbox answer or an API token alone, and nothing another Oby sends makes, widens or renews one.

**`POST /v1/obid/decisions`** asks for one: `{"type": "host_approval", "fromOwner": "ernesto", "repo": "obi-ai-bot/tetrix"}` (`repo` may be `*`). It keeps nothing: it answers `202` with a `confirm` item, `From now on, approve on GitHub the pull requests ernesto's Oby asks you to on obi-ai-bot/tetrix, when your Oby's review passes and their code owners approved` (`… on any repository …` for `*`), whose Confirm keeps it (action `remember_approval`, payload `{"fromOwner", "repo"}`). `422` for any other `type` (every other decision comes from an answer in the inbox), a `fromOwner` that isn't an owner id, or a `repo` that isn't `owner/name` or `*`. Loopback only, like the rest of `/v1/obid/decisions`. The Mac app doesn't list decisions yet: they're forgotten through the API (#1394).

**`DELETE /v1/obid/decisions/{id}`** forgets one: `204`, `404` for one obid doesn't have.

### obid only: questions from other Obis

A **question** (spec `protocol.md` 5.2) asks for words: obid answers it, or its owner does, with an **`answer`** (5.8) that ends the exchange. Nothing else ever leaves because of a question, and nothing in one can make obid run, change or publish anything: it never starts a job, pushes, merges, changes a setting, a sharing rule or a decision because of one. Its card must accept `question` (`POST /v1/obid/sphere/card`) or obid declines one `out_of_scope`.

When one arrives (after the checks every request gets, spec section 11, steps 1 to 4):

1. **A remembered decline** that covers it ("obid only: remembered decisions", repository by repository): `decline` with reason `owner_declined` and its `by_rule`.
2. **The same question twice**: one with the same `text` and `about` as a question from the same owner that is still `inbox` or `answering` is declined `busy`.
3. **By itself, under a sharing rule** ("obid only: sharing rules"): the oldest rule for the sender's owner (from the directory, never from the message) that covers each repository the question is about (one about none: a rule for every repository), while that owner and every sender together are under the self-answer limits. The exchange is `answering`, `decidedBy` `sharing`, `rule` the rule's id, and the question counts against both limits there and then, whatever the run ends in (an answer, the owner's inbox, a decline). At most 2 such runs go at once, whoever asked; one waits for its turn. When its turn comes, and again before its answer goes, obid checks that the question is still `answering` and unexpired, that no remembered decline covers it now (the owner may have said "Decline, and remember" meanwhile: then it is declined `owner_declined` with that decision's `by_rule`, and nothing the run wrote goes), and that the rule is still there and still covers it (else nothing the run wrote goes, and the question goes to the owner). obid gives the run what the rule allows, and nothing else (below). It answers `{"by": "obi", "text": …, "refs": […], "choice": …, "by_rule": "<rule id>"}` (`refs` and `choice` only when there are some) once the publish gate passes: a possible secret, or a scanner that can't run, declines it with reason `other` and no text (`publishScan` says which). A model that says it can't answer from the material, that fails, is slow (90 s) or writes something obid refuses, and an obid restart during the run, send the question to the owner instead (step 4).
4. **The owner's inbox** (or `busy`, past the inbox limit): an `obiRequest` item, below. obid sends no `status` while it waits.

**The model run** (spec section 11, "Questions"). On the owner's first Claude Code account (a copy of its `configDir`'s login file without the refresh token, never the folder itself nor the Keychain's login, or a copy of the tool's own login as a job gets, removed when the run ends, and at obid's next start if a run was cut short), in a new folder of its own under `home/answers/` that holds two files and is removed after: `question.json`, `{"quoted", "fromOwner", "question": <the body exactly as received>}`, and `material.json`, `{"quoted", "items": […]}`. The question reaches the model only in that file, never in its prompt or arguments: the system prompt and the prompt are fixed, and say what the files are. It runs `-p --model haiku --tools Read --permission-mode default --restricted --permission-prompts none --strict-mcp-config --setting-sources "" --no-session-persistence --max-turns 4 --output-format json --json-schema <schema> --system-prompt <fixed>`. Two layers keep it to that folder, each holding alone:

- **The system: the job sandbox, confined.** The job sandbox ("obid only: Obi-to-Obi review jobs": writes only to its own folders, nothing of obid's `home` or the person's home, no vault secret, no code-host login, no API token), and on top of it **no file's contents but the system's software and the run's own**: on macOS a Seatbelt rule that denies every read, then allows `/System`, `/usr`, `/bin`, `/sbin`, `/Library/Apple`, `/private/etc`, the time zones and the dynamic linker's caches, `/private/var/select`, `/private/var/run/resolv.conf`, `/dev`, the folder `/private/tmp/claude-<uid>` itself (Claude Code opens it whatever `TMPDIR` says; never what is in it), the tools on `PATH` with their install prefixes, Claude Code's own files, the run's folders and its account (every path's metadata stays readable, so paths resolve); on Linux bubblewrap binds only `/usr`, `/bin`, `/sbin`, `/lib*`, `/etc`, `/opt`, `/nix` and those same paths, never `/`. So `/Users/Shared`, `/Volumes`, `/private/var/folders`, `/tmp`, `/mnt` and every other world-readable folder are out of reach. `the_model_reads_nothing_outside_its_folder` shows it on macOS with a process that obeys the question (no permission prompt to stop it); on Linux `a_run_with_the_network_resolves_a_host_name` runs the confined form and shows it resolves the model API's host and sees nothing of `/run` but the one resolver file bound there.
- **Claude Code.** Read is its only tool, `--restricted` confines its file tools to the working directory, `--permission-prompts none` refuses anything that would ask, and there is no `--allowedTools`. This is the layer that keeps the model out of its account folder (with the sign-in that Claude Code itself must read, so the system lets the process read it) on every platform. Tested by hand with Claude Code 2.1.296 and a model told to read a file outside its folder: refused, `is outside` the working directory. A Claude Code without `--restricted` fails to start the run, and the question goes to the owner. It returns `{"answerable", "text", "refs", "choice"}` (`text` at most 2,000 characters, `refs` indexes into the material, `choice` an index or null). obid cleans the text as `block_text` (CR LF and CR become LF; other control, DEL and bidirectional characters removed; trimmed), keeps only `refs` that name material items (each once) and a `choice` the question has, and checks the answer against the spec's schema. The material is all the model may draw on:

- `jobStatus`: obid's own jobs in the repositories the question is about that the rule covers (all the rule covers, for a question about none), the jobs its `about` names first, then the latest, 20 at most: `{"index", "kind": "job", "ref": {"kind": "job", "repo", "job"}, "title", "stage", "state", "pr"}`. Never a job's logs, diff, prompt, files or agents' words, and never a review another Obi asked for (that is someone else's request).
- `sharedResults`: the results obid already sent that owner's Obis for reviews they asked for, in those repositories, the latest 10: `{"index", "kind": "sharedResult", "ref": {"kind": "repo", "repo"}, "type", "result"}`.
- Never anything about a repository the owner said no to that owner's questions about (a remembered decline for it, "obid only: remembered decisions"), even under a rule for every repository.

**The question's inbox item** is an `obiRequest`, like a review request's, with `title` `Question from <owner>` and `text` built from typed fields only: `ernesto's Obi (obi-ernesto) asks a question about obi-e2e/fixture-app pull request #251, quoted below. Answer it in your own words, or say no.` (`about` in words: a repository, `<repo> pull request #N`, `<repo> issue #N`, `<repo> job <id>` for a job that is obid's own in that repository, while a job reference obid couldn't check is left out of `text` and stays only under `obi.about`; without `about`, `asks a question, quoted below.`):

```json
{"id": "obi-5f0e8c1a-…", "kind": "obiRequest", "job": "", "title": "Question from ernesto", "text": "…",
 "options": ["Decline", "Decline, and remember for this repo"],
 "details": ["Say no this time.", "Say no, and say no to ernesto's questions about obi-e2e/fixture-app from now on without asking."],
 "obi": {"correlationId": "5f0e8c1a-…", "from": "obi-ernesto", "fromOwner": "ernesto", "type": "question",
         "about": [{"kind": "pr", "repo": "obi-e2e/fixture-app", "pr": 251}], "repos": ["obi-e2e/fixture-app"],
         "expiresAt": "2026-10-14T13:00:10Z", "quoted": {"text": "…", "choices": ["Yes", "No"]}}, …}
```

- `obi.about` is the question's references as sent (typed; null when it has none), `obi.repos` the repositories obid counted (above). The question's words and its `choices` are only under `obi.quoted`: choices are data, so they are never `options`; an app may show them as buttons that send their words.
- `POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer`: `Decline` (or `{"done": true}`) sends `decline` with reason `owner_declined`; `Decline, and remember for this repo` does the same and keeps a decline per repository (`details` says so: for a question about none, `Say no, and say no to every question from ernesto from now on without asking.`). **Any other words are the owner's answer**: 1 to 2,000 characters, sent as `{"by": "owner", "text": <the words>}`, with `choice` when the words are exactly one of the question's choices (a choice that is literally `Decline` or `Decline, and remember for this repo` reads as that button). They must make an answer the spec's schema allows, and they pass the publish gate first: either failing is `422` with why (never repeating a possible secret), nothing is sent, and the item stays open. `{"later": …}` puts it off; the question's `expires_at` still applies.

**What obid sends in a question's exchange.** One `answer` or one `decline`, nothing before it: no `accept` (allowed, never needed) and no `status`.

### obid only: sharing rules

What the owner lets obid tell some people without being asked, and how much each sender's owner can make it do (spec `protocol.md` section 11, "Questions" and "Rate limits"). Only the owner changes them, here; nothing another Obi sends can, and agents can't reach these endpoints. Kept in `home/sharing.json` (0600).

**`GET /v1/obid/sharing`**:

```json
{"rules": [{"id": "s-3f9a0c1b2e4d", "for": ["ernesto"], "repos": ["obi-e2e/fixture-app"], "tells": ["jobStatus"],
            "created": "2026-10-16T12:00:00Z", "uses": 1, "lastUsed": "2026-10-16T12:05:00Z"}],
 "limits": {"selfAnswersPerHour": 30, "selfAnswersPerHourTotal": 60, "inboxPerOwner": 5}}
```

**`POST /v1/obid/sharing/rules`** with `{"for": …, "repos": …, "tells": […]}`, nothing else, adds a rule after the others and answers it, `201`:

- `for`: owners from the Sphere's directory (`["ernesto"]`, 1 to 50), or `"*"` for every member.
- `repos`: repositories (`owner/name`, 1 to 50, compared ignoring ASCII case), or `"*"` for every one.
- `tells`: some of `jobStatus` (the title, stage and state of the owner's jobs, and the pull request each opened) and `sharedResults` (what obid already sent that person's Obis). The spec's third, published pull requests as the code host shows them, needs the code host, so obid doesn't offer it yet.

`id` is `s-` and 12 lower-case hex, a `rule_id` the answer names in `by_rule`; `uses` counts the answers sent under it. `400` for a body that isn't JSON, `422` with why for one that isn't a rule; `409` past 100 rules. A question already being answered under a rule that is deleted or narrowed meanwhile goes to the owner instead. **`DELETE /v1/obid/sharing/rules/{id}`** forgets one: `204`, `404` for one obid doesn't have.

**`PUT /v1/obid/sharing/limits`** with exactly `{"selfAnswersPerHour": 0 to 1,000, "selfAnswersPerHourTotal": 0 to 10,000, "inboxPerOwner": 0 to 100}` answers the limits; `400` for a body that isn't JSON, `422` otherwise. A sender's owner comes from the directory (an owner with several Obis counts once):

- `selfAnswersPerHour` (30 unless changed): questions from one sender's owner in the last hour that obid took on to answer by itself, each counted when obid takes it on, whatever its run ends in (an answer, the owner's inbox, a decline). Past it a question goes to the owner, as one no rule allows. A question no rule covers goes straight to the owner and doesn't count.
- `selfAnswersPerHourTotal` (60 unless changed): the same, every sender together, so many senders can't spend the owner's account either. Past it a question goes to the owner too.
- Both count across a restart: each exchange keeps when obid took it on.
- `inboxPerOwner` (5 unless changed): requests of every type open in the inbox at once ("obid only: Obi-to-Obi requests in the inbox").

### obid only: Obi-to-Obi review jobs

What obid does when it accepts a `review_request` (by the owner's answer or a decision):

1. It starts a job: `kind` `review`, `repo` the request's, `title` like `Review for ernesto: fixture-app`, and two keys only obid sends on jobs: `obi` (`{"correlationId", "from", "fromOwner", "requestId"}`, null on other jobs) and `worktree` (the job's worktree, an absolute path, null before it exists; the gateway nulls it). It runs on an account Routing picks.
2. It fetches `head_branch` and `head_sha` from the repository's `remote` into its clone (whose push URL is invalid, as the runner's is), and makes a fresh worktree, `home/worktrees/<job id>`, detached at `head_sha`. When `head_sha` can't be fetched, the job fails. So does a request with `approve_on_host` whose `base_branch` doesn't fetch, or that obid can't compare `head_sha` with (`Couldn't fetch the base branch <base> from <repo>, so obid didn't review a change it was asked to approve: …`): its approval would cover what the review couldn't see. When the commit holds a symbolic link that leads out of the worktree, followed as the system would open it (`x -> ../../accounts`, `x -> /`, an absolute path, a chain that ends outside, or one obid can't follow), the job fails before the agent starts, naming the link (`The branch commits a link that leads out of the review's worktree ("x"), so obid didn't review it: …`): Claude Code reads inside its working folder without asking, so a link out would let the agent read what the job sandbox lets it read, its own account folder included. A link that leads to a file inside the worktree, existing or not, is fine.
3. It writes the request as data to `.obi/inputs/obi-request.json` in the worktree, and the change as data to `.obi/inputs/change.diff` (what `head_sha` changes against the fetched `base_branch` from their merge base, the files it touches listed first: `git --no-replace-objects diff --no-color --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --find-renames --patch-with-stat --src-prefix=a/ --dst-prefix=b/ origin/<base_branch>...<head_sha>`, cut at 8 MiB with a line saying so; when the base didn't fetch, a line saying obid couldn't compare), and the repository's rules as the base has them, quoted, to `.obi/inputs/base-rules.md` (`AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` on `origin/<base_branch>`, each or `(<base> has no <name>.)`): never the change's own, which its author wrote. The request is `{"from", "from_owner", "type", "body"}` with `body` exactly as received. obid excludes `.obi/` in the worktree's `info/exclude`, and writes both files itself, outside the sandbox, into a worktree that holds whatever the other Obi's branch commits, so it follows no link: when `.obi` or `.obi/inputs` is there and isn't a real folder (a link out of the worktree, or a file), the job fails, saying so, and nothing is written; a link or file named `obi-request.json` or `change.diff` is removed, not written through, and the file is made new.
4. It runs the agent in the worktree with a fixed prompt that names only the commit, the conditions' kinds and the two files, never a field the other Obi wrote words into: the repository's and the branches' names (`repo`, `head_branch`, `base_branch`) reach the agent only inside the quoted file, and the prompt refers to them there by name; with the account's binary and `-p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --permission-prompt-tool stdio --permission-mode default --setting-sources "" --strict-mcp-config --restricted --json-schema <the review output schema> --tools Read`, the environment cleaned as for any Claude Code job (no `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_*`, `ANTHROPIC_*`, `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`), and the job sandbox below. `--restricted` keeps Claude Code from loading a `CLAUDE.md` of the worktree as instructions (`--setting-sources ""` keeps settings files out, not instructions): an `AGENTS.md` or `CLAUDE.md` the change adds is part of what it reviews, and the prompt says every file of the worktree is data. The agent has Read and nothing else (`--tools Read`): Claude Code doesn't ask before every call (it runs read-only shell commands such as `ls`, `grep -r`, `grep -R` and `find -L`, and reads inside its working folder, without a permission prompt), so obid gives it only a tool that is safe to run unasked. Claude Code 2.1.204 has no Grep, Glob or LS in this mode; the agent reads the diff from step 3 and the files it touches. obid answers the agent's permission prompts itself and never asks the owner: after its fixed rules, only Read, and every path input must lead inside the worktree once symlinks are followed (`path`, `file_path` and `notebook_path` resolved as the system would open them, relative ones from the worktree); a path starting with `~`, a link that leads out, and an input obid doesn't know that may name a path are refused. Claude Code asks before reading outside its working folder, and before reading through a link that leads out of it. The sandbox lets the agent read its own account folder (it signs in with it), so these together keep the account out of the review: Read only, no link out of the worktree (step 2), and obid's answers for what Claude Code asks. The publish gate in step 8 is not a barrier against an account's contents: it catches what looks like a known secret, including one written spaced out, backwards, or split with dashes or zero-width characters, not every way to encode one. It closes the agent's stdin after the turn's `result` line. A review job that makes a commit fails.
5. It reads `structured_output` from the `result` line; when that is missing, the last fenced code block labeled `obi-review` in `result`, holding the same JSON. Neither, or one that doesn't match the schema, fails the job.
6. It runs the repository's `check` in the worktree, **in the same job sandbox as the agent** (the test-run limit applies): `tests_pass` is met when it exits 0. Without a `check`, `tests_pass` is not met. The check runs code from another Obi's branch, and with a remembered decision it does so without the owner looking first, so it gets nothing the agent doesn't.

7. It builds the result body (spec `protocol.md` 5.7): each finding gets the spec's recommended id (`f-` + 16 hex of SHA-256 of `file\nline\ntitle`), and findings are de-duplicated **by that id**: when two have the same id (same file, line and title, whatever their severity or detail), only the first the agent reported is kept, so ids are unique in the result as the spec requires; each acceptance condition its `met` (`custom` conditions are not judged by obid yet: not met), and `outcome` is `approved` when every condition is met, else `changes_requested`; a job that failed gives `failed` with no findings. A finding that doesn't match the spec's schema (a path outside the repository, a title on two lines) is dropped, and the summary says how many were; when one dropped would have failed a condition (a `blocker` under `no_blocker_findings`, or one at or above a `no_findings_at_or_above` severity), the result can't say so and stay consistent, so it is `failed`, never an approval that hides it.
8. **Publish gate.** Every text field of the reply (finding titles and details, the summary) goes through the secret scan Publish uses (its rules, the vault's secrets, their encoded copies). Clean: it sends the `result`. A finding, or a scanner that can't run: it sends a `result` with `outcome` `failed`, `findings` `[]` and no text, and records that in the exchange's `publishScan`.
   When the request has `approve_on_host` and the review came out `approved`, the result's `host_review` says what obid did about approving it on GitHub, before the result goes ([teammate approval](#obid-only-teammate-approval)).
9. The job ends `done` (or `failed`) and publishes nothing: no push, no pull request, no check run, no comment, `outgoing` stays `[]`, but for the one approving review [teammate approval](#obid-only-teammate-approval) allows (`pullRequestApproval`).
10. **Its worktree is kept** after the job ends, unchanged, so the owner (and the e2e scenario's steps 5.2, 6.3 and 8.4.3) can look at what was reviewed. The cleanup pass (`cleanup`, above) removes the worktree of a review job from another Obi (a job whose `obi` isn't null) only once that job ended more than 24 hours ago, like a settled job's, and never earlier: the rule that removes an Oby review job's worktree as soon as it has a verdict doesn't apply to it. A runner restart keeps it too.

**The job sandbox** (steps 4 and 6), bubblewrap on Linux and a Seatbelt profile (`sandbox-exec`) on macOS. It holds for the process and everything it starts:

- **Writes** go only to the job's worktree (`home/worktrees/<job id>`), the worktree's own git directory (`<common dir>/worktrees/<name>`: its `HEAD`, `index`, logs), and the job's own temporary folder, which is its `HOME` and `TMPDIR` (for the agent also its own login folder, `home/logins/<job id>`). Never an account's own folder (`configDir`, #655): no box mounts it, so nothing a job writes is ever read by an unboxed process (the tool's own sign-in, the person's own `claude`); every configured account's folder is also hidden from every box, so none reads it either, wherever it is. At its start obid looks at each configured account's folder once and logs anything there that would run something or redirect a tool the next time it runs unboxed (Claude Code's `hooks`, `env`, `apiKeyHelper`, `statusLine` and `mcpServers` in `settings.json`, `settings.local.json` or `.claude.json`; Codex's `notify`, `mcp_servers`, `model_providers` and `profiles` in `config.toml`; Cursor's `mcp.json` and a non-empty `permissions.allow` in `cli-config.json`), since a box of an older obid could have put it there. On macOS, when the Keychain holds an account's login, it takes out a `.credentials.json` an older obid copied into that folder (one with no refresh token), whether `claudeKeychain` is on or off now (#646: left there, it showed the account `signedIn` and was copied for its runs); one the Keychain doesn't hold, or one with a refresh token, may be the login itself and stays. A `configDir` that holds obid's home is refused. Nothing else is writable, the clone included: a review job makes no commit.
- **Reads, from `obid`'s `home`**: the worktree, and the clone's git common directory (`home/clones/<owner>/<name>/.git`: objects, refs, `packed-refs`, `config`, `info/`), which git needs to work in a linked worktree, read-only. Nothing else of `home`: not the database, `keys/`, `api-token`, the delegation, `plugins/`, `plugin-data/`, other clones or other worktrees.
- **Reads elsewhere**: the system's files and the tools it runs (read-only), as for any job; not the person's real `HOME`. Of it, only the person's Rust toolchain, read-only: rustup's home (`RUSTUP_HOME`, else `~/.rustup`) and cargo's own `bin` (`CARGO_HOME/bin`, else `~/.cargo/bin`), never the rest of the cargo home (its `credentials.toml`, its registry).
- **Environment**: cleaned as in step 4, with no vault secret, no code-host login (`GH_CONFIG_DIR` empty), no `obid` API token and no Obi key. `HOME`, `TMPDIR` and, on macOS, `CFFIXED_USER_HOME` are the job's temporary folder: Foundation takes its home from the password database whatever `HOME` says, so without it SwiftPM's caches and Xcode's DerivedData went in the person's hidden `~/Library` (xcodebuild: `You don't have permission to save the file "repositories"`). `RUSTUP_HOME` is the person's, and a job's `cargo clippy` and `cargo fmt` are the ones the person's shell runs: cargo looks for `cargo-*` in its own `bin` before `PATH` only when that `bin` isn't on `PATH`, and otherwise in `PATH`'s order (cargo#11020), so when the person's cargo `bin` isn't on their `PATH`, the job's own cargo home (`<temp>/.cargo/bin`) holds a link to each of its `cargo-*`, and when it is, the box's `PATH` (the person's) decides as theirs does (2026-10-24: with neither, the box found Homebrew's older clippy where the person's cargo found rustup's, and their lints differ). obid makes or takes out the links before each run, never through a link the job left (`.cargo` and its `bin` must be real folders, and a link of the same name is replaced, not followed). `a_jobs_cargo_is_the_persons` shows the box and the person's shell print the same `cargo clippy -V`, `cargo fmt --version` and `rustc -V`. Builds are sized to memory (obid only): the agent and the check get `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS`, `RUST_TEST_THREADS` and `OBY_BUILD_JOBS` (for the repository's own scripts: this repository's `scripts/oby-check.sh` passes it to `swift test -j` and `xcodebuild -jobs`), all the same number, one for each 1.5 GiB (about what one rustc of a big crate holds) of the room memory leaves the build (Jobs run side by side, above): what's spare (`available`, less a fifth of `total` kept free for the person's own apps), less what the other running jobs' agents here may still take (each one's budget less what it holds now), each at least what its turn was told to build with (N × 1.5 GiB for N, the whole turn: the width given is room taken), and, for an agent, one of the other running jobs' checks (any of them may start its check during the turn; its own can't); a check isn't held back from itself. Memory only narrows a build this way: it never keeps a job or a check waiting. At most half the machine's cores, at least 1. It's worked out at every turn (each starts the agent again) and every check, so a turn that starts next to others builds narrower. With memory unreadable, none is set and builds keep their own defaults. A command can still say otherwise for itself. A committing job's `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` and `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES` hold for every git it runs, in any repository (git has no setting that scopes them to one), so a process that runs git in another repository, a test's fixtures, takes both out, as obid's own git does and its tests do.
- **Network**: the check has none (`--unshare-net`; a profile that denies network, and the DNS resolver's socket to anything without the network: Seatbelt kept that socket's allow over the profile's `(deny network*)`, so until 2026-10-24 a check, a review's or a no-network command resolved host names, and a lookup carries what it names out), but a check of the owner's own job whose agent ran here (never another Obi's review, nor a job that ran on a teammate's Mac, whose commits came from there: `Job::check_keeps_loopback`) keeps loopback, for the servers its tests start on 127.0.0.1. bubblewrap's network namespace has a loopback of its own with nothing of the host's on it; Seatbelt can't make one, so on macOS that check reaches the host's loopback (`localhost`, IPv4 and IPv6) and Unix sockets in its own folders, as the job's agent does anyway, and still no resolver (no host name but `localhost` resolves) and nothing beyond loopback: `an_owners_check_keeps_loopback_and_nothing_more`. On macOS an agent reaches nothing but loopback, and no resolver: its model's API, and any other site, only through obid's egress proxy ([obid only: the egress proxy](#obid-only-the-egress-proxy-616)). On Linux the agent has a network namespace of its own too, with `slirp4netns` so it still reaches its model's API (on Linux obid needs `slirp4netns` installed, Ubuntu: `sudo apt install slirp4netns`; without it no agent job and no answering run can start, and an answering run's log says only `obid couldn't answer by itself (the model's answer wasn't JSON)`) (in the stub's case, a local HTTP endpoint, at `10.0.2.2` from inside the namespace). An abstract-namespace Unix socket on the host is not in that namespace. `an_agent_reaches_its_api_but_not_an_abstract_socket` shows both. Inside the namespace the host's `/etc/resolv.conf` can't serve (on Ubuntu it names systemd-resolved's stub, `127.0.0.53`, the namespace's own loopback there, and the confined form has no `/run` for its link to lead into), so obid binds a file of its own over the one file it leads to, naming only slirp4netns's DNS forwarder, `10.0.2.3` (the host's `search` and `options` lines aren't carried over). That file is in a folder only obid's user can open and no sandbox shows (`/run/user/<uid>/obid-resolver-<uid>`, else the same name in the temporary folder), never in a folder a job writes, and it's replaced with a rename, never written through what is at its path. `a_run_with_the_network_resolves_a_host_name` resolves the model API's host in both forms, and `a_link_a_job_leaves_is_never_written_or_read_through` shows a link a job leaves is neither written nor read through.
- **No terminal, no one to run things for it.** The process leads a session of its own (`obid __session`, `setsid`; bubblewrap's `--new-session`), so it has no controlling terminal to push keystrokes into. On macOS the first process in the sandbox starts the job's program and, when that program exits or the process is signalled, kills every process in the sandbox (`kill -1`, which the profile allows only inside the sandbox). A descendant that called `setsid` ends with the job, on stop and when obid stops. On Linux, bubblewrap's PID namespace and `--die-with-parent` do the same. On macOS the profile also denies what would run or write something for the job outside its sandbox: LaunchServices opening an app (`lsopen`), Apple events, launchd jobs (`job-creation`), posted input events (`hid-control`, the window server's services), and every Unix socket but the job's own and, for a process with the network, the DNS resolver's.
- **On macOS, no preferences, pasteboard or keychain.** cfprefsd reads and writes the person's preferences for whoever asks, whatever the asker's `HOME`, `CFFIXED_USER_HOME` or file rules, so the profile denies `user-preference-read` and `user-preference-write` and the lookup of cfprefsd's services: a job has no preferences at all (CoreFoundation's writes fail; its reads find only what the job may read as files, and the person's home is hidden). It denies the pasteboard server's services (`com.apple.pasteboard.1`, Universal Clipboard's), secd's (`com.apple.securityd.*` and `com.apple.security.*`: the data protection keychain and iCloud Keychain, which secd reads and writes for whoever asks), the system keychain's securityd and SecurityAgent (which would ask the person for a keychain's password). The person's login keychain is a file in their home, which is hidden, and a client reads a file-based keychain's file itself, so it is out of reach too. Claude Code's login for a job is a `.credentials.json` in the job's own login folder, not the keychain: for an account with a `configDir`, a copy of that folder's login (the Keychain item's with `claudeKeychain`, else its `.credentials.json`) without the refresh token, in a folder that is the job's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, with the account's `.claude.json` trimmed to what keeps it signed in (copied once); when `configDir` is null, the copy of `~/.claude/.credentials.json`. Claude Code 2.1.296 no longer writes it when the Keychain is denied, so with `claudeKeychain` on (obid only: configuration) obid copies Claude Code's own Keychain item instead (`security find-generic-password -s <service> -a <user> -w`, where the service is `Claude Code-credentials` for the default folder and `Claude Code-credentials-<first 8 hex digits of the folder's SHA-256>` for an account's `configDir`): into the job's private login copy as `.credentials.json` (removed when the run ends, like a copied file). The copy carries exactly what calls the API: `{"claudeAiOauth": {accessToken, expiresAt, scopes, subscriptionType, rateLimitTier}}`, and nothing else the item holds (no refresh token, no MCP logins, plug-in secrets or device tokens). Anthropic rotates refresh tokens, so a boxed run that refreshed with a copy revoked the Keychain's own and signed the Mac's own `claude` out (2026-10-17). Instead the Mac's own Claude Code refreshes the default login (`configDir` null), and the login of each account obid added itself (#877: its `configDir` is obid's own `home/accounts/<id>`, which the Swift engine never runs on), unboxed: `<absolute path of claude> -p ok --model haiku --max-turns 1 --tools "" --strict-mcp-config --setting-sources "" --no-session-persistence`, with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `TMPDIR` set to a new empty private folder of obid's (Claude Code applies a `.claude.json`'s `env` from its config folder whatever `--setting-sources` says), `CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR` set to empty (it names the Keychain item: empty is the default one), `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`, the kept variables and `HOME`, and no `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_*` or `ANTHROPIC_*`, within 60 seconds, its output discarded. Claude Code 2.1.296 has no command that only refreshes (`claude auth status` reads the login without refreshing it), and it refreshes only in a token's last 5 minutes, so that is when obid asks: a keeper on a thread of its own looks every 60 seconds at each login it keeps (the default one, and every account obid added, each its own Keychain item), while a boxed run used it in the last hour or a job of it still runs, and when its token ends within 5 minutes or doesn't say when, has it refreshed, reads the Keychain again and writes each running job's and conversation's private copy of it again (only copies made from the Keychain, never through a link; a copy is let go the moment its run or turn ends, so a refresh never writes one account's login into a folder another account's run uses next; the brain's one-call copy is never kept). A boxed run that starts in those 5 minutes waits for that refresh (or runs it) first. A login far from its end takes no lock; else one refresh at a time per Keychain item, at most one per 4 minutes, so many runs starting at once refresh it once and a failed refresh isn't hammered; never on obid's async runtime. After a refresh the old access token stays a secret obid redacts, next to the new one. If the refresh fails or the token has ended, the run doesn't start, saying the Mac's Claude login for that account needs signing in again. Claude Code keeps the item's refresh lock, write lock and plaintext fallback in `CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR`, which is the account's folder, so an added account's refresh runs only while its `.credentials*` files there are the person's own and no link. An account the configuration names with a `configDir` of its own is never refreshed by obid: no box of obid's gets that folder (#655), but on a Mac the Swift engine still runs agents unboxed with it (#655's open item). Its runs start while its token is far from its end and stop after, saying it ends soon or has ended and to run `claude` on that account. With `claudeKeychain` off, an account's login file is copied the same way, without its refresh token, and refused near its end the same way. A running Claude Code (2.1.296) reads its `.credentials.json` again when the file changes (its modification time, looked at each time it builds a client to call the model), so a long job goes on with the renewed token; a 401 alone doesn't make it read the file again when the login has no refresh token, so a job whose token ends while the keeper can't refresh it fails at its next call. obid never writes the Keychain. The value is never logged, shown or kept anywhere else. An account whose login is in neither place shows `signedIn: false` and routing skips it (#626); with `claudeKeychain` off, a login only in the Keychain is not one a job can use. Claude Code makes its Bash folder in `/tmp/claude-<uid>` unless told, so obid sets `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR` to the job's temporary folder.
